## Inbox

- [x] **I-129** ‼️ HIGH PRIORITY: links in chats can't be opened in the Mac app — `shell` / `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — a link in an agent reply can't be clicked to open it in the browser (Chrome) in the Mac app; the user wants this queued as a high-priority Inbox item. (Seen with the `https://login.tailscale.com/admin/dns` link in a reply)
  - Notes:
    - Markdown links are rendered by Streamdown (`features/chat/Markdown.tsx`, `linkSafety` off) as `<a target="_blank">` with the I-104 sky chip style.
    - In the Tauri WKWebView, `target=_blank` does nothing: no new window and no system browser. The desktop app has no opener/shell plugin (`apps/desktop/src-tauri`: dialog, single-instance, window-state only) and no `on_navigation`/new-window handler. It works in a normal browser (`pnpm dev`), which is why it went unnoticed.
  - Fix idea:
    - Open external links (http/https/mailto) in the default browser from the app: `tauri-plugin-opener` (or an `on_new_window`/`on_navigation` handler in `lib.rs` that calls macOS `open`), plus one delegated click handler in the web app (`lib/desktop.ts` `openExternal`) for every `<a>` with an external href. That covers chat, tool output, settings and dialogs.
    - Never navigate the app's own webview away. Keep in-app routes as they are. Consider ⌘-click and a context menu with "Copy Link".
    - A remote environment's web view (served from another origin) needs the same behaviour.
  - Acceptance: clicking a link in a reply opens it in the default browser (Chrome here) and the app stays where it was; test in the installed app, since a browser-only test won't catch it.

    - **Web:** `lib/external-links.ts` has one delegated click handler (plain, ⌘/⇧/⌥ and middle clicks) → `openExternal()`; in a normal browser external links open a new tab.

- [x] **I-008** Downscale attached images to the model's limits before sending — `chat`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — attaching an image failed with an error. The user asked whether we could pass the model a pointer to the image instead when sending, and how other chat apps handle this.
  - Notes: A phone photo (10.8 MB) was rejected by Anthropic: `image exceeds 10 MB maximum`. pi resizes images its `read` tool loads (`images.autoResize`), but images sent with an RPC `prompt` pass through untouched. ChatGPT, Claude.ai, Codex and Claude Code all downscale client-side before upload. Providers downscale large images anyway (Claude works at ≤ ~1568px on the long edge), so there's no quality loss.
    pi reports per-model limits (`model.inputLimits.images.resize`: e.g. maxWidth/maxHeight 2000, maxBytes ~4.7 MB, jpegQuality 80). Add optional `imageLimits` to protocol `ModelInfo` (translated in `harness/pi/translate.ts`), and in the composer (`features/chat/`) resize on attach with canvas/`createImageBitmap`: fit within max dimensions, re-encode photos as JPEG at the given quality (keep PNG for small/transparent screenshots), step quality/size down until under maxBytes. Defaults when a model has no limits: 2000px / 4.5 MB / 0.8. Respect EXIF orientation. Show the thumbnail from the resized image. Server-side guard: reject images over the limit with a clear 400 instead of letting the provider fail.
    A "pointer" (saving the file and passing its path) would still need the model to read it as an image, which costs the same tokens plus an extra tool call, so resizing is the standard fix. For non-image files, see I-010.
  - Acceptance: attaching the same ~11 MB photo sends successfully; a unit test for the resize/fit/quality logic (pure function for dimensions and quality steps).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): verified with a real 10.9 MB 6000×4000 JPEG → sent as 204 KB 2000×1333, Haiku answered. Limits come from pi (`imageLimits`); server rejects oversized images with a clear 400. Undecodable formats (HEIC outside Safari) show "This image format isn't supported".

Add fixture-based tests for Anthropic/OpenAI error shapes.

    Root scripts: `tauri:dev` (runs the web dev server + API server + `tauri dev` window pointing at Vite, hot reload), `tauri:build` (builds web + server bundle, then `tauri build --bundles app`), `tauri:install` (runs `tauri:build`, quits a running pi-ui, replaces `/Applications/pi-ui.app` with the built `.app` using `ditto`, ad-hoc signed; prints the path). Local builds only, no notarization.
  - Acceptance: `pnpm tauri:install` produces a working `/Applications/pi-ui.app` that opens from Finder/Spotlight and can run a real chat.

- [x] **I-012** Run the pi-ui server inside the desktop app — `infra`
  - Notes: The Tauri app starts the Node server on launch and stops it on quit.
    Recommendation: bundle the server to a single JS file (esbuild) shipped in the app's Resources, run it with the user's own Node (pi already requires Node). No embedded runtime, small app. Alternative: a compiled single-file sidecar (Node SEA / `bun build --compile`) if we ever want to drop the Node dependency.
    GUI apps launched from Finder don't get the shell's PATH (nvm's `node`/`pi` won't be found). Resolve `node` and `pi` via the login shell (`$SHELL -lc 'command -v pi'`) at startup, pass them to the server (pi path setting still overrides), and show a clear error screen if pi can't be found.
    Use a separate port from the dev server (pick a free one at launch, pass via `PI_UI_PORT`); the window loads the server URL, which also serves the built web app, so there's no cross-origin setup. Same data dir as the web version (`~/Library/Application Support/pi-ui`). Single instance (focus the existing window on relaunch).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): `src-tauri/src/server.rs` resolves node/pi via `$SHELL -ilc` (falls back to nvm/volta/Homebrew dirs), requires Node ≥ 22, free port, waits for `/api/settings`; server exits if the app dies (stdin close). Native error dialog when pi/node is missing. Log: `~/Library/Logs/io.github.jas7457.pi-ui/server.log`.

- [x] **I-014** Show context usage (and session cost) in the chat UI — `chat`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — asked whether pi exposes how much context is used and available, and whether the chat UI could show it.
  - Notes: pi RPC `get_session_stats` returns `contextUsage { tokens, contextWindow, percent }` (the same estimate pi uses for its footer and auto-compaction), plus session token totals and `cost`. `tokens`/`percent` are `null` right after compaction until the next response.
    Server (pi adapter, `harness/pi/pi-harness.ts`): fetch stats after init, after each `turn_end`/`agent_settled` and after `compaction_end`; emit `state` with `contextUsage` (already in `SessionState`) and add `sessionCost`/`sessionTokens` (protocol change; lead makes it first). Throttle so it's at most once per turn.
    UI (`features/chat/`): a small circular meter in the composer toolbar next to the thinking picker, like Codex's; tooltip/popover with "42.1k / 200k tokens (21%)", "Auto-compacts near the limit", and session cost "$0.45". Amber above ~80%, red above ~95%. Unknown after compaction: show a dashed ring with "Updates after the next reply". Hidden for chats that haven't started (or show the model's window from `ModelInfo.contextWindow`).
    Optional in the popover: a "Compact now" button (pi RPC `compact`); would need a small endpoint.
  - Acceptance: the meter updates after each turn in a real pi chat; unit tests for the stats translation and the meter's thresholds/formatting.
  - Decision (2026-09-26): no "Compact now" button; compaction comes from the `/compact` slash command (I-016). The popover can mention "Type /compact to free up context".
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): ring in the composer toolbar (amber >80%, red >95%, dashed when unknown); tooltip with tokens, auto-compact hint and cost (hidden when 0). Real test: 8.2k/200k after one reply, unknown after /compact. Compaction adds a transcript divider "Compacted context: 9.3k → 655 tokens".
- [x] **I-015** Show Claude subscription limits (session / weekly / per-model) — `server`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the user is on a Claude subscription with session and weekly limits and asked whether we can get any information about those limits to show. (Screenshot of Claude's usage page: Current session 44%, This week 6%, Fable this week 0%)
  - Notes: pi itself doesn't track this, but it's available:
    `GET https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage` with `Authorization: Bearer <oauth access token>` and `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20` (the same endpoint Claude Code's `/usage` uses). Verified 2026-09-26 with the user's token: it returned exactly the numbers on Claude's usage page. The useful part is `limits[]`: `{ kind: "session" | "weekly_all" | "weekly_scoped", percent, severity, resets_at, scope: { model: { display_name: "Fable" } } | null, is_active }`. Also `extra_usage`. Many other keys are internal codenames; ignore them.
    The token is pi's: `~/.pi/agent/auth.json` → `anthropic.access` (+ `expires`, `refresh`), written by the `pi-anthropic-oauth` extension. **Only read it; never refresh it ourselves** (refreshing rotates the token and could log pi out). If it's expired, show the last known values as stale; pi refreshes it on its next request.
    Undocumented endpoint: parse defensively, hide the feature if the shape changes or the request fails.
    Design: an optional harness capability `getUsageLimits?(): Promise<UsageLimits | null>` (protocol types `UsageLimit { id, label, percent, resetsAt, severity, active }`), implemented in the pi adapter for the `anthropic` provider when auth type is OAuth. The server polls about every 60s while a client is connected and after each `run_end`, then pushes a `usage_limits` ServerMessage.
    UI: compact bars ("Session 44% · resets 6:40 PM", "Week 6% · resets Sat 12:00 PM", "Fable week 0%") in a popover from the sidebar footer (a small gauge next to Settings), and/or in the I-014 context popover. Warn (amber/red) using `severity`, and show a toast when a limit becomes active/critical.
  - Alternative/extra: Anthropic responses also carry rate-limit headers, which a small pi extension could read via the `after_provider_response` event (status + headers). Not needed if the endpoint works.
  - Acceptance: numbers match Claude's usage page; nothing breaks for non-Anthropic or API-key users (feature hidden).
  - Status (2026-09-26): modules committed (`harness/pi/anthropic-usage.ts`, `services/usage-limits.ts`, `state/usage.ts`, `features/sidebar/UsageGauge.tsx`); real call returned Session 48% / Week 7% / Fable 0%. Wired (2026-09-26): `PiHarness.getUsageLimits`, poller hosted in AppService (polls only while a client is connected, refreshes after each run), current value sent to new WebSocket clients. Verified live: gauge "52%" next to Settings, popover matches Claude's usage page.
- [x] **I-016** Slash-command menu in the composer — `chat`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — in pi's terminal UI, typing `/` lists commands to run. The user asked whether that list is exposed so our chats can offer the same; with it there'd be no need for a Compact button, since they could just run `/compact`.
  - Notes: pi RPC `get_commands` returns `{ name, description, source: "extension" | "prompt" | "skill", location?, path? }`. With the user's setup: 19 extension commands (/mcp, /kg, /vibe, /team, /websearch, /cd, /reply, /powerline, …) and 4 skills (/skill:web-design, …). They run by sending the text as a normal `prompt` (pi expands skills/templates; extension commands run immediately, even while streaming).
    pi's **built-in** TUI commands (/compact, /model, /new, /name, /tree, /export, /session…) are NOT returned and don't work via `prompt`, so pi-ui provides its own built-ins that map to RPC or app actions:
    `/compact [instructions]` → RPC `compact` (+ `customInstructions`); `/new` → new chat in the same project; `/name <title>` → rename; `/model [query]` → open the model picker (or set it directly if the query matches); `/thinking <level>`; `/export` → RPC `export_html` and reveal/open the file; `/stats` → session stats (tokens, cost; ties into I-014); `/settings` → navigate. Keep the list in one registry so more can be added.
    Protocol: add `SlashCommand { name, description?, source: "builtin" | "extension" | "prompt" | "skill", argsHint? }`; harness capability `listCommands()`; server endpoint `GET /api/chats/:id/commands` (needs the live session; cache per session and refresh on open) and `POST /api/chats/:id/compact` (+ export) for the built-ins. The pi adapter maps `get_commands`.
    UI (composer): typing `/` at the start of the input opens a popup above the textarea listing commands filtered by fuzzy match on name + description, grouped (Built-in, Extensions, Skills, Prompts), each with its description; ↑/↓ to move, Tab/Enter to complete (insert `/name ` and keep typing args), Esc closes. Enter on a complete built-in runs it instead of sending a message. Show a short system notice in the transcript for built-ins ("Compacted context: 150k → 32k tokens"). Some extension commands are TUI-only (e.g. /powerline, /bash-mode use `ctx.ui.custom`, a no-op in RPC); they're still listed, and if one produces nothing the user just sees no response. Optionally mark known-TUI ones later.
    Also works in the new-chat composer (commands from a utility session, or built-ins only until the chat exists).
  - Acceptance: typing `/` shows the menu with built-ins + the user's extension commands and skills; `/compact` compacts a real chat and the context meter (I-014) drops; `/skill:…` expands; keyboard-only operation; component tests for filtering/keyboard handling; server test with FakeHarness.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): built-ins live in `apps/web/src/features/chat/slash/builtins.ts` (/compact, /new, /name, /model, /thinking, /export, /stats, /settings); harness commands from `GET /api/chats/:id/commands`. Real test listed 8 built-ins, 19 extensions, 4 skills. `/export` writes to ~/Downloads with a Reveal button (`POST /api/fs/reveal`). Not done: marking TUI-only extension commands; visible confirmation when picking a skill.

The hover state brightens slightly.
    Across the board: lower dark `--pi-fg` a little (e.g. 0.88 → ~0.82) so transcript and settings text is also less harsh. Keep headings and emphasis at the strong level so hierarchy is kept. Check contrast stays readable (WCAG AA for body text on `--pi-window` / `--pi-sidebar`). Tune the light theme to match.
  - Acceptance: before/after screenshots of the sidebar, a chat and settings in dark and light; the only colour values live in `styles.css` tokens.
  - Build after: `tauri` (it may be editing `styles.css` / sidebar background for vibrancy). Vibrancy makes the sidebar background translucent, so check the grey reads well on it.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): `--pi-fg` 0.88 → 0.82 dark / 0.84 light, new `--pi-fg-strong` for titles; sidebar tokens `--pi-sidebar-fg` (0.72 dark / 0.75 light), `-strong` (selected/unread/hover), `-muted` (headers, ages). Real vibrancy not checked in the packaged app yet (simulated only).
- [x] **I-018** Dialogs open shifted left, then jump to the centre — `shell`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the Create project dialog first appears a bit to the left and only centres itself a few hundred milliseconds later.
  - Notes: Root cause found. `ui/Dialog.tsx` centres the panel with Tailwind v4's `-translate-x-1/2`, which sets the CSS **`translate`** property. The open animation `pi-sheet-in` (`styles.css`) animates **`transform: translate(-50%, …)`**. Both apply, so during the animation the panel is shifted by −100% of its width, then snaps to centre when the animation ends. `pi-pop-in` (AlertDialog/confirm) has the same double −50%.
    Fix: keep centring on one property and animate only what changes, e.g. keyframes `from { opacity: 0; transform: translateY(-12px) }` for the sheet and `from { opacity: 0; transform: scale(1.06) }` for the pop-in, leaving the x/y centring to the `translate` utilities. Alternatively, centre with a fixed flex wrapper and no translate at all. Check every other keyframe or animation that combines with translate utilities (popovers, toasts).
  - Acceptance: Create Project and confirm dialogs appear centred from the first frame (record a few frames via screenshots or a Performance trace).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): keyframes animate only the slide/scale; verified centred on the first (paused) frame.
  - Build with/after: `tauri` (it may be editing `styles.css`).
- [x] **I-019** Stop auto-sorting by recency; order projects manually with drag and drop — `shell`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the sidebar should not re-sort when new messages arrive or by recency at all; instead the user wants to order projects by dragging them above/below each other.
  - Notes: Today `sortedProjects` and `chatsForProject` (`apps/web/src/state/store.ts`) sort pinned-first, then by `lastActivityAt`, which the server bumps on every prompt/run (`app-service.ts` `touchProject`, run_start/run_end). So rows jump when a message arrives.
    Projects: add a persisted manual order: protocol `Project.sortOrder: number` (new projects go to the top or bottom; lead decides with the user, default top), `PUT /api/projects/order { ids: string[] }` which rewrites `sortOrder` for all projects in one go and broadcasts `project_upsert`s (or one `projects_reordered`). Migrate existing projects by their current order. Sidebar: drag a project row (whole group moves, collapsed or not) above/below others, with an insertion line, auto-scroll near edges, and Esc to cancel; keyboard alternative (e.g. context menu "Move Up / Move Down" or ⌥⌘↑/↓). Pinned: decide whether pin still means "top section" or becomes redundant with manual order (see open question).
    Library: `@dnd-kit/core` + `@dnd-kit/sortable` are React libraries (they work via preact/compat, but check), or a small custom pointer-based sortable (the list is short). Must not break the row's click/rename/context menu.
    Chats: never re-sort on activity (see open question for their order).
  - Decisions (2026-09-26, user + lead recommendation):
    1. **Chats**: unpinned chats are ordered by **creation time, newest first** and never move on activity (sort by `createdAt`, not `lastActivityAt`). They are not draggable.
    2. **Pinned chats** sit at the top of their own list (the project's chats, or the standalone Chats section), with a subtle divider, and **can be dragged to reorder among the pinned chats of that same list**. Persist with `Chat.pinOrder` (+ `PUT /api/chats/pin-order { projectId, ids }`). Pinning adds to the top of the pinned group; unpinning returns the chat to its date position.
    3. **Projects**: always manually ordered (drag and drop); **project pinning is removed** (protocol `Project.pinned` dropped, menu item removed).
    4. **New projects go to the top** of the manual order (user, 2026-09-26).
    5. Pinned chats stay **inside their own list** (per project / standalone), no global Pinned section (user, 2026-09-26).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): projects ordered by `sortOrder` (drag a project group; menu Move Up/Down); pinned chats first by `pinOrder` (draggable within their pinned group), unpinned by creation date; nothing moves on activity. Custom pointer-based sortable (`features/sidebar/useSortable.ts`, math in `reorder.ts`) with Finder-style insertion lines, 4px threshold, Esc cancels, edge auto-scroll. Server: `PUT /api/projects/order`, `PUT /api/chats/pin-order`, store migration from the old order; project pinning removed.

- [x] **I-022** Never run two servers on the same data folder — `server`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — decision: `pnpm dev` keeps sharing the data folder with the desktop app (the user wants dev and Tauri to share it).
  - Notes: The desktop app, `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` all use `~/Library/Application Support/pi-ui`. Two servers at once would both write `chats.json`/`projects.json` and could drive the same pi session files from two processes (corruption, duplicate agent runs).
    Add a data-dir lock: on start the server writes `<dataDir>/server.lock` `{ pid, port, startedAt, kind: "dev" | "desktop" }` (created exclusively; stale if the pid is dead) and removes it on exit.
    Desktop app (`src-tauri/src/server.rs`): if a live lock exists, **connect to that server's port** instead of starting its own, and show a small "Using the dev server" hint (it won't stop that server on quit).
    `pnpm dev` / `pnpm start`: if the desktop app's server holds the lock, exit with a clear message ("pi-ui.app is running and owns the data folder; quit it or use the app"), or optionally a flag to take over.
  - Acceptance: starting the app while `pnpm dev` runs reuses the dev server (and vice versa refuses cleanly); tests for lock acquire/stale/release.
  - Status (2026-09-26): server side done (`services/data-lock.ts`, exit code 3, `PI_UI_SERVER_KIND`, `PI_UI_NO_LOCK`); desktop side done: the app reuses a live lock owner (window notice, never stops it on quit), connects to the winner after an exit-code-3 race, and offers "Start Server" if a borrowed server dies (that button untested). Also fixed: the server now checks for the built web app per request (a dev restart mid-`vite build` used to leave `/` as "Not found").

Default it to a cheap fast model: `anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5` when that model is available (check `listModels`), else the chat's model. Settings → Models still lets the user change it. Not needed for closing the window (it only hides).

- [x] **I-025** Detect runs interrupted by quitting/crashes and offer "Continue" — `server`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — when the app is relaunched, the user wants to know if a run was interrupted, with something like a Continue button to resume it.
  - Notes: Persist a `runInProgress: true` flag on the Chat record at `run_start` and clear it at `run_end` (and on user abort). On server start, any chat with the flag still set was interrupted (app quit, crash, server killed, agent process died): clear the flag, set `interrupted: true` (protocol `Chat.interrupted?: boolean`), and push it. Also set it when an agent process exits mid-run.
    UI: the sidebar shows interrupted chats with the failed/attention marker (tooltip "Interrupted"), and they count toward the attention badge; the chat shows a banner above the composer: "This run was interrupted when pi-ui quit." with **Continue** (sends a follow-up prompt such as "Continue where you left off.", which clears `interrupted`) and **Dismiss**. Sending any message also clears it.
    Consider a `ChatStatus` "interrupted" vs. reusing `unread` + `lastRunFailed`; lead decides when making the protocol change.
  - Acceptance: start a long run, quit the app (or kill the server), relaunch: the chat is marked, Continue resumes the work; server test with FakeHarness (simulate restart with a persisted flag).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): `runInProgress` persisted at run_start; on startup (or agent exit mid-run) the chat becomes interrupted + unread + lastRunFailed. Amber banner with Continue / Dismiss. A normal ⌘Q mid-run also counts as interrupted. Not done: "Interrupted" tooltip text in StatusIndicator.

- [x] **I-031** App icon missing in AltTab (and other app switchers) when running `pnpm tauri:dev` — `infra`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the app switcher AltTab doesn't show our app's icon; the user suspects something extra needs setting so it picks it up. (Screenshot: AltTab shows the generic "exec" icon for pi-ui)
  - Notes: Diagnosed: the running window was `tauri dev`'s `target/debug/pi-ui`, a bare executable without an `.app` bundle, Info.plist or `.icns`, so `NSRunningApplication.icon` (which AltTab reads) is the generic executable icon. The installed `/Applications/pi-ui.app` has `CFBundleIconFile` + `icon.icns` and should be fine (user to confirm).
    Fix for dev: run the dev binary inside a minimal bundle. E.g. a `runner` wrapper for `tauri dev` (`build.runner` / `tauri dev --runner`) or a small script that, after cargo builds, assembles `target/debug/pi-ui.app` (`Contents/MacOS/pi-ui` symlink, `Contents/Info.plist` with bundle id `io.github.jas7457.pi-ui.dev`, name "pi-ui (dev)", `CFBundleIconFile`, `Contents/Resources/icon.icns` from `src-tauri/icons/`) and launches that. Also set the Dock icon at runtime in debug builds as a fallback (`NSApplication.setApplicationIconImage`), but that alone doesn't fix app switchers.
    Consider a visually distinct dev icon (e.g. a "DEV" ribbon) so the dev and installed app can be told apart in AltTab/Dock.
  - Acceptance: with `pnpm tauri:dev` running, AltTab and the Dock show the pi-ui icon; the installed app is verified too.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): root cause for the "installed" case: dev and installed shared the bundle id, so the single-instance socket made the installed app hand off to the running dev process (AltTab was always showing the bare dev binary). Fix: dev builds run inside `target/debug/pi-ui (dev).app` via a cargo runner (`scripts/dev-app-runner.sh`) with their own id `io.github.jas7457.pi-ui.dev` and a DEV-banded icon; install re-registers the bundle with LaunchServices. Verified with `scripts/check-app-icon.swift` (non-generic icons for both); AltTab check pending from the user.

- [x] **I-063** Keep "Working…" visible for the whole run instead of blinking between tool calls — `chat` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the "Working…" indicator keeps appearing and disappearing, which causes UI jank. It should stay visible the whole time tool calls are running and disappear only once the actual response arrives.
  - Notes: `features/chat/Transcript.tsx`: `showWorking = isRunning && !streaming && !toolRunning`. During a run of tool calls the flags flip constantly (assistant message streams its tool-call args → tool runs → short gap → next message streams…), so the row mounts/unmounts several times per second and the transcript height jumps.
    Proposed: show it for the whole run (`isRunning`), and hide it only while the assistant is streaming visible **text** (the actual reply) — i.e. `streaming` counts only when the last streaming assistant message has a non-empty text block. Thinking-only and tool-call-only streaming keep it shown. Keep a fixed-height slot so text appearing below the tool group doesn't shift it; optionally debounce hiding (~300 ms) to swallow brief flickers. "Compacting context…" unchanged.
  - Acceptance: during a multi-tool run the indicator stays put under "Ran N tool calls" until the reply text starts streaming; component test for the visibility rule (tool streaming/running → shown; text streaming → hidden; idle → hidden).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): the row stays mounted for the whole run (`workingStatus` in `features/chat/working.ts`) and hides only while the reply's last streaming block is non-empty text, with a 300 ms fade; it stays hidden between the final reply and `run_end`.
- [x] **I-064** Harness registry: several harnesses per server, chats routed by `session.harness` — `server` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — we need some abstraction (e.g. an abstract class) that makes adding new harnesses easy later. (audit item A)
  - Notes: Today `index.ts` picks one harness (pi or fake) and `AppService` holds a single `this.harness`: new sessions are stamped with `this.harness.id`, but reopening ignores `session.harness`. Add a `HarnessRegistry` (`Map<id, AgentHarness>`), open/delete/read transcripts via the session's harness, a default-harness setting for new chats, and a clear error for a session whose harness isn't installed. Keep interfaces (not an abstract class): optional methods fit harnesses with different feature sets.
  - Acceptance: AppService tests with two fake harnesses (different ids) show routing by `session.harness`.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): `HarnessRegistry` (`harness/registry.ts`); sessions open/delete/read through their own harness, new chats and app-wide features use the default (`agent.defaultHarness`, else the first registered), sub-agents inherit their parent's; unknown harness → 409 "This chat was created with the … agent, which isn't available". No UI to pick the default until a second harness exists.

Add to `ToolCallBlock` a `kind: "shell" | "read" | "write" | "edit" | "search" | "list" | "web" | "task" | "other"` plus normalized fields (`path`, `command`, `edits`, and a normalized diff on `ToolResult`), filled by each adapter's translator (`harness/pi/translate.ts`); renderers switch on `kind` and fall back to the raw name/args for `other`.

Verified with real pi (read/bash/write/edit identical before/after, and after reload). the extension kit's web_search/fetch_content/spawn_agent stay `other` (unchanged look); mapping them to `web`/`task` is a one-liner if wanted.

- [x] **I-070** Timers: how long the current ask and its tool calls have been running — `chat` (2026-09-26)

  - Notes: Proposed (like Codex / Claude Code):

    - Tool group summaries show their total duration ("Ran 10 tool calls · 48s"), and each tool row shows its own duration when expanded (live while running).

Add `SessionState.runStartedAt` (server sets it on run_start, so reloads/other clients see the true start) and `ToolResult.startedAt`/`endedAt` (stamped by the server/translator from tool start/end events). For history loaded from session files, use the message timestamps where available and otherwise hide the duration rather than guess.

  - Outcome (2026-09-26): "Working… 1m 12s" ticks from `state.runStartedAt` (server-stamped, survives reloads); groups show "Ran N tool calls · 48s" (wall clock first start → last end), each tool row its own time; times under 1s hidden once finished; old history shows none (`features/chat/duration.ts`). Not done: the optional "Worked for …" line (would need a persisted run end time).
- [x] **I-071** Redesign dialogs so they look polished (confirm alerts first, then all modals) — `shell` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the confirm dialog looks unprofessional (the user can't say exactly why); make it look nicer, and presumably the same applies to all modals. (Screenshot: the "Delete “Casual greeting and checking in”?" confirm)
  - Notes: The confirm is `ui/AlertDialog.tsx` (used by every `confirm()`, 7 call sites); the other modal is `ui/Dialog.tsx` (Create project). Why the alert reads as cheap:
    - It imitates a macOS NSAlert (272px wide, everything centred) but without what makes that work (app icon, native material), so it looks like a mobile popup.
    - The chat title is inside the heading, so it wraps mid-name ("…checking / in”?") and the heading gets 2 ragged centred lines.
    - Two big equal-width, full-width buttons with a bright red fill dominate the box; the body text is centred grey with tight spacing.
    - A weak backdrop (`bg-black/40`) and a surface only slightly lighter than the page, so the edge is mushy; the ring is barely visible.
    Proposed direction (Linear / Raycast / Arc style, still native-feeling):
    - ~400px wide, left-aligned. Short heading ("Delete chat?"), and the item name in the body in the strong colour ("“Casual greeting and checking in” will be permanently deleted. This can't be undone."), truncating very long names.
    - Optional leading icon in a tinted circle for destructive actions (trash, red tint).
    - Footer right-aligned with natural-width buttons (Cancel secondary, Delete danger), regular height; the destructive button uses a softer red fill or a red label; Enter/Esc hints stay implicit.
    - The floating-surface tokens from I-033 (`--pi-popover`, hairline border, deeper shadow), a slightly darker backdrop with a light blur, and a subtle scale+fade in.
    - Same header/body/footer spacing, typography and button sizes shared with `Dialog.tsx` (one set of dialog tokens/parts, e.g. `DialogHeader`/`DialogFooter`), so Create project and every confirm match.
    Update the `confirm()` callers' copy to the short heading + body pattern (7 call sites; `grep -rn "confirm({" apps/web/src`).
  - Acceptance: before/after screenshots of Delete chat, another confirm and Create project in light and dark mode; long titles don't wrap the heading; the existing AlertDialog/Dialog tests still pass (focus: Cancel first for destructive).
  - Open question (not blocking): the direction above vs. a more faithful macOS alert (app icon on top, compact, stacked buttons). Going with the direction above unless you say otherwise.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): shared `dialogClass` parts + `DialogIcon` in `ui/Dialog.tsx`, used by `Dialog` and `AlertDialog`; 400px left-aligned confirms, `confirm({ subject, icon })` puts the (shortened) name in the body, red trash icon for destructive ones, right-aligned natural-width buttons, softer `--pi-danger-fill`, `bg-backdrop` + 2px blur, panel anchored near the top with a scale+fade (`pi-dialog-in`). All 7 confirm call sites use the new copy.

- [x] **I-072** Replies seem to arrive all at once after a silent pause, instead of streaming — `chat` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — replies don't stream in: after a pause where nothing seems to happen, the whole message appears at once. With long enough messages a first chunk appears and then the rest streams; shorter ones arrive all at once after the pause.
  - Notes: Streaming itself works end to end (pi `text_delta` → `block_delta`, no throttling in the server or web). The pause is **hidden thinking**:
    - Checked the user's latest Glade session file (read-only): all 23 thinking blocks have empty text + a signature. The current Claude models don't send the reasoning text, only a signed placeholder, so the "thinking" phase streams nothing visible.
    - During that phase the assistant message is `streaming`, so `showWorking` (`Transcript.tsx`: `isRunning && !streaming && !toolRunning`) hides "Working…". `grouping.ts` also drops empty thinking blocks, so no "Thinking…" row shows either. Result: a blank gap of several seconds, then the text.
    - Short replies then arrive in one or two large deltas from the provider, so they appear to pop in whole; long ones visibly stream after the first chunk.
    Fix:
    1. Keep the indicator visible while the model is thinking with no visible text (covered by I-063's rule: hide only while reply **text** streams). Label it "Thinking…" while an empty/redacted thinking block is streaming, "Working…" otherwise, same row, no remount.
    2. Optional: smooth reveal of streamed text (a small typewriter buffer that drains big deltas over ~150–300 ms, as ChatGPT/Claude.ai do), so short replies stream in instead of popping. Must never lag behind at the end of a run (flush on message end) and must not re-render Markdown too often (rAF-batched).
  - Acceptance: with a thinking model, the indicator is visible from send until text starts (no blank gap); a replayed fixture with one large delta reveals progressively (if 2 is done); test for the indicator label/visibility rule.
  - Build with: I-063 and I-070 (same code in `Transcript.tsx`).
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-26): do both (1) and (2).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): "Thinking…" label while hidden reasoning streams, so no blank gap; `useSmoothText` (`features/chat/smooth-text.ts`) reveals each chunk over 200 ms (rAF, ≤30 fps re-renders) and finishes its reveal after the stream ends, so short replies stream in too.
- [x] **I-073** "Mark as Unread" for chats in the sidebar (and tabs) — `shell` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the user wants to mark chats as unread, as a reminder to come back to them later: a button in the sidebar marks the chat unread, and the flag goes to the chat's active tab (whichever tab was last open).
  - Notes: The plumbing exists: `PATCH /sessions/:id { unread }` (`AppService.updateSession`), and the sidebar context menu already has "Mark as Read" (`features/sidebar/ChatRow.tsx` → `markWorkspaceRead` in `state/actions.ts`). Unread is per session; a sidebar row (workspace) is unread when any of its sessions is (`protocol/workspaces.ts`).
    Proposed:
    - Sidebar row context menu: "Mark as Unread" when the chat isn't unread (toggles with "Mark as Read"), plus a ⌘K palette action for the current chat. Optionally a small hover action on the row (the user mentioned a button; an envelope/dot icon next to the existing hover actions); decide when building, keeping the row uncluttered.
    - Which session gets the flag: the workspace's focused main tab (`layout.activeMainSessionId`, see `activeMainSession…` in `protocol/workspaces.ts`), i.e. the last tab you had open, so opening the chat lands on it and clears it. New `markWorkspaceUnread(workspaceId)` in `state/actions.ts`.
    - Tab strip: "Mark as Unread" in a tab's context menu too (same flag on that tab's session).
    - Marking the chat you're currently looking at: the server clears unread when a viewer starts watching, but not while you keep viewing; the flag should survive until you leave and come back. Simplest: marking the open chat as unread navigates away (like Mail's behaviour of leaving it highlighted) or it stays open and simply shows as unread until you re-open it. Verify the server doesn't immediately clear it (`setViewing` only clears on a new view; the lease/sync path around `pushSessions` also clears unread for viewed sessions — check and exempt a manual mark).
    - Unread from a manual mark counts toward the Dock badge / title count like any other unread (it's the same flag).
  - Acceptance: right-click a read chat → Mark as Unread → it shows the unread dot and bold title; clicking it opens the last tab and clears it; works for multi-tab workspaces; test for the action choosing the focused main tab and for the server not clearing a manual mark while the chat is still on screen.
  - Status (2026-09-26): web side done (`markWorkspaceUnread`/`markSessionUnread`/`markSessionRead` in `state/actions.ts`; sidebar row menu toggles Mark as Read/Unread on the `unread` flag; tab context menu; ⌘K "Mark as Unread"). A manual mark survives while the chat stays on screen on one server. Left (phase 2, server): `AppService.applyExternalChange` clears unread for any viewed session on an external upsert, so with two servers (app + `pnpm dev`) a mark made in one is cleared by the other while it shows the chat. Fix: only clear on a run that ended while viewed (e.g. `runInProgress` true → false), never a manual mark; FakeHarness test (PATCH unread while viewing, then an external upsert → stays unread).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): web side as in the Status line; server: `Session.markedUnread` (set by PATCH `unread: true`, cleared by a new view or Mark as Read), and `applyExternalChange` no longer clears a manual mark for a viewed session, so a second server showing the chat keeps it (multi-server test).
- [x] **I-074** `/name` with no title names the chat from its content; one generic "small model" setting — `server` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — `/name` with no title should read the chat and give it a good name, using the same small model that names chats in the first place. The setting should become more generic, since more features will use a small model for quick tasks; there should be just one small model setting rather than one per feature.
  - Notes:
    - Today `/name` without args shows "Usage: /name <title>" (`features/chat/slash/builtins.ts`). Auto titles are generated once from the **first message only** (`AppService.generateTitle` → `harness.generateTitle({ firstMessage })`).
    - New: `POST /api/sessions/:id/title/generate` (or `PATCH` with `{ generateTitle: true }`) builds a compact excerpt of the conversation (first user message + the latest few user/assistant texts, tool output skipped, capped to a few thousand chars), asks the small model for a short title, and applies it like a rename (the main-tab/workspace rule in `renameFromSession`). Mark it `titleSource: "user"` so nothing overwrites it later. `/name` with no args calls it: show a "Naming…" state (toast or inline) and the result; `/name <title>` unchanged; update `argsHint` to `[title]`. Also a ⌘K action "Rename with AI"/"Suggest name" is cheap to add.
    - Harness side: use I-067's one-shot `complete?()` (or extend `generateTitle` to take a conversation excerpt instead of only `firstMessage`); keep the prompt in one place shared with the initial title.
    - **One small model for all quick tasks**: rename `settings.models.titleModel` → `settings.models.smallModel` (store migration), used for titles, `/name`, chat summaries and the ⌘K finder (`search-service.ts` already reuses the title model as its "fast model"), and future features. Settings → Models: "Small model — Used for quick tasks: naming chats, summaries and search. A small, fast model works best." Default stays Haiku when available, else the chat's model (`defaultTitleModel` → `defaultSmallModel`). Rename the internals to match (`fastModel`/`titleModel` → `smallModel`).
  - Acceptance: `/name` on a chat with several turns gives a fitting title from the small model; `/name Foo` still renames; settings shows one "Small model"; migrated settings keep the user's choice; server test with FakeHarness (excerpt building + title applied + titleSource), migration test.
  - Build after: `harness-core` (it is changing `packages/protocol/src/api.ts` Settings, AppService and search right now; I-067 adds `complete?()`).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): `POST /api/sessions/:id/title/generate` (`conversationExcerpt`: first user message + latest 6 texts, ≤ 4000 chars; same prompt as the first title; `titleSource: "user"`; single-tab chats rename the workspace too). `/name` without args toasts "Naming this chat…" → "Renamed to “X”". `titleModel` → `smallModel` (migrated, also on read), "Small model" row in Settings → Models. Not done: a ⌘K "Rename with AI" action.
- [x] **I-075** Sub-agent reports in the chat: collapsed card with Markdown instead of a huge user bubble — `chat` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — a sub-agent's report shows up in the parent chat as a very long response, which is unnecessary. It should be rendered as Markdown and collapsed by default, expandable when you want to read it. (Screenshot: "[agent-teams] harness-core finished:" shown as a long raw-text user bubble with `##`, `**`, backticks)
  - Notes: The server delivers sub-agent results to the parent as a normal prompt (`services/agents.ts`: `doneText` → "[agent-teams] <name> finished:\n<summary>", also `messageText` "message from <name>" and `exitedText` "<name> exited"), so the transcript shows it as the user's own message (`UserBubble` in `features/chat/Transcript.tsx`), as plain text.

    - A shared parser in protocol (e.g. `parseAgentMessage(text)` → `{ kind: "finished" | "message" | "exited", from, body }`, next to the format functions, which move to protocol so both sides agree) so live and history (pi session files hold the same text) are recognized. Harness-neutral: the format is Glade's own agent API.
    - Render these user messages as a compact, left-aligned (not a right-hand user bubble) card: agent icon + "harness-core finished" / "Message from dialogs" / "tool-kinds exited", the first line of the body as a one-line preview, a chevron; **collapsed by default**; expanding shows the body as Markdown (same renderer as replies, selectable, copy button). Clicking the agent name could open its tab (if it still exists).
    - The trailing note appended by `openNote` ("The sub-agent is still open…") shows as a muted footnote inside the card.
    - Also check the ⌘K search / sidebar preview / chat summaries don't treat these as the user's words (optional).

  - Outcome (2026-09-26): formatters + `parseAgentMessage` in `packages/protocol/src/agent-messages.ts` (server text unchanged); `AgentMessageCard` renders finished / message / exited as a collapsed left-aligned card with a one-line preview, Markdown body, copy button and the open-tab note as a footnote (live and history). Not done: clicking the name to open its tab; titles/search still treat these messages as the user's words (e.g. a chat titled "[agent-teams] h…").
- [x] **I-076** `!command` and `!!command` in the composer: run a shell command yourself (shared with the agent, or not) — `chat` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — support pi's `!` and `!!` commands in the composer: `!cmd` runs the command and lets the agent know about it; `!!cmd` runs it without telling the agent.
  - Notes: pi supports this over RPC already:
    - `{"type":"bash","command":"…","excludeFromContext":true|false}` runs the command in the session's cwd right away (no model turn); output streams as `bash_execution_update` events (with the request `id`), the response has `{ output, exitCode, cancelled, truncated, fullOutputPath? }`; `{"type":"abort_bash"}` stops it. `excludeFromContext` isn't in rpc.md but the RPC handler reads it (`modes/rpc` → `emitUserBash`/`recordBashResult`, checked in the installed pi 2026-09-26).
    - `!` output is stored as a `bashExecution` message and reaches the model with the **next** prompt ("Ran `cmd`" + output); it does not start a turn. `!!` is stored with `excludeFromContext: true` and never sent to the model.
    - History already has these: `translate.ts` maps `bashExecution` to a `notice` of kind `"bash"` (`$ cmd\n output`), shown today as a plain notice.
    Proposed:
    - Harness-neutral: capability `shell` in `HarnessCapabilities` (pi: true), optional `HarnessSession.runShell?({ command, shareWithAgent }) → ShellResult` + `abortShell?()`, streaming via new `AgentEvent`s (e.g. `shell_start`/`shell_update`/`shell_end` with an id) and a protocol message shape for a user shell command (command, output, exitCode, cancelled, truncated, `shared: boolean`) instead of the raw-text notice. The pi translator maps `bashExecution` (history, incl. `excludeFromContext`) and the live events to it. Server: `POST /api/sessions/:id/shell { command, shareWithAgent }` + abort; works while the agent is idle; while it's running, decide: run anyway (pi allows it) or refuse with a clear message (start with: allowed).
    - Composer: text starting with `!` switches to "shell mode" (visual cue like pi's: accent border/prompt glyph `$`, placeholder "Run a command — shared with the agent"; `!!` → "Run a command — not shared"); Enter runs it instead of sending a prompt; slash/`@` menus off in shell mode; ↑ history optional. Hidden when the harness lacks `shell`.
    - Transcript: a user-side shell card like the shell tool rows (`$ command`, streamed output, exit code badge when non-zero, "Stop" while running, copy), with a small "Not shared with the agent" tag for `!!`; long output collapsed like tool output.
  - Acceptance: `!ls` shows streamed output and the next prompt's reply can refer to it; `!!ls` shows output and the agent can't see it (verify with a real pi prompt asking what the last command printed); both survive a reload with the right shared/not-shared tag; Stop aborts a long command (`!sleep 30`); tests for the composer mode parsing, the translator (history + live) and the server endpoint (FakeHarness).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): capability `shell`; `HarnessSession.runShell`/`abortShell`, `shell_start`/`shell_update`/`shell_end` events and a user shell message in the transcript (history from pi's `bashExecution`, incl. `excludeFromContext`); `POST /api/sessions/:id/shell` + `/shell/abort`; composer shell mode on `!`/`!!`; `ShellCard` with streamed output, copy, Stop and a "Not shared with the agent" tag. Verified with real pi (Haiku): `!ls -la` output was visible to the agent on the next prompt, `!!echo secret-123` was not, both unchanged after reload. Not verified by hand: Stop on a long real command (unit-tested only); the worker hung on a headless-Chrome screenshot and was closed.
- [x] **I-077** More colour in the chat: Markdown, the working/thinking shimmer, tool calls — `chat` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — (after a screenshot of pi's terminal: teal inline code, coloured list numbers, bold white) everything in the chat looks too bland; the user wants more colour in what we render: e.g. a rainbow of colours in the thinking/working highlight animation, and tool calls that pop more.
  - Notes: pi's colours come from its TUI theme (`mdCode`, `mdListBullet`, `mdHeading`, … in `~/.pi/agent/settings.json` → theme "dark"), not from the model. Glade renders Markdown with its own styles: inline code is a grey pill (`features/chat/markdown.css` `[data-streamdown="inline-code"]`, `--pi-code`), tool rows are all `text-fg-subtle`/`text-fg-muted` (`features/chat/tools/ToolViews.tsx`), the working/thinking shimmer is `.pi-shimmer` (grey sweep).
    Proposed (all as tokens in `styles.css`, tuned for light and dark, WCAG AA for text; no raw colours in components):
    - **Markdown**: inline code in a teal/cyan tint (text colour + faint tinted background, like pi's `mdCode`); list numbers/bullets in an accent colour; headings a touch stronger; links in the accent colour; bold at `fg-strong`; blockquote border tinted; table header row subtly tinted. Tokens like `--pi-md-code`, `--pi-md-bullet`, `--pi-md-heading`, `--pi-md-link`.
    - **Working / Thinking shimmer**: a rainbow gradient sweeping across the label ("Working… 12s", "Thinking…", "Compacting context…") instead of the grey shimmer (animated `background-clip: text` gradient, slow and soft, respects `prefers-reduced-motion`: static gradient); the spinner can pick up the same hues. Maybe a distinct palette per state (thinking = violet/blue, working = rainbow).
    - **Tool calls**: each kind gets a colour (shell green, read blue, edit/write amber, search violet, web cyan, task pink, other grey), used for the icon (in a small tinted rounded square), and the verb ("Ran", "Read", "Edited"); the target (command/path) in `fg` monospace; errors red; "+3 −1" badges already green/red. Group rows ("Ran 10 tool calls") get the mixed icon stack in colour. Expanded panels keep the neutral code background.
    - Keep it tasteful and native: accents, not a colourful wall; check against the user's real chats in both themes.
    - **Tool group row** ("Ran 10 tool calls"): user (2026-09-26): would like it to get some colour, maybe animated while it's working, though that may be overkill if the Working row already has a colourful animation. Proposal: finished groups get static colour (the coloured icon stack, the count in the strong colour); while a group is still active, only its label gets a gentle shimmer in the colour of the **currently running tool's kind** (e.g. green while a shell command runs, blue while reading), not the rainbow, so it reads as "this is what's happening now" and doesn't compete with the rainbow Working row below. If both together look busy in practice, keep the group static and let the Working row carry the motion; decide from screenshots.
  - Acceptance: before/after screenshots (dark + light) of a reply with inline code, lists, a table, a tool group with shell/read/edit, and the working/thinking row mid-animation; reduced-motion respected; contrast checked.
  - Build after: I-075 and I-076 if they run in the same wave (same `Transcript.tsx`/tool views), or give one worker all chat-view styling.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): tokens in `styles.css` (`--pi-md-*`, `--pi-tool-<kind>`, `--pi-rainbow`, `--pi-thinking-gradient`, light + dark, contrast ≥ 4.5:1 for text): teal inline code, accent list markers, link colour, stronger headings/bold, tinted quote border and table header; rainbow sweep on Working/Compacting (spinner cycles hues), violet/blue sweep for Thinking, static under reduced motion; tool rows coloured per kind (tinted icon square + verb, failed = red); group row shows the kinds used as small coloured icons, bold count, and while active shimmers in the running tool's colour (kept: not too busy next to the rainbow row).
- [x] **I-078** Settings: a default model (and thinking level) for sub-agents — `settings` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — add a setting to choose the sub-agent model, since sub-agents may not need as strong a model as the main agent.
  - Notes: Today `AppService.spawnAgent` uses `req.model` (from the `spawn_agent` call or the agent definition) and otherwise **inherits the parent's model and thinking level** (`caller.model`, `caller.thinkingLevel`).
    Proposed: `settings.models.subagentModel: ModelRef | null` (null = "Same as the parent chat") and `settings.models.subagentThinkingLevel: ThinkingLevel | null` (null = same as the parent). Precedence: explicit model/thinking in the `spawn_agent` call or agent definition → the setting → the parent's. Settings → Models: "Sub-agent model" and "Sub-agent thinking" rows with the same pickers as the defaults (hidden models excluded), description "Model for agents started by a chat (spawn_agent). A cheaper model saves usage." Resolve the model within the parent's harness (I-064); if the setting's model isn't in that harness, fall back to the parent's. The sub-agent tab's model picker still lets you change it per agent.
  - Acceptance: with the setting on Sonnet/Haiku, a sub-agent spawned from an Opus chat runs on the chosen model (visible in its tab's model picker); an explicit `model` in `spawn_agent` still wins; "Same as the parent chat" keeps today's behaviour; server tests for the precedence; settings UI test.
  - Build with: I-074 (both edit Settings → Models and `Settings.models`).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): `settings.models.subagentModel`/`subagentThinkingLevel` (null = same as the parent); precedence request → setting (only if the parent's harness lists it) → parent; "Sub-agents" group in Settings → Models.
- [x] **I-079** Big replies still arrive in one large chunk before streaming starts — `chat` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — streaming needs another look: especially with bigger responses, all of it or a large first chunk arrives at once before streaming starts, instead of streaming from the beginning. (Earlier the user had asked whether responses popping in rather than streaming was ever fixed.)
  - Notes: I-072 (smooth reveal, `features/chat/smooth-text.ts`) is installed but doesn't fix it. Measured 2026-09-26 with a direct `pi --mode rpc` probe (no Glade in between): **pi streams from the start**. Haiku sends small `text_delta`s every ~25 ms from the first token; Opus 5.5 (thinking medium, the user's default) sends bursts of ~150–200 chars every ~0.4–0.6 s starting ~1 s after the prompt. So the big first chunk comes from somewhere between pi's stdout and the screen. Not yet checked (for the sub-agent, in a `--real` sandbox):
    - Server: `harness/pi/rpc-process.ts` line reading / `translate.ts` (are deltas held until `text_end` or `message_end` in some path, e.g. after tool calls or for the final message of a run?), `AppService.handleEvent` → broadcast, `http/ws.ts` (buffering/batching?). Timestamp events at each hop.
    - Web: `state/chat-session.ts` (is the session store `ready` when the first deltas arrive, or are early events dropped and the text only appears via `message_end` / a reload? `handleSessionEvent` returns early unless `status === "ready"`), grouping/`Transcript.tsx` (does the reply's text part mount late, e.g. only after the tool group closes, so `useSmoothText` starts with a large backlog or `live` is false and it shows everything at once?), `Markdown` (streamdown) rendering only completed blocks while streaming, `useStickToBottom`.
    - Situations to compare: a reply after tool calls vs. a plain reply; a long reply (thousands of chars); the chat being viewed vs. switched to mid-stream; the desktop app vs. the browser; thinking on vs. off.
  - Acceptance: with a long Opus reply (thinking medium), the text visibly starts streaming within ~1–2 s of pi's first delta and grows steadily to the end, both after tool calls and without; a test covering the found cause.
  - Build with: I-077 if it runs in the same wave (same chat files).

- [x] **I-080** Sub-agents: a status summary in the main chat; the right-hand pane only opens when you click one — `shell` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the user wondered whether sub-agents should live in the main window, with a UI showing a brief status summary of all sub-agents that you can click into. After comparing with Codex, they chose: sub-agents don't open by default, the main chat shows a summary, and clicking one opens it in a panel on the right.
  - Background (Codex, 2026-09): sub-agents live inside the parent chat. The IDE extension shows active sub-agents in a strip above the composer (expand → status, stop all, open a thread); the desktop app has a per-task Subagents panel with Active / Done lists (role/nickname, status, duration, result summary), threads open read-only. Users miss: talking to a sub-agent directly and seeing its model/effort. Glade keeps both (sub-agent tabs are writable).
  - Today: `features/workspace/WorkspaceView.tsx` shows the active main tab's sub-agents as tabs in a resizable right pane that appears as soon as there is a sub-agent (`activeSubagentId` in `layout.ts`); `AgentBar.tsx` shows done/closing/stopped above a sub-agent's conversation; `agent-status.ts` derives display state.
  - Design:
    - **Summary strip in the main chat**, just above the composer (like Codex's IDE extension), when the chat has sub-agents: one compact chip per sub-agent with its name, a status dot/spinner (working, needs input, done, failed/exited, closed), elapsed time (reuse `features/chat/duration.ts`), the model (short name), and the latest one-line activity (current tool summary or last reply line). Collapsed header when many: "3 agents · 2 working · 1 done" with an expand chevron; expanded list shows all, done ones dimmed, with a "Stop all" action for running ones (uses the existing close/abort APIs).
    - **Click a chip → opens that agent in the right-hand pane** (the existing sub-agent tab UI, still writable). The pane is **closed by default**, even when agents are spawned; closing the pane (× / ⌘W on the last sub-agent tab / Esc) just hides it and the agents keep running. Clicking another chip switches the pane's tab. The pane's open state and width are remembered per workspace (layout).
    - Hover/expand on a chip: the task (first line of its prompt/role) and, when done, its report summary (reuse the I-075 card Markdown), plus "Open".
    - Attention: a sub-agent waiting for input or failing highlights its chip (amber/red) and still counts in the badge as today; the main chat's status can say "Waiting on reviewer" if the main agent is idle but an agent needs input.
    - Sub-agents' reports keep arriving as the I-075 cards in the transcript; clicking the agent name in a card opens it in the pane (closes the I-075 gap).
  - Acceptance: spawning agents doesn't open the pane; the strip shows each agent's live status/time/model/activity; clicking opens it on the right, closing hides it without stopping it; reload keeps the pane state; tests for the chip state derivation and the open/close layout behaviour; screenshots with 1 and 4 agents in both themes.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): `SubagentStrip` above the composer (rows: status, name, model, latest activity, time; expand → task, report, Open; ≥ 3 agents start collapsed to "4 agents · 1 needs input · …" with pills; "Stop all" aborts running turns without closing agents); needs-input rows amber, failed/exited red (`--pi-warning-tint`/`--pi-danger-tint`). Pane closed by default (`WorkspaceLayout.subagentPaneOpen`), opened by a row or a report card's agent name; ×, Esc and ⌘W on its last tab hide it without stopping agents. Logic in `features/workspace/agent-chips.ts` (tested). Not done: "Waiting on reviewer" in the main status; hover popover (rows expand on click). Checked with injected fake agents only (the fake harness can't spawn agents).
- [x] **I-081** Status shimmer: grey text with a rainbow highlight sweeping across (not a full rainbow label) — `chat` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the full rainbow on the thinking label is too much. The user wants the earlier look back (grey text with a shimmer sweeping across), except the shimmer band should be a rainbow instead of white.
  - Notes: I-077 made the whole "Working…"/"Compacting…" label a moving rainbow (`.pi-rainbow`, 6 s) and "Thinking…" a violet/blue gradient (`.pi-thinking-text`), with the spinner cycling colours (`.pi-rainbow-color`), in `features/chat/chat.css` (tokens `--pi-rainbow`, `--pi-thinking-gradient` in `styles.css`). The old look was `.pi-shimmer`: grey text (`--pi-fg-subtle`) with a lighter band sweeping across every 2 s.
    Change: back to the `.pi-shimmer` structure (grey base, a narrow band moving left to right, same speed/feel as before), but the band is a small rainbow instead of `--pi-fg`: e.g. `linear-gradient(90deg, grey 0 35%, <rainbow stops> 38%…62%, grey 65% 100%) 0 0 / 250% 100%`, animated by background-position, one class used by Working, Thinking and Compacting (the separate violet thinking gradient goes). Keep the elapsed time ("1m 12s") in plain muted text. Spinner back to plain muted (no colour cycling), or at most a subtle tint; decide from a screenshot. Reduced motion: plain grey text. Light + dark: rainbow stops from `--pi-rainbow` so contrast holds. Tool-group shimmer (single tool colour) stays as it is.
  - Acceptance: screenshots mid-sweep in both themes showing grey text with a rainbow band; reduced motion shows plain grey.

- [x] **I-082** `pnpm tauri:install` installs without quitting the running app — `infra` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the install shouldn't close the running app; it should just install, so that the next time the user quits and reopens the app, the new version launches. Decided right away: do that now and remove the when-idle and auto-quit behaviour.
  - Notes: macOS lets a bundle be replaced while the app runs if it's swapped by rename (overwriting files in place can get the running app killed for a code-signature mismatch). The one thing a running Glade loads from its bundle later is the web app (served per request from `Contents/Resources/app/web`), so an old server could serve new web code after a reload.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): `apps/desktop/scripts/install.mjs` builds, `ditto`s to `/Applications/.Glade.app.incoming`, renames the old bundle aside and the new one into place, deletes the old copy, re-registers with LaunchServices and prints "quit and reopen" if Glade is running; never quits anything. The desktop server serves the web app from a snapshot read at startup (`http/static-snapshot.ts`, `snapshotStatic` when `GLADE_SERVER_KIND=desktop`; test). `--when-idle` (`idle.mjs` + its test) and the auto-quit removed; README/AGENTS/ARCHITECTURE updated. The pre-rename pi-ui.app is removed only when it isn't running.

- [x] **I-083** Reopening the app returns to the last open screen (chat, tab, settings page) — `shell` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the app should remember the last open screen, so quitting and reopening it brings the user back to the chat they last had open.
  - Notes: Every screen is routable (URL = `/projects/:id/chats/:id?tab=…`, settings, new chat), and a browser refresh keeps you in place, but the desktop app always starts at `/`: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs` navigates the window to the server's root URL (`running.url()`), on a **new free port every launch** (`server.rs` `free_port`), so the page's origin changes each time and even `localStorage` from the previous launch isn't visible (localStorage is per origin; this also silently resets other UI state kept there, e.g. collapsed projects, sidebar width — check `state/ui.ts`).
    Proposed:
    - Web: remember the last route (path + search) on every navigation, and on startup at `/` restore it if it still exists (the chat/project wasn't deleted; otherwise fall back to `/`). Only restore on the app's first load at `/`, never override an explicit URL (links, the dev browser).
    - Storage that survives the port change: either (a) a stable port for the desktop server (pick a fixed preferred port, fall back to free if taken), which also fixes localStorage for everything else; or (b) persist it server-side (e.g. `PUT /api/ui-state { lastRoute }` in the data folder, per server kind) or on the Tauri side (store the last URL path and navigate to `url + path`). Recommendation: (a) a stable preferred port (fixes all per-origin state at once) + the web-side "restore last route" in `state/ui.ts`; check that `pnpm dev` (5317) and the app don't share/collide.
    - Scope: the whole route incl. the focused tab (`?tab=`) and settings section; window size/position is already remembered (window-state plugin).
  - Acceptance: open a chat (second tab focused), quit, reopen → same chat and tab; deleted chat → lands on the home screen; UI state in localStorage (collapsed projects etc.) also survives a relaunch; test for the restore logic (valid route, deleted chat, explicit URL wins).

- [x] **I-084** Sub-agents v2: small horizontal chips above the composer, a live card in the chat where each agent is spawned, fun names and a colour per agent — `shell` (2026-09-26)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 (after trying I-080 with 5 demo agents) — keep the status in the chat, but as a very small status above the composer: each sub-agent should not take a full row. Show sub-agents side by side horizontally (wrapping when needed), each with the last message sent or received, truncated so it stays narrow; clicking one opens the sub-agent panel. Where a sub-agent is spawned in the chat, show an entry with a bit more information (still collapsed) that also leads to the sub-agent; once it finishes, the entry becomes the last message received. Give each sub-agent a fun human name at spawn, with its functional name shown greyed out, and its own colour so each one feels unique.
  - Replaces the row layout of I-080's `SubagentStrip` (`features/workspace/SubagentStrip.tsx`, `agent-chips.ts`); pane-on-demand behaviour stays.
  - Design:
    1. **Chips above the composer** (instead of full-width rows / the summary header): small pills side by side, wrapping when needed. Each: the agent's colour (dot or left edge), its fun name, a tiny status marker (spinner / needs-input / check / failed), and the latest message sent or received, truncated to a short width (~24–32 chars, full text in a tooltip). Needs-input/failed pills keep the amber/red emphasis. Click → opens it in the right-hand pane (as today). No expand/collapse header, no per-row details; "Stop all" moves to a small overflow button at the end of the chip row (or the pane).
    2. **Live card in the transcript where it was spawned**: the parent's `spawn_agent` tool call (today a generic "other" tool row) renders as an agent card in the agent's colour: fun name + role (greyed), status + elapsed time, collapsed by default; expanded shows its task and live latest activity, with "Open" (→ pane). When the agent finishes, the card shows its final report (last message received), still collapsible. The separate report message that arrives later (I-075 card) links back / could be folded into this card (decide when building; avoid showing the same report twice at full size).
    3. **Fun names**: at spawn the server assigns each sub-agent a human first name ("Maya", "Otis", "Juniper"…) from a curated list, unique among the workspace's active agents; the functional name from `spawn_agent` ("reviewer", "tool-kinds") stays the id used by `message_agent`/`close_agent` and is shown greyed next to it ("Maya · reviewer"). Display-only, like Codex's nicknames. Stored on the agent record/session (e.g. `Session.agentDisplayName`) so every client and reloads agree; shown in chips, the transcript card, the report card, the pane's tabs and the sub-agent's own header.
    4. **A colour per agent**: assigned at spawn from a palette of distinct hues (tokens in `styles.css`, readable in light and dark; can reuse/extend the tool-kind hues), unique among the workspace's active agents, stored with the name (`agentColor`). Used for the chip, the transcript card accent, the report card, the pane tab and its header.
  - Acceptance: 1, 3 and 5 agents: chips stay compact and wrap; clicking opens the pane; the spawn card goes live → done with the report; names/colours are stable across reload and consistent everywhere; tests for name/colour assignment (unique, stable, recycled after close) and chip text truncation; screenshots both themes.
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-26): the report lives only in the spawn card. The later report message (I-075 `AgentMessageCard` for "<name> finished") is not shown separately in the transcript when its spawn card is present (history included); it still reaches the main agent as today. Messages from agents ("message from") and "exited" notices: show in/next to the spawn card too (e.g. as its latest message/status) rather than as separate cards, unless the spawn card isn't in the transcript (e.g. compacted away), then fall back to the I-075 card.

- [x] **I-085** Sub-agent pane pops open on spawn once it has been opened before — `web` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — sub-agents opened automatically in tabs when spawned, although the user understood we had made them not open by default.
  - Cause (lead): nothing opens the pane on spawn, but `layout.subagentPaneOpen` is persisted and stays `true` after the user opens an agent once (the user's workspace has it `true`). When all agents close, the pane just hides because it has nothing to show (`WorkspaceView`: `paneOpen && activeSub !== null`); the flag stays set, so the next spawn brings it back with the new agents as tabs.
  - Fix: the pane is "open" only for the agent the user chose. When the agent shown in the pane closes and none is left (or when the main chat has no sub-agents), clear `subagentPaneOpen`; new spawns never open it. Possibly also: a newly spawned agent never steals the pane's active tab. Test: open agent → it closes → spawn another → pane stays closed.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `shouldClearSubagentPane` (layout.ts, tested): once the main session is loaded and has no sub-agents left, `WorkspaceView` clears the saved `subagentPaneOpen`, so the next spawn stays in the chips/cards until the user opens it. Also clears a stale flag left from before (the user's workspace).

Picked from two previews: violet for inline code and sky blue (option five) for list numbers.

- [x] **I-089** Colours for every tool call: web, agent and MCP tools — `shell` (2026-09-27)

Tests for mapping and summaries.

- [x] **I-090** Attach any file by reference (was F-007 / I-010) — `chat` / `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — promoted from Future features (the user chose to do all of the proposed ones); original request: pass the model a pointer to an attached file when sending the message.

The sent message shows attachments as file chips (name, size/kind icon), not the raw line. Limits: size cap (e.g. 50 MB), sanitized names, only inside the attachments folder.

Not done (optional): using a dropped file's real path under Tauri.
- [x] **I-091** Agent tools to find and open chats (was F-008 / I-046 part b) — `server` / extension kit (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — promoted (the user chose to do all of the proposed ones); original I-046: pi should know about our chats and pick the one that best matches a request.
  - Design: Glade agent API endpoints (token-authenticated like the agent-teams ones) + the extension kit agent-teams (Glade backend only) tools: `find_chats({ query, limit })` → best matches (the ⌘K Ask pipeline: keyword candidates + fast model, keyword fallback) with ids, titles, project, date, summary, snippet; `read_chat({ id, limit })` → the chat's summary plus its last N user/assistant messages (text only, bounded); `open_chat({ id })` → shows that chat in the Glade window (a server event the web follows, like a ⌘K pick).
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): agent API `POST /api/agents/chats/{find,read,open}` (`services/chat-tools.ts`, types in `protocol/chat-tools.ts`): find = ⌘K Ask pipeline + keyword hits (caller's own chat excluded via search `exclude`), read = summary + last N messages bounded (no agent started), open = pushes `open_chat` so windows navigate like a ⌘K pick. the extension kit agent-teams (Glade backend) registers `find_chats`, `read_chat`, `open_chat` (the extension kit 7834931). Verified with one real Haiku run. Open: the tools show as grey `other` (proposal: a `chat` tool kind); `open_chat` doesn't bring the app to the front; no snippet when only the title matched.

- [x] **I-094** Split `app-service.ts` into focused services (no behaviour change) — `server` (2026-09-27)

Pure move + wiring; no behaviour or protocol change; each file with a header comment; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md module map updated.

  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `app-service.ts` is a 408-line facade over 11 modules in `services/app/` (context, errors, records, live-pool, lease-sync, titles, session-actions, sessions, workspaces, projects, agent-team; largest 357 lines); acyclic (upward calls via hooks wired by the facade); public API, order of events/awaits and all 819 tests unchanged (no test edits); fake-sandbox API smoke test clean.

- [x] **I-095** Paragraphs in replies had no space between them — `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 (screenshot) — replies are split into paragraphs but there's no space between them, so it's hard to see they're separate paragraphs; add spacing between them.
  - Cause: the model's text had blank lines (real paragraphs → separate `<p>`), but in `markdown.css` `.pi-md p { margin: 0 }` (specificity 0,1,1) outranked the block spacing rule `.pi-md > * + * { margin-top: 0.7em }` (0,1,0), so only paragraphs lost their gap (lists, code etc. kept it).
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): removed the `p` rule (the CSS reset already zeroes paragraph margins), so consecutive paragraphs get the same 0.7em gap as other blocks.

  - Lead recommendation: per conversation (sidebar row), since its tabs and sub-agents share one folder and one changes view. A "Work in a new worktree" switch when creating a chat (off by default); branch named after the chat, shown in the header; on delete ask keep / merge / discard. Converting an existing chat later (harder: changes already in the main folder).
  - Status: promoted 2026-09-27 (the user wants these worked on now rather than later, but not started yet). Not started; waiting for "go".
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `services/worktrees.ts`; `POST /workspaces {worktree:true}` creates `glade/<slug>` (unique) from the project's current branch in `<dataDir>/worktrees/<repo>/<slug>` (git projects with commits only); tabs and sub-agents run there via `Workspace.cwd`. Delete asks Keep branch (default) / Merge into <base> (only into a clean project folder on the base branch; conflicts abort, 409) / Discard (`branch -D`); the folder is always removed. Web: "New worktree · branches off main" switch under the new-chat composer, "New Chat in Worktree" in the project menu, branch glyph in the sidebar, shared `confirmDeleteChat` dialog showing uncommitted files / unmerged commits. `GET /projects/:id/git`, `GET /workspaces/:id/worktree`. Open: converting an existing chat; uncommitted files are lost on removal (warned); merge needs a git identity.

Header button with the changed-file count; the panel replaces the right pane while open (`layout.changesPanelOpen`; the sub-agent pane keeps its own flag): rows with checkbox, status, +/−, expandable `DiffView`, discard on hover; "Commit…" dialog with Generate and ⌘↩; dot on files this chat's agent edited; worktree branch badge in the header. Note: the count only refreshes on open / run end / panel open / refresh (a commit made outside Glade shows a stale count until then).

- [x] **I-100** Sub-agent reports don't count as the user's words in titles, summaries and search — `server` (2026-09-27)

Open: `read_chat` (chat-tools) still returns them as role `user`; chat creation with a prompt (`sessions.ts`) isn't guarded (agent deliveries don't use it).

- [x] **I-102** Composer loses the thinking picker and context meter after a server restart — `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 00:20 — the thinking-level picker and the context usage disappeared from the composer; the user asked what happened (after the dev server restarted); user agreed to fix it (quick win #5).
  - Suspected: after the socket reconnects, the chat's state (model info, thinking levels, context usage) isn't re-fetched, so the pickers stay hidden until a reload. Reproduce in a sandbox first (restart its server while a chat is open), then re-fetch/re-subscribe on reconnect; test.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): cause: with two servers on one data folder, a chat opened while the other server holds it gets a file-based view with thinking levels `["off"]` and no context usage, and the browser never reloaded it after the other server let go. Fix: file-based views are `offline: true` with the model's real thinking levels; offline chats reload on the next session update; failed loads retry on reconnect. Open: while the other server really holds the chat, its context meter stays hidden here.
- Quick win #2 (bring Glade to the front on `open_chat`) — not queued: the user asks from inside Glade, so the window is already in front.
