- [x] **I-105** New-chat context bar: project, Local/Worktree and branch pickers above the composer (Codex-style) — `chat` / `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 (4 screenshots of Codex) — the user likes Codex's small bar above the composer for picking the project, whether to run locally or in a git worktree, and the branch. Not a copy of its look (it should follow our design language), but the ability to switch between these.
  - Codex (from the screenshots): a bar attached above the composer with three buttons: 📁 project (popover: search projects, list with ✓, "New project", "Don't work in a project"), 💻 "Work in" (Local ✓ / New local worktree / cloud options), branch (popover: search branches, each with "Uncommitted: N files", ✓ current, "Create and checkout new branch…").
  - Design (ours, in Glade's look; replaces I-096's "New worktree" switch):
    1. **Project** button: popover with search, projects (✓ current), "Add project…", "No project" (standalone chat). Changes which project the new chat goes to (and the new-chat route).
    2. **Work in** button: Local (the project folder) / New worktree (I-096). Only for git projects; hidden or disabled otherwise.
    3. **Branch** button (git projects): popover with search, local branches (current ✓, "N uncommitted files" on the current one), "New branch…". In **Worktree** mode it picks the base the worktree branches from (safe, no checkout). In **Local** mode it shows the current branch; picking another branch checks it out in the project folder — only when the folder is clean (else disabled with the reason), since it changes the user's real folder. "New branch…" creates and checks out (Local) or names the worktree branch (Worktree).
    - After the first message the bar becomes read-only info in the chat (project · Local/Worktree · branch), e.g. in the header or a quiet line; I-097's header branch badge can merge into it.
    - Server: list branches + current + uncommitted count (extend `GET /projects/:id/git`), checkout / create branch for Local (refuse when dirty), `baseRef` for worktree creation.
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-27): in Local mode, switching branch is refused while the project folder has uncommitted changes, telling the user to commit first. Offer a "Commit…" button there (reuse the I-097 commit dialog), then switch.
  - Codex for comparison (researched 2026-09-27; source: a community reconstruction of the Codex app's code, github.com/JimLiu/decode-codex, not official docs): it just runs the checkout, so git carries non-conflicting uncommitted changes to the new branch; only when git refuses ("would be overwritten") it shows "Commit changes to switch branch" listing the files, with "Commit and switch branch…". Worktree mode (official docs, developers.openai.com/codex/app/worktrees): starts from the selected branch's HEAD, *applies its uncommitted changes to the worktree*, detached HEAD by default ("Create branch here" later), and "Hand off" moves a chat between Local and Worktree. Worth considering for us: carrying uncommitted changes into a new worktree, and hand-off (not in scope unless the user asks).
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `features/chat/context-bar/` (ContextBar, ProjectPicker, WorkInPicker, BranchPicker, SwitchBranchDialog, NewBranchDialog) + `ui/SearchPopover`; replaces I-096's switch. Project (search, ✓, Add Project…, No Project), Work in (Local / New worktree, git only), Branch (search; current, default, then newest; "N uncommitted files"). Worktree: base branch + optional branch name (`CreateWorkspaceRequest.baseRef`/`branch`). Local: checkout only when clean, else "Commit your changes to switch branch" with the files and Commit… (reuses `CommitDialog` via `POST /projects/:id/changes/commit`) then switch; blocked while a Local chat of the project runs; New Branch… creates + switches (changes come along). Server `services/project-git.ts`: branches, uncommitted files, checkout/branch with `check-ref-format`, 409s. Open: commit covers only the project folder's paths (subfolder projects / user-staged changes can leave it dirty → dialog again).

Sidebar: worktree glyph stays; optional tooltip with the branch. Becomes the read-only form of I-105's bar after the first message (build together with I-105 or first).

- [x] **I-108** Agent tool rows use the sub-agent's fun name and colour — `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 (screenshot of "Messaged context-bar: OK. location-line may…" with a raw JSON body) — rows like "Messaged context-bar" should show the sub-agent's fun name instead (e.g. "Messaged Kit" / "Messaged Fern").

Expanded body: the message text as Markdown (not JSON), then the tool result. Fallback to the functional name when unknown. Also apply to `spawn_agent`-adjacent rows if any still show the raw name.

Tests.

- [x] **I-109** A sub-agent's task shows as a compact "Task" card, not a giant user bubble — `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 (screenshot of Fern · location-line's pane: the whole task as a right-aligned user bubble) — when an agent is spawned, its task shows as a very long message that looks like it came from the user, expanded by default and taking up a lot of space; find a better way to display it.
  - Design: in a sub-agent session, its first user message (the task from `spawn_agent`) renders as a full-width card in the agent's style, not a user bubble: header "Task from <parent chat / main agent>" (+ agent colour accent), the first ~3 lines of the task as Markdown, collapsed by default with "Show full task" / "Show less"; copy button. Later messages the parent sends with `message_agent` render the same way ("Message from main", collapsed if long). Messages the user types into the sub-agent stay normal bubbles. Also: very long user bubbles in general collapse after ~15 lines with "Show more" / "Show less" (user, 2026-09-27: agreed to the optional idea of collapsing any very long bubble after 15 lines).
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `DelegatedCard` (+ `delegated.ts`): a sub-agent's first message (the task; matched to its stored task text when known) renders as a full-width card in the agent's colour — "Task from main · <parent chat>", Markdown clamped to 3 lines, Show full task / Show less, copy, time; later `[agent-teams] message from main:` messages get the same card ("Message from main", 6 lines). User-typed messages stay bubbles; any bubble over ~15 lines collapses with a fade and Show more (`ui/Clamp.tsx`, measures real overflow).

- [x] **I-114** Confirm dialogs look off: alignment and toy-like buttons — `ui` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 (screenshot of "Close tab?") — the confirm dialog looks bad overall: nothing lines up properly and the buttons look toy-like.
  - Observed: the red trash icon sits in a large tinted circle whose centre doesn't line up with the title; the body text is indented under the title but the buttons align to the dialog edge; buttons are tall, pill-ish and heavily coloured (big red "Close Tab").
  - Direction: follow macOS alert conventions (NSAlert / System Settings sheets): compact width, app-style icon or no decorative circle, title + message in one left-aligned column with the buttons aligned to that column's right edge, regular-size macOS push buttons (22–24px tall, 6px radius, subtle bezel; the default/destructive button filled but restrained), consistent spacing on an 8px grid. Applies to `ui/AlertDialog` / shared `ui/Dialog` parts, so every confirm (close tab, delete chat, worktree delete, discard file, commit…) improves. Screenshots before/after both themes.

- [x] **I-116** ⭐ Glade's own bundled pi extension for sub-agents and chat tools (no extension kit needed), plus a "Use sub-agents" setting (was F-019) — `server` / `settings` / the extension kit (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — add a setting to turn sub-agents on or off, on by default, since some people won't like them. Also, the sub-agent tooling should come from Glade itself rather than the user's personal pi extensions (other people won't have those; Glade should be what creates that bridge). First filed as a future feature, but a high-priority one.
  - Today: I-037 put the tools in the extension kit's agent-teams (Glade backend); anyone without the extension kit has no sub-agents or chat tools. Glade's own `-e` extension idea exists only in F-001.
  - Design: (1) Glade ships a small pi extension (e.g. `apps/server/src/harness/pi/extension/`, bundled into the app) loaded into every pi process it starts (`pi -e <path>`), registering `spawn_agent`, `message_agent`, `close_agent`, `list_agents`, child `report_done`, `find_chats`, `read_chat`, `open_chat` against the existing agent API. (2) the extension kit's agent-teams skips those tools when running inside Glade (keeps its cmux backend for terminal pi) — avoids duplicate names. (3) Setting "Use sub-agents" (Settings → Agent, default on): off → Glade's extension doesn't register the four sub-agent tools (chat tools stay) and the spawn endpoint refuses (403). Applies to new chats / when a chat's agent process restarts (optionally restart idle ones); running sub-agents finish normally. (4) Other harnesses (F-011) get the same tools via a Glade-served MCP server (fits F-001).
  - Status: promoted 2026-09-27 (the user moved F-019 into the Inbox). Not started; waiting for "go".
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): shared `ui/Dialog` grid (20px padding, one column for title/message/body/buttons, buttons on its right edge, 4/16/8px gaps); `AlertDialog` ~372px, no icon; `DialogIcon` a 16px glyph without the tinted circle (confirm/commit/branch dialogs now use none — an icon pushed the title out of line with the message); `Button` regular 22px, 6px radius, bezel + soft shadow, primary filled accent; destructive = red text on a plain button (macOS), Cancel stays default with a visible ring. Commit dialog starts in the message field. Before/after screenshots `/tmp/glade-dialogs2-*`.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): pending image thumbnails in the composer open the lightbox (←/→ between them), zoom-in cursor + hover dim; the remove × sits outside the button.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): Glade's own extension `harness/pi/extension/glade-tools.ts` (node-only imports, JSON Schema params; tool names/descriptions/results ported from the extension kit) loaded with `-e` into agent sessions only (`extension-path.ts`; bundled as `app/pi-extension/glade-tools.ts`; override `GLADE_PI_EXTENSION`); reads agent definitions from the same places as the extension kit. Glade sets `GLADE_TOOLS=1` (the extension kit agent-teams steps aside, the extension kit 7a4c487) and `GLADE_SUBAGENTS=off` when Settings → Agent → Use sub-agents is off (only the chat tools remain; spawn → 403). Compatible with old the extension kit (pi loads `-e` first; first tool name wins). Verified with real pi (Haiku): each tool once, spawn + report, find_chats, setting off. Open: `/team` not ported; idle chats keep old tools until their agent restarts.

- [x] **I-117** Carry uncommitted changes into a new worktree (was F-021) — `server` / `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the user asked to record the two Codex ideas as future features. (Codex applies the selected branch's uncommitted changes to a new worktree.)
  - Idea: an option when creating a worktree chat (I-096/I-105) to copy the project folder's uncommitted changes (tracked diff + untracked files) into the new worktree, so the agent starts from exactly what the user has; the original folder keeps its copy.
  - Status: promoted 2026-09-27 (the user added F-021 to the list).
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): "Bring my uncommitted changes (N files)" in the branch popover (worktree mode, base = current branch, default off; bar shows "from main + N changes"); `CreateWorkspaceRequest.carryChanges`: `git stash create` in the project folder + `git stash apply --index <sha>` in the worktree, then untracked non-ignored files copied (modes, symlinks); folder and stash list untouched; on failure the worktree + branch are removed. Open: a big untracked tree without .gitignore is copied in full; nested repos/submodules not carried.

- [x] **I-118** Pointer cursor on sub-agent chips — `web` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the sub-agent chips above the composer don't look clickable; change the cursor on them so they do.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): chips use `cursor-pointer` (`SubagentStrip.tsx`) — a second deliberate exception to the default-arrow rule, at the user's request (recorded next to I-113's in ARCHITECTURE).

- [x] **I-119** ACP harness, built to the contract without running any real agent (was F-020) — `server` / `settings` / `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the user likes having in-depth connectors for the agents they care most about and ACP for all the others; asked whether that would let them try Claude Code over ACP before a dedicated connector exists. Filed as a future feature.
  - Background: ACP (agentclientprotocol.com, started by Zed) is JSON-RPC over stdio between a client app and a coding agent. ~35 agents speak it (Gemini CLI, Copilot, Cursor, goose, OpenCode, Qwen, Kimi, …), Claude via Zed's `@zed-industries/claude-agent-acp` (Claude Agent SDK; replaces `claude-code-acp`), Codex via Zed's adapter, pi via `pi-acp`. There's a public agent registry. Competitors: Tcode does exactly this split (native pi / Claude Code / Codex / OpenCode + ACP for the rest); Codeg, AionUi and many editors are ACP clients.
  - Design: one `harness/acp/` adapter; each configured ACP agent (command, args, env) is its own entry in the agent picker. Mapping: `openSession` → `initialize` + `session/new` or resume; `prompt` → `session/prompt`; `abort` → `session/cancel`; `session/update` (message/thought chunks, tool calls with kinds + diffs, plan, usage, available commands) → `AgentEvent`s and normalized tools; model/thinking/mode pickers from the session's config options; Glade tools (sub-agents, chat tools) offered to every agent as an MCP server passed at session start (fits F-001 / F-019). Use the official ACP SDK, not a hand-written protocol layer.
  - Harder parts: (1) a permission card in the chat for `session/request_permission` (allow once / always / reject), also needed by a native Claude Code connector; (2) ACP chats need a Glade-kept transcript copy, because history is only available by resuming through the agent, while sidebar/search/Ask read session files; (3) features ACP lacks (titles, fast model, usage limits, export) hidden via capabilities or served by the default harness; (4) the spec is still moving (v1 `session/load` vs v2 `session/resume`).
  - Settings: Settings → Agent lists ACP agents (add by command; later browse/install from the ACP registry). ACP entries that duplicate a dedicated connector are hidden or labelled.
  - Uses: try Claude Code through `claude-agent-acp` before F-011's dedicated connector (fidelity limited to what the adapter exposes; check whether it uses the Claude subscription login or needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`). Run pi through `pi-acp` next to the native pi harness to test the ACP adapter against a known agent and see what the dedicated connector gains.
  - Size (estimate): about the pi adapter's size (~1–1.5k lines + tests) plus the settings panel and permission card; one round of work.
  - Constraint (user, 2026-09-27): build it to the ACP contract as faithfully as possible but never test or run it against a real agent; the user doesn't want any accidental charges or a big bill.
    → Build strictly from the ACP spec/SDK types; NO real agent CLI is ever launched (no `claude`, `gemini`, `*-acp`, no `--real` sandbox, no network calls to model providers). Unit tests only against an in-repo scripted fake ACP agent (no model, no network). No ACP agent is configured by default, so nothing can start until the user adds one in Settings. The first real run is the user's.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `harness/acp/` (official `@agentclientprotocol/sdk`, Apache-2.0): each agent in `Settings.harnesses.acp.agents` is a harness `acp-<id>` (registry `dynamic`, none by default), started on a chat's first prompt; initialize (fs read/write only), session/new|load|resume, prompt, cancel (10 s fallback); updates → text, thought, tool calls (ACP kinds → Glade kinds, diffs), plan (new harness-neutral plan card), slash commands, usage; stop reasons and crash / not-found / sign-in errors; fs confined to the chat folder (symlink-safe); request_permission → permission card (allow once / always / reject; rejected tools show "Rejected"); Glade keeps the transcript in `<dataDir>/acp-sessions`. Web: Settings → Agents "ACP agents" (add/edit/remove with the account/subscription note), agent picker in the new-chat bar, agent badge in the header for non-default agents, model pickers hidden (`capabilities.models:false`). New tabs inherit the chat's agent; a crash error shows once; Stop keeps queued follow-ups (like pi, with a hint).
  - Verified (2026-09-27) by the lead in a sandbox browser against the in-repo scripted fake ACP agent only (no real agent/model ever launched, per the user's constraint): streaming, tools, permission reject, fs inside/outside the folder, stop, queue, error, crash + session/load restore, reload persistence, settings, new tab, plan card. First real run is the user's. Open: modes / model options, terminals, elicitation, session titles not mapped; slash commands only after the agent started once.

- [x] **I-120** Tell the lead agent its sub-agents' fun names, so it calls them "Leo" rather than "t3-research" — `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the lead agent refers to its sub-agents by their code names (e.g. "t3-research") instead of the fun names the UI shows (e.g. Leo); asked whether the app controls this and whether the agent can be told the fun names.
  - Notes: Glade controls this. The fun name (`agent-names.ts`, stored as `AgentRecord.displayName`) is shown in the UI, but the model never sees it:
    - the `spawn_agent` result is `Spawned "<name>" …` (`harness/pi/extension/glade-tools.ts` ~486), and the `/spawn` response's `AgentInfo` has no `displayName` (`packages/protocol/src/agents.ts`);
    - reports and messages arrive as `[agent-teams] <name> finished:`;
    - `list_agents` shows only the code names.
  - Proposal:
    - add `displayName` to `AgentInfo`;
    - spawn result: `Spawned Leo ("t3-research") …`;
    - report header: `[agent-teams] Leo (t3-research) finished:`;
    - `list_agents` shows both;
    - one line in the tool guidelines: "Refer to sub-agents by their display name when talking to the user".
    
    The code name stays the id for `message_agent`/`close_agent`. The `agent-text.ts` search marker and the delivered-message parsing (I-099/I-100) must accept the new header, and old headers keep working.
  - Open question: optionally let the lead choose the fun name itself (`spawn_agent` `display_name`)? Default: no, Glade picks.

  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `AgentInfo.displayName`/`color`; `agentLabel()` gives "Leo (t3-research)". The spawn result reads `Spawned Leo ("t3-research")`; `list_agents` and the finished/message/still-open headers use the label, and a guideline says to use the display name with the user and the code name as the id. Children are told the name the user sees them by. The parser and search accept old and new headers, and reports still link by code name. Open: the "exited" note still uses only the code name (in live-pool.ts / sessions.ts, left to the store worker).
- [x] **I-121** Glade's own store: one SQLite database with a normalized conversation format for every harness (phase 1) — `server` / `protocol` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — instead of reading pi's JSONL session files, Glade should have its own standard conversation format and store conversations itself, whatever the harness (pi, Claude Code, Codex), while still keeping a pointer to the harness's own session so it can be resumed. Use an SQLite database rather than JSON files.
  - Also reported (the whole request, 2026-09-27): remote access to other Glade environments over Tailscale, with pluggable transports; invitations by QR code or a desktop link with the handshake built in; revocable access; each project locked to one environment (backfill existing ones); Glade's own folder picker; show the current environment; live streaming to every connected client; research T3 Code for ideas. Split into I-121–I-127 (promotes F-005).
  - Design: docs/design/environments-and-store.md §3.1.
    - `<dataDir>/glade.db` (`node:sqlite`, WAL, migrations); import from the JSON files once;
    - `messages` rows with stable ids written in `LivePool.handleEvent`;
    - an opaque `resume_json` per adapter;
    - sub-agent messages modelled explicitly;
    - pi JSONL and ACP JSON become import sources only (background import, lazy for chats not yet imported; re-import when pi's file changes outside Glade);
    - search, titles, Ask and chat tools read the store;
    - multi-server sharing (I-062) via the `events` table instead of file watching; leases stay.
  - Open question: move all app data into the database (proposed) or only conversations? Minimum Node 22.13?
  - Decisions (user, 2026-09-27): everything goes into `glade.db` (fine to move all app data there; don't keep the old JSON files as a long-term backup, though keep them temporarily since the user's server is running; this conversation must be moved into the database too). So: import once, leave the JSON files untouched until the migration is confirmed, then delete them automatically; refuse to migrate while an older Glade server uses the data folder; import all chats in the background (lazy on open), including this one. See design §5.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `glade.db` (`node:sqlite`, WAL, migrations in `store/db/`) holds projects, workspaces, sessions (`session_ref`, `resume_json`), agents, settings (plus `settings.export.json` for the desktop app), summaries, messages with ULID ids and plain text for search, tool results, transcript sources, `events` and `meta`. `json-file`/`file-lock`/`dir-watcher` are gone; servers poll `events` (150 ms). pi JSONL and ACP JSON are imported in the background or on open; changes made outside Glade are merged (turns added, ids kept). JSON files are imported once and deleted after 3 starts with no older server. An older-server guard serves "Quit the older Glade first". Minimum Node is 22.13.
  - Verified by the lead: migration on a copy of the real data folder (blocked while the real app ran, then imported 1 project / 2 chats / 4 sessions, and 1087 messages in 158 ms), stable ids across a restart, search, two servers on one folder, and a pi file changed outside Glade merged on open (after a fix). No model calls. Open: running an older build after migrating isn't picked up; the start counter counts every server's starts; message events are unused until I-122.
- [x] **I-122** Sequenced live sync to every connected client (phase 2) — `server` / `protocol` / `shell` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — see I-121. Design: docs/design/environments-and-store.md §3.2.
    - `events` log with a global sequence, committed with the change;
    - `subscribe {afterSeq}` → replay or snapshot → live;
    - duplicate and gap handling;
    - per-chat transcript subscriptions with snapshots paged by turn;
    - ~50 ms batching with a size limit, and pings;
    - client `commandId` so a retry can't send a prompt twice;
    - "ready" only after catch-up, and the list is always checked again on reconnect.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): the events seq drives everything (`services/sync/hub.ts`, protocol `sync.ts`, `hello.protocol: 2`). `subscribe {shell|session, afterSeq}` → replay or snapshot → `live`. Pushes are tagged with seq; there's gap/duplicate handling (`classifySeq`), 50 ms batches with a 4 MB budget, and 20 s/45 s pings. Session snapshots hold the newest 50 turns, with "Load earlier messages". Commands carry ids (`command_receipts`, 24 h). The web resubscribes from its last seq instead of reloading and re-checks the list on every reconnect. Migration 002 is additive.
  - Verified by the lead in a sandbox: two socket drops in the middle of a 300-word stream gave 300/300 words, each once, with no page reload; sending after a server restart works. Open: snapshots hold only the newest 50 turns (a search jump into older history says "not in loaded history"); resubscribing starts the chat's agent process as opening it does; an older app (schema 1) refuses to start once a newer build has upgraded the database.
- [x] **I-123** Environments: each server is an environment; projects belong to one; the app is a multi-environment client (phase 3) — `server` / `shell` / `settings` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — see I-121. Design: docs/design/environments-and-store.md §3.3.
    - a permanent `environmentId` and name, and `GET /api/environment` (capabilities);
    - `projects.environmentId`, filled in with the local environment and locked after creation;
    - web `EnvironmentConnection` per environment (base URL, credentials, supervisor, status), with environment-scoped API, routes (`/e/:env/…`; old routes = local) and localStorage;
    - several addresses per environment, with fallback;
    - sidebar grouped by environment when there's more than one;
    - environment badge in the chat header;
    - settings split: host settings (agents, models, harnesses) vs client settings (look and feel).
  - Open question: one window with all environments grouped (proposed), or switch one at a time?
  - Decisions (user, 2026-09-27): one window, **not grouped**, with free drag-and-drop order across environments; a remote icon on remote rows and in the chat header, and clicking it shows which environment. Pickers follow the host: project chats use the project's environment, and their agent, model and thinking pickers list only that environment's options. New chats without a project pick the environment first (Local / remotes), then agent, then model and thinking. Remote choices appear only when remote access is on; turning it off hides everything remote (not deleted), and turning it back on restores it. Design §5.
  - Note (user, 2026-09-27, for F-022): the iPhone app never runs a server and only connects through environment connections. So the multi-environment client must not assume a local environment exists: zero or more environments, all remote, and pairing started from the client side. Keep this core in code that can move into `packages/app-core`.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Server:** environment id and name (`services/environment.ts`, `GET`/`PATCH /api/environment`, `hello.environmentId`); migration 003 backfills `projects.environmentId` and locks it; loopback-only CORS and WebSocket for cross-server clients until I-125.
    - **Web:** multi-environment client (per-environment API, socket and sync; works with zero local environments); merged, ungrouped sidebar with per-device drag order and globe `RemoteBadge` on remote rows and in the header (popover: name, address, status); `/e/:envId/…` routes; pickers and host settings follow the environment; "This Mac" versus remote names (location keeps Local/Worktree); a new chat picks its environment first; Settings → Remote Access switch plus a temporary loopback "Connect to Environment…"; host-only actions only for this machine; remote export downloads.
  - Verified by the lead with two sandboxes: connect by URL, merged sidebar, remote chat and prompt answered by B, switch off/on (24 → 15 → 24 sidebar entries).
  - Open: only the first address is used (no fallback yet); Ask and usage limits are local only; collapsed state isn't per environment.
- [x] **I-124** Folder browser and environment-aware host actions (phase 3) — `shell` / `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — see I-121. Design: docs/design/environments-and-store.md §3.4.
    - `GET /api/fs/browse` (directories only, git markers, limited to home and `/Volumes`);
    - `ui/FolderBrowser` (path autocomplete, breadcrumbs, recent, new folder), used by Add Project, with "Choose in Finder…" only for the local environment;
    - Open in / Show in Finder / osascript picker only when the environment is this Mac;
    - export downloads through the browser.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `GET /api/fs/browse` and `POST /api/fs/mkdir` (home and `/Volumes` only, symlinks resolved, 2000-entry cap, errors inline); `ui/FolderBrowser`: path autocomplete, breadcrumbs, Home/Volumes/Recent, git markers, Show hidden, New Folder, keyboard (↑↓, ⌘↑, ⌘↩, type-to-select). Add Project uses it with an environment choice; "Choose in Finder…" appears only for this machine.
- [x] **I-125** Auth for remote clients (phase 4) — `server` / `infra` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — see I-121. Design: docs/design/environments-and-store.md §3.5.
    - hashed device tokens with sliding expiry;
    - a local-app secret from Tauri, because loopback is no longer trusted once serve proxies to it;
    - short-lived socket tickets;
    - Host allow-list, Origin checks and CORS for paired origins;
    - rate limits and an audit log;
    - a `scopes` column kept for later read-only devices.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): request classification in `http/security.ts` (local owner versus remote client; no local-app secret needed), the host switch (off by default), bearer device tokens (hashed, 90-day sliding expiry), single-use WebSocket tickets, a Host allow-list, CORS only for authenticated responses, rate limits, an audit log, host-only routes refused for remote clients, revoke or switch-off closing sockets at once (migration 004, `services/auth/`). Remote attachments upload to their own environment with the token.
- [x] **I-126** Pairing and devices: invite by QR code, link or short code; confirm; devices list with revoke (phase 4) — `settings` / `server` / `shell` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — see I-121. Design: docs/design/environments-and-store.md §3.5.
    - Add device → one-time grant (5 minutes, one active invite) shown as a QR code of `https://<host>.ts.net/pair#g=…&e=…&fp=…`, a link to copy and a short code;
    - the host confirms the device;
    - the client checks the host fingerprint;
    - devices list (last seen, Tailscale login hint) with Revoke and Revoke all;
    - revoking closes the device's sockets at once;
    - the client side: "Connect to another environment…" (paste a link or code, or pick a discovered host).
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-27): build the QR code and link now; the phone itself is a later project (F-022), so the QR code may not be testable end to end yet.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Host:** Settings → Remote Access in two parts ("This Mac": allow switch, addresses, Add Device…, devices with rename/Revoke/Revoke All, recent activity; "Other Environments").
    - **Add Device:** a QR code (`ui/QrCode`, `uqr`, MIT) of `glade://pair?…`, a copyable link, a short code with the address, and a countdown. It follows the invite: waiting → Paired ✓ / used after a deny → Create New Code.
    - **Allow prompt:** a global "Allow “X” to use this Mac?" (Deny focused).
    - **Client:** Connect to Environment (link, or code plus address; Return submits) → "Waiting for X…" → paired; `/pair?link=`; "Needs pairing" → Pair Again…
  - Verified by the lead with two sandboxes: code plus address → Allow → remote projects in the sidebar; revoke → "Needs pairing"; pairing again by link; deny → "code used" and Create New Code.
  - Open: the Allow prompt shows a laptop icon for phones too; no automatic discovery yet (phase 5); the token is kept in localStorage (Keychain later).

    - `Transport` interface (detect, status, enable, disable, reconcile, identify, discover);
    - `TailscaleTransport` using the user's Tailscale CLI: `tailscale serve --bg --https` to Glade's loopback port, reconciled at startup, never funnel, falling back to the `100.x` address over HTTP;

I-114 made `danger` red text on the plain bezel (strict macOS style), which in dark mode reads as washed out and looks almost the same as Cancel. Change `danger` to `bg-danger-fill text-white shadow-bezel-filled` with the same hover/active tone changes as `primary`. `--color-danger-fill` already exists in `styles.css` but nothing uses it. Keep the size and radius. Cancel stays the default (focused, Return cancels). Update the I-114 comments in `Button.tsx`/`AlertDialog.tsx`. Check the other `variant="danger"` users (`features/sidebar/delete-chat.tsx`) and take screenshots in both themes.

Written from the shipped code: the plan's old plain-HTTP fallback note is superseded (HTTPS only).
- [x] **I-128** README: remote access setup guide (Tailscale, HTTPS required) — `docs` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — note this down: the user will want a README section with all this remote-access information, and it must tell everyone to use HTTPS.
  - Content to cover (write after I-127, from what actually shipped):
    1. Install Tailscale on every machine (Standalone or App Store variant) and sign in with the same account. The CLI must be reachable: `/usr/local/bin/tailscale` (Settings → CLI integration) or the one inside the app.
    2. **Enable HTTPS for the tailnet (required):** in the **web** admin console (https://login.tailscale.com/admin/dns, not the Mac app's Settings window), make sure MagicDNS is on, then under "HTTPS Certificates" click **Enable HTTPS**. Note: machine names are published in public Certificate Transparency logs; the tailnet name (`tailXXXX.ts.net`) is random.
    3. On the host (e.g. Mac Studio): Settings → Remote Access → "Allow other devices to connect". Glade runs `tailscale serve` (tailnet-only, never Funnel) and shows its `https://<machine>.<tailnet>.ts.net` address.
    4. Add Device → QR code / link / code; on the other Mac, Connect to Environment → paste → Allow on the host.
    5. Revoking devices, turning remote access off, what stays private (pairing is the security boundary; anyone on the tailnet can reach the port but not use it without a token).
    6. Troubleshooting: HTTPS not enabled (plain-HTTP `100.x` fallback, desktop only), a new certificate briefly rejected, Tailscale signed out, a different Glade version on each machine.

- [x] **I-130** Sub-agent cards and chips flash "Starting…" between steps — `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — sub-agent status keeps flipping back to "Starting…" between steps (starting → running → starting → writing → starting…); asked why it shows Starting so often.
  - Cause:
    - `latestActivity()` (`features/workspace/agent-chips.ts`) only looks at the **latest** assistant message.
    - After every tool result pi starts a new assistant message, which at first holds only a thinking block (skipped) or nothing, so `latestActivity` returns "".
    - `spawnCardState` (`features/chat/agent-spawns.ts` ~l.160) then falls back to "Starting…". So every model turn between tool calls reads "Starting…" until the next tool call or text arrives.
  - Fix:
    - "Starting…" only before the agent has produced anything at all.
    - Otherwise: "Thinking…" while the newest message has only thinking (or nothing yet); or keep the previous step's line, e.g. "Ran grep …", dimmed.
    - Same in the sub-agent strip chips and cards (both use `latestActivity`).
    - Unit tests for: a new empty assistant message after tool results, thinking-only, and first start.

  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `latestActivity()` looks back through the assistant messages: the newest one still streaming with only thinking or nothing → "Thinking…"; finished without text or tools → the previous step's line; "Starting…" only before the first message. Same in the cards and the strip chips. Tests.
- [x] **I-131** Very wide pasted images overflow the transcript (cut off on the left) — `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — a very wide pasted image gets cut off; it needs a max width or similar. (A wide screenshot of the sub-agent cards)
  - Cause (`features/chat/UserBubble.tsx`):
    - The image row (`flex flex-wrap justify-end`) has no max width.
    - `ImageThumb`'s button is `shrink-0`, and the `<img>` only has `max-h-40`.
    - So a wide image at 160 px height becomes wider than the column and, right-aligned, spills out to the left, where the transcript clips it.
  - Fix:
    - Row `max-w-[85%]` (same as the bubble); button `min-w-0 max-w-full` (not shrink-0); img `max-w-full h-auto max-h-40 object-contain`.
    - Check the composer's pending thumbnails and tool/agent images for the same problem.
    - Test with a 4000×400 image in both themes.

- [x] **I-132** Remote Access settings: one master switch, then "let others connect" under it — `settings` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the current two switches are confusing. The user expected one switch to enable remote at all, with the other options shown only once it's on. Two switches still make sense, but nested: one to enable remote at all, and under it one to let others connect to this device, since you may want to connect to other machines without letting them connect to you.
  - Today: two independent switches. "Allow other devices to connect" (host, stored on the server, runs `tailscale serve`) and "Connect to other Glade environments" (client, per device in localStorage; shows or hides remote projects). Their order suggests the host one matters first.
  - Proposed layout:
    1. **Remote access** master switch at the top, off by default: "Use Glade across your devices". Off: nothing below is shown; remote environments are hidden and their connections closed; this Mac doesn't accept devices and serve is off. Nothing is deleted.
    2. When on: the Tailscale status row (the transport applies to both directions), then **Your environments** (Connect to Environment…, discovered Macs, the list). Connecting out is simply what "on" means, so the client switch disappears.
    3. Below it, **Let other devices use this Mac** (the host switch, off by default) → reveals This Mac's address, Add Device…, Devices and Recent activity.
  - Open question: where the master switch is stored (per device, since it's the client side). The host switch stays on the server, and turning the master off also turns hosting off. Decide whether turning the master back on restores hosting (proposal: yes, remember it).
  - Decisions (user, 2026-09-27): one main switch turns off everything remote, while the hosting choice is remembered in the background. When remote is turned off, anyone connected loses access to this Mac's projects at once; their UI should still list this computer but say it's disconnected / remote has been disabled. There should also be a status for when the computer is turned off and can't be reached.
    - The master switch off stops hosting too: connected devices lose access at once (sockets closed with 4403, serve removed); the host switch's value is remembered and restored when the master goes back on.
    - **Status on the connecting side:** a remote environment stays listed (its projects hidden or greyed) with a clear status in the globe popover, the sidebar and Settings → Your environments:
      - **Connected**;
      - **Remote access turned off on <Mac>**: the last close was 4403 or a 403 `remote_disabled`, remembered even once the host becomes unreachable because serve was removed;
      - **<Mac> is offline**: unreachable, and Tailscale on this device reports the peer offline (`tailscale status --json` via the local server; best effort);
      - **Can't reach <Mac>**: unreachable for another reason (Tailscale off here, network), with a retry;
      - **Needs pairing**: 401 or revoked.
      
      Reconnect automatically with backoff; the status updates live. Some of this exists from I-126 (Needs pairing, "Remote access is off there"); extend it and make it consistent.


  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Server:** `meta.remote_master` plus a remembered `remote_host`; `remote_access` stays the effective hosting flag, so older servers stop hosting too. Master off stops hosting (4403, 403 `remote_disabled`, serve removed); master on restores the host switch. Migration: master = on if hosting was on. `PATCH /api/auth/remote {master}`; local-only `GET /api/auth/peers`.
    - **Web:** `state/remote-master.ts` (stored on the server, or on the device when there's no local server); `state/remote-status.ts` gives Connected / Remote access turned off on X (remembered) / X is offline (Tailscale peer offline) / Can't reach X (Retry) / Needs pairing, used in the popover, sidebar, Settings and pickers. A down environment's projects are hidden, and a greyed "<Mac> · status" row is shown instead.
    - **Settings layout:** master → Tailscale → Your environments → This Mac ("Let other devices use this Mac" → address, Devices, Recent activity).
  - Verified in two sandboxes with a fake read-only Tailscale CLI (11 screenshots, every state).

Remember the last settings section (per device, localStorage, like `lastRoute`) and open it instead, including the environment chosen in the settings switcher when it's a host section. Deep links to a specific section still win.

- [x] **I-134** Paired-device tokens in the macOS Keychain — `shell` / `infra` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the user wants the device tokens in the macOS Keychain → after the explanation, asked for it to be done right away.
  - Today: `state/saved-environments.ts` keeps `{envId, name, urls, token}` in localStorage (plain file in the web view's data).
  - Design:
    - In the Mac app, tokens live in the Keychain (service `io.github.jas7457.glade`, or the app identifier; account = environment id), through Tauri commands in `src-tauri` using the `keyring` crate (apple-native) or an equivalent; `get`/`set`/`delete`, capability-scoped to the main window.
    - The saved-environments list keeps only the non-secret fields.
    - One-time migration moves existing tokens out of localStorage and deletes them there.
    - In a plain browser (`pnpm dev`), localStorage stays as the fallback, clearly commented.
    - The iPhone app (F-022) will plug its own secure storage into the same small interface.
    - Removing an environment deletes its Keychain item; Pair Again overwrites it.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Rust:** `src-tauri/src/secrets.rs` adds `secret_get`/`secret_set`/`secret_delete` (`security-framework` 3; service = the app identifier; only `env:<id>` keys), allowed for the main window only (an app manifest in `build.rs` plus `allow-secret-*` in capabilities).
    - **Web:** `lib/secret-store.ts` has the `SecretStore` interface (desktop via the commands; localStorage fallback in browsers; `setSecretStore()` for F-022). saved-environments no longer holds tokens. A one-time migration removes a token from localStorage only after the store write succeeds. Connections wait for tokens to load.
  - Verified with a throwaway test under a test identifier; the real items are checked on the user's install. Note: rebuilt ad-hoc-signed apps may ask for Keychain access.
- [x] **I-135** System notifications, with a setting (reverses I-028), including remote Macs — `shell` / `settings` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — notifications need to work for chats on a remote Mac; the user considers that important to the whole remote setup → chose the lead's suggestion: banners for everything (local and remote) with a setting, by default for "needs input" and "finished", only while Glade isn't in front.
  - Design:
    - Settings → General → Notifications: when a chat needs your input (default on), when it finishes (default on), when it fails (default on?); plus "only when Glade is in the background" (default on).
    - A banner shows the chat title, the environment name for remote chats ("On Mac Studio"), and a short line (the question / the last sentence / the error). Clicking it focuses Glade and opens that chat (`/e/:env/…` for remote).
    - Driven by the client's merged state, so remote environments work the same as local, from sequenced sync events (no duplicates after a reconnect/replay; never notify for events older than the connection).
    - Mac app: `tauri-plugin-notification` (it was removed in I-028; re-add it with its permission prompt), with the web Notification API in a browser. The Dock badge and title count stay.
    - Sub-agents: notify only for the top-level chat, or setting-controlled (open question).

  - User check (2026-09-27): the macOS permission prompt appeared on the installed app. Update (2026-09-27): notifications worked in real use (a chat running on the MacBook Pro, driven from the Air).
- [x] **I-136** Remote Access: one "Connections" list for both directions, with "Connect to a Device…" / "Share This Device…" — `settings` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the difference between "environments" and "devices" is confusing. Don't say "Mac", since this may work on PCs eventually. Better: one "Your connections" list of what you're connected to and what's connected to you, with two buttons along the lines of "Connect to device" and "Share this device".
  - Proposal (lead):
    - The **Remote access** master switch and the Tailscale row stay at the top.
    - **Connections:** one list of every other device, each row with direction labels: **"You use it"** (it's an environment you connected to; its status: connected / offline / remote access off / needs pairing) and/or **"Uses this device"** (it's paired to use this one; last seen, connected dot). One device can be both, as a single row with both labels. Row actions depend on direction: Retry / Pair Again / Disconnect (remove the environment) · Rename / Revoke.
    - Two buttons above the list: **Connect to a Device…** (today's Connect to Environment) and **Share This Device…** (today's Add Device invite). If sharing is off, Share This Device… offers to turn it on first.
    - **"Sharing this device"** status line: on/off switch (today's "Let other devices use this Mac"), the address, and "Recent activity" below the list.
    - Wording: "device"/"this device"/"computer" in general text instead of "Mac"; the device's own name (e.g. "MacBook Air") wherever one is known.
  - Matching the two directions to one device: environment id ↔ a device's `clientEnvironmentId` (sent at pairing), so a Mac that both uses and shares shows as one row.

- [x] **I-137** Devices found on the tailnet only update when Settings is opened — `settings` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — remote access was enabled first on one Mac (Air), then on the other (Pro). The Pro lists the Air as a device to connect to, but the Air doesn't list the Pro; asked whether a refresh button for the list is needed.
  - Cause: `useDiscovered()` (`features/environments/Connections.tsx`, and the same in `ConnectEnvironmentDialog.tsx`) calls `GET /api/auth/discover` once, when the page mounts. A device that starts sharing later only appears after leaving Settings and coming back. The server side works: `/api/auth/discover` on the Air returned the Pro ("Jason’s MacBook Pro (2)", https://macbook-pro.taile347d9.ts.net, reachable) at the time of the report.
  - Fix: refresh discovery while the page is visible (every ~10 s, and on window focus / visibilitychange), plus a small **Refresh** button (with a spinner) next to the found-devices rows; a new device should appear within seconds. Keep one shared hook for both places. Also check the discovered name looks right ("Jason’s MacBook Pro (2)" comes from the computer name, so that's expected).

  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `features/environments/use-discovery.ts` is shared by Connections and the Connect dialog. It refreshes at once, every 10 s while visible, and on focus or visibility change; it never runs two requests at once, stops when hidden, and keeps the last list on errors. There's a "Found on your tailnet" row with Refresh.
- [x] **I-138** Connect to a Device: read-only target, no name field; each device names its connections itself — `settings` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the Connect dialog's two fields are unclear: "name of this device" (is it the device being connected to, or this one? It shows "MacBook Air" when connecting from the Air to the Pro), and the address, which shouldn't be editable; maybe a read-only field instead.
  - Notes: "Name of this device" is the connecting device's own name (shown on the other device's Allow prompt and Connections list), so "MacBook Air" is correct but unlabelled. The address is only needed when typing a short code; with a found device or a link it's already known.
  - Fix (`features/environments/ConnectEnvironmentDialog.tsx`):
    - When the target is known (found on the tailnet, a pasted or `/pair?link=` link, Pair Again), show a read-only "Connecting to <name> · <host>" line and no address field.
    - When a code is typed, show the address field with the found devices as quick picks and the hint "the address shown on the other device under Share This Device".
    - Relabel the name field "Show this device as", with the hint "How <target name> will list this device" (default: this device's name).
    - Tests.
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-27): a device shouldn't choose what others see it as. It connects with its default name, and the other machine can rename it; each machine is in charge of the names of its own connected devices, and a two-way connection shows one name on each side.
    - **No name field when connecting.** The device sends its own environment name automatically (the computer name, or its renamed environment name).
    - **Each device names its connections itself:** one local name per connected device, used for the whole Connections row (both directions), the sidebar/remote badge, pickers and notifications ("On <name>").
      - The host's Rename on a paired device already exists; make it the row's name.
      - Add Rename for devices you use: a client-side alias stored with the saved environment, falling back to the other device's own name.
      - For a device in both directions, one rename covers both (keyed by environment id ↔ clientEnvironmentId).
    - The Allow prompt shows the device's own name (e.g. "Allow ‘MacBook Air’…"); you can rename it afterwards.

- [x] **I-139** Add Project closes itself when you pick a remote device — `projects` / `bug` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — in Add Project, choosing an already-connected remote device makes the dialog disappear completely.
  - Where: `features/projects/AddProjectDialog.tsx`, the "Environment" `Select`. Its onChange sets `envId`, clears the path and reopens the `FolderBrowser` (keyed by envId) with `envFolderSource(envId)` → `requestFor(envId)` against the remote.
  - Suspects, to reproduce in two sandboxes first:
    - (a) The Select's pick is treated as an outside click and closes the Dialog.
    - (b) A render error when the remote `FolderBrowser` mounts (e.g. a remote `/fs/browse` answer, an auth/CORS failure, or an older remote without `/fs/browse`), which unmounts the dialog.
    - (c) Something that changes `addProjectOpen` on environment change (a navigation or route guard).
  - Fix and add a regression test. Also check the same pattern in the New Chat environment picker.
  - Wording: the dialog's "Environment" label and "remote" detail should follow I-136 (e.g. "Device", with the device's local name from `connectionName`).
  - Note (user, 2026-09-27): both Macs run the same Glade version.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - Root cause (a): Radix spots clicks in nested layers by bubbling through the React tree, but Preact portals only bubble through the DOM. So a pick in a portaled Select menu counted as an outside click and closed the Dialog. Every Select, Menu or popover inside a Dialog was affected (e.g. the Prompt editor's "Available in").
    - Fix: `ui/Dialog.tsx` ignores outside clicks and focus changes that land in a Radix floating layer (`[data-radix-popper-content-wrapper]`); clicking the backdrop still closes it.
    - "Environment" → "Device" with the device's local name. A failing remote browse stays inline.
    - New Chat pickers weren't affected.
    - Verified in two sandboxes, including creating a project on B from A.

- [x] **I-140** `/btw`: ask a side question while the agent keeps working — `chat` / `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — like Claude Code's `/btw`: ask the agent a question (or maybe add to the list of features) while it's still working, without interrupting it, and get an answer almost immediately instead of waiting for it to finish. Especially useful in sub-agent tasks.
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-27): option (b), answer only; no tools and no file edits.
  - Scope:
    - **The call:** `/btw <question>` in any chat (top-level or sub-agent tab), also while it's running. Glade makes a separate, short model call with the chat's context so far (including the in-progress turn) plus the question. The main run is untouched and the agent never sees the question or answer.
    - **The card:** the answer shows as a "Side question" card in the transcript, streaming, with a Stop button. Buttons:
      - **Tell the agent:** queue it or steer with it; prefill the composer with the note.
      - **Add to queue:** turn the note into a queued message for the main agent.
      - **Dismiss.**
    - **Storage:** cards are saved with the chat (Glade store) but excluded from the harness's context.
    - **Model:** the chat's model by default, with an optional cheaper "Side questions" model in Settings.
    - **Harness:** a capability (`sideQuestions`) with an implementation per harness. For pi, a one-off no-tools completion over the serialized context (e.g. a throwaway pi process / RPC without tools, or a direct provider call through pi's auth). For ACP, only if feasible; otherwise hide `/btw`.
    - **Remote:** works on remote environments (runs where the chat runs).
    - **Tests:** FakeHarness side-answer, and a card UI test. No real model in tests.
  - Addition (user, 2026-09-27): when the user has typed something while the agent is still running, suggest sending it as a side question, as a first-class feature: e.g. a button next to Send to send it as `/btw`, without the text shifting around when the chat becomes idle again.
    - Proposal: a permanent **Ask Aside** button next to Send/Stop (speech-bubble icon, tooltip "Ask aside (⌥↩): answered now, the agent won't see it").
      - It's always present, so nothing shifts when the chat goes idle. While idle it's quieter but still works, as a question that stays out of the agent's context.
      - Shortcut ⌥↩. `/btw <text>` stays as the typed form.
      - While the agent is running and there's text, the button gets the accent colour. The placeholder reads "Queue a message, or ⌥↩ to ask aside".
    - Decision (user, 2026-09-27): no button while the chat is idle (type `/btw` yourself then). While it's busy and the composer has text, recommend it. The button always keeps its space but is only visible and clickable while there's activity.
      - The button's slot is always reserved, so there's no layout shift. The button is visible and clickable only while the agent is running and the composer has text; otherwise it's invisible (`visibility: hidden`, not focusable).
      - ⌥↩ works only in that same state. When idle, use `/btw`.

  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Side call:** each question runs a separate `pi -p --mode json --no-session --no-tools --no-skills --no-context-files --no-prompt-templates --system-prompt … [--model … --thinking off]` (`harness/pi/side-question.ts`). The chat's pi process is never touched.
    - **Prompt:** built by the server from Glade's live transcript (including the in-progress turn, capped at 60k chars) plus the question.
    - **Streaming:** the answer streams from `text_delta`; Stop sends SIGTERM.
    - **Cost:** one request per question, thinking off, no shared cache.
    - **Protocol:** `side-questions.ts`, `side_*` events, the `sideQuestions` capability (pi and fake; ACP off, so it's hidden there), and `settings.models.sideQuestionModel`.
    - **Storage:** cards are stored in Glade's store, never in pi's session.
    - **Web:** `/btw`; a SideQuestionCard with Tell the Agent / Add to Queue / Dismiss; Ask Aside next to Stop/Send (slot reserved; visible only while running with text; ⌥↩). Works in sub-agent tabs and remote chats.
    - **Not yet run against a real model.
- [x] **I-141** Sub-agent pane: closing a tab shouldn't look like hiding the pane — `workspace` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — sub-agent tabs in the right pane are closable, and people may close one thinking it only hides the pane. Make it clearer that closing the tab kills the sub-agent, and make it easier to see how to hide the pane.
  - Today (`features/workspace/WorkspaceView.tsx`):
    - The sub-agent TabStrip passes `onClose`, so each tab has a ×. It runs `closeTab` (a "Close tab?" confirm, only when the tab has content) and deletes the sub-agent.
    - Hiding is a small `PanelRightClose` icon button at the far right ("Hide Sub-agents (Esc)").
  - Proposal:
    - **No × on sub-agent tabs.** Removing moves to the tab's right-click menu and the AgentBar's ⋯ menu as **Remove Sub-agent…**, with a clear confirm: "Remove Leo? Stops it if it's running and deletes its conversation. This can't be undone." (the confirm always shows, even for an empty tab).
    - **A clear Hide control:** a labelled **Hide** button (icon + text) where the × would be expected, i.e. at the right of the sub-agent tab bar. The tooltip keeps "Esc". A shortcut ⌘⌥B (or similar) toggles the pane. The sub-agent strip chip toggles the pane too.
    - Main-chat tabs keep their ×.
  - Tests: no close buttons on sub-agent tabs; Remove confirm; Hide / Esc / toggle.

  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Closing:** sub-agent tabs have no × and can't be closed by middle-click or ⌘W (⌘W in the pane hides it).
    - **Removing:** **Remove Sub-agent…** is in the tab's right-click menu and the AgentBar ⋯ menu, always confirmed (`removeSubagent()` shares its delete code with `closeTab`).
    - **Hiding:** a labelled **Hide** button at the right of the sub-agent tab bar; clicking the open agent's chip hides the pane; ⌥⌘B ("Toggle Sub-agents" in the palette) toggles it.
- [x] **I-142** Remote status: notice a device coming back sooner, and show status as an icon — `settings` / `sidebar` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — turning remote access off works, but after turning it back on, Settings on the other device still said remote access was off; asked when that refreshes. Also wants an icon showing whether a device is connected, not just the word, so it's easier to tell at a glance.
  - Cause: after a 4403/403 `remote_disabled`, the socket retries only every 30 s (`REMOTE_DISABLED_RETRY_MS` in `lib/socket.ts`). The host's peers poll is 20 s (`remote-status.ts`). So "Remote access turned off on …" can stay up to ~30 s after the other device turns it back on.
  - Fix:
    - **Retry sooner:** while remote-disabled, retry every ~5 s, and immediately on window focus / visibilitychange and when Settings → Remote Access opens.
    - **Discovery:** when tailnet discovery (I-137) sees that device reachable again, retry it right away.
    - **Peers:** the peers poll on the host follows the same focus rules.
  - **Status icon:** a small status dot/icon on each Connections row ("You use it" line) and in the globe badge popover, plus the device's status in the sidebar remote badge:
    - green: connected
    - amber: connecting / reconnecting
    - grey: remote access off / offline
    - red: needs pairing / can't reach
    The text stays as the tooltip and the label; the icon is readable at a glance.
  - Tests: the retry timing for the disabled state, and focus retry; the icon per state.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Retry:** while remote access is off on the other device, Glade retries every 5 s (was 30 s), and at once on focus / visibility, when Remote Access opens, or when discovery sees the device (`retryNow()`). The host's peers poll refreshes on focus while a device is unreachable.
    - **Status dot:** new `ui/StatusDot` (green / amber / grey / red, semantic tokens) on the Connections rows, the globe popover and as a tiny dot on the sidebar globe.
    - **Measured in sandboxes:** off shows within ~0.1 s; back on within ~4 s, or ~0.1 s on focus.

- [x] **I-143** Code-free pairing on your own tailnet: click Connect, compare a matching number, Allow — `settings` / `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — clicking Connect asks for a code. Instead, the machine being connected to could pop up the code, or a check that the codes match, so the user doesn't have to type one; asked whether that would be less secure. Decision: the user liked the proposed numeric-comparison pattern.
  - Design (numeric comparison, as in Bluetooth):
    - **Flow:** Connect on a device found on the tailnet sends a pair request without an invite code. The host shows "<device's own name> wants to use this device" with a 4-digit number and Allow / Deny. The client shows "Check that <host> shows 4729" and waits. Allow finishes pairing (the same token flow as today); Deny or a timeout (~2 min) cancels.
    - **The number:** derived from both sides' random nonces (e.g. the hash of the client nonce + the host nonce, mod 10000), so neither side picks it alone.
  - Security limits:
    - Code-free requests are accepted only over Tailscale Serve, and only when the request's Tailscale identity (`Tailscale-User-Login`, set by serve, can't be spoofed inside the tailnet) matches the host's own Tailscale login. Everyone else still needs an invite code / QR.
    - The host must have sharing on. Otherwise the request is refused silently.
    - At most one pending code-free request per host, a cooldown after Deny (e.g. 60 s), rate limiting per peer, and everything in the audit log.
  - Keep: invite code, link and QR code (for other accounts and phones later), and Pair Again.
  - Tests: server (identity match / mismatch, sharing off, one pending, cooldown, number derivation, Allow and Deny), client dialog states. No real Tailscale in tests (fake headers, fake CLI).
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Flow:** `POST /api/auth/pair {mode:"tailnet", clientNonce, …}` → the host nonce + request id. Number = SHA-256(client:host) mod 10000 (pure `protocol/sha256.ts`). The client long-polls `POST /api/auth/pair/wait`. The host's PendingPairingHost shows the number with Allow/Deny; Allow issues the same token as today.
    - **Security:**
      - sharing on;
      - the login counts only from a loopback TCP peer (i.e. via serve, which replaces client `Tailscale-User-*` headers);
      - it equals the host's own `tailscale status` login (tagged devices refused);
      - one pending; a 60 s cooldown after Deny; rate limits;
      - the wait must bring the same nonce (hash, constant-time) and login;
      - everything audited.
    - **Fallback:** refusals fall back to the code field with a reason.
    - **Storage:** no migration: `pairing_pending` with `invite_id='tailnet'`, plus `meta`.
    - **Known limit:** the host picks its nonce after seeing the client's, so no commit-reveal. Acceptable: TLS, same account, and the number is only a "right device" check.
    - **Verified** in two sandboxes with a fake CLI and a header-injecting proxy (8813 on both; a mismatched login fell back to the code).

- [x] **I-144** More agent names, and fewer repeats within a chat — `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — more variety in sub-agent names: prefer names not yet used in this chat, and grow the list from 60 to around 300.
  - Fix (`apps/server/src/services/agent-names.ts`):
    - **More names:** grow `AGENT_DISPLAY_NAMES` to ~300 friendly, distinct, easy-to-read first names (varied origins, no offensive or confusing ones, and no two too alike: keep the "first three letters differ" rule where possible, otherwise no exact or near-duplicates).
    - **Fewer repeats:** pick from names never used by any sub-agent of this chat (the whole parent session's history, including closed ones). Once all are used, pick the least recently used. Still unique among active agents.
    - Tests.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): 316 names (the original 60 plus 256 from varied origins; unique first three letters). `pickAgentIdentity(taken, history, random)` picks at random from names this chat has never used (the history from `ctx.agents.childrenOf`, closed agents included), then least recently used; always unique among active agents. Tests.

- [x] **I-145** Spawning a sub-agent shows "Running task <whole prompt>" until its card appears — `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — while an agent is being created, a "Running task" row shows until the agent card replaces it. Show the card sooner, or at least something like "Creating an agent", so it's more obvious what's happening.

    - Also make the plain tool row read "Starting agent <name>" rather than "Running task <prompt>".
    - Tests.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `AgentSpawnCard.tsx` shows a placeholder at once (spinner, short name, definition, "Starting an agent…") and becomes the normal card in place. The tool row reads "Starting/Started agent <name>". `harness/pi/tools.ts` treats namespaced spawn/message/close tools (`mcp__pi__spawn_agent`, …) like the plain ones and passes the `agent` argument as `ToolInput.agentDefinition`.

- [x] **I-146** Sub-agent "Task from main · <chat title>": the cut-off title has no tooltip — `chat` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — in a sub-agent tab there's what looks like a summary, cut off (e.g. "Glade Chat Interface adding project Ann…"), and hovering doesn't show the full text.
  - Notes: that text is the parent chat's title (where the task came from), not a summary. `features/chat/DelegatedCard.tsx`.
  - Fix: a tooltip with the full title. Make it clearer that it's the parent chat, e.g. "Task from **main** in “<full title>”" on hover, and clicking it opens the parent chat.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): DelegatedCard reads "Task from **main** in “<title>”", has a tooltip with the full title, and clicking it opens the parent chat.

- [x] **I-147** Keep the Mac awake while a chat is working, and while it's shared with a connected device — `shell` / `settings` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — for remote connections it matters that the connection doesn't die when the Mac goes to sleep; asked whether connections stay alive through sleep. Decision: the user agreed with the plan below.
  - Today: Glade holds no power assertion. This Mac's `pmset`: `sleep 1`, `womp 0`. When the host sleeps, its server, agents and Tailscale pause, so a device using it goes offline and a reply that was running is usually cut off (interrupted / Continue). The client reconnects and catches up after wake. A sleeping client is harmless.
  - Plan (desktop app, `src-tauri`; IOKit `IOPMAssertionCreateWithName` with `kIOPMAssertionTypePreventUserIdleSystemSleep`, the display may still sleep; no admin, no `pmset` changes):
    1. **While any chat on this server is working** (running or waiting to retry), hold the assertion. Always on, with a Settings → General toggle ("Keep this Mac awake while a chat is working", default on).
    2. **While sharing is on and another device is connected** (an active device socket), hold it too. Remote Access toggle "Keep this device awake while it's shared": default on when on AC power; on battery, an option (default off).
    3. **Status:** Remote Access (and General) shows "Keeping this device awake: <reason>". Release the assertion as soon as no reason is left, and on quit.
    4. **Hint:** a closed laptop lid still sleeps unless it's on power with an external display. Say so in the hint and the README (I-128).
  - The server decides the reasons and tells the desktop shell (it runs the server). A web-only `pnpm dev` doesn't hold assertions.
  - Tests: reason tracking (server, fake harness), toggle and AC/battery rules, the shell command contract (stubbed). Manual check: `pmset -g assertions` shows Glade's assertion while a sandbox chat runs, read-only.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Server:** `services/power.ts` works out the reasons (a chat working; sharing + a connected device, with AC/battery from a read-only `pmset -g batt`). It serves `GET /api/power`, a `power` push, and the long-poll `GET /api/desktop/state` for the shell. `Settings.power` holds whileWorking (on), whileShared (on) and whileSharedOnBattery (off).
    - **Shell:** `power.rs` holds a PreventUserIdleSystemSleep assertion, released on quit.
    - **UI:** a Power group in General and keep-awake rows in Remote Access, with the closed-lid hint.
    - **Verified by hand** in a test bundle: `pmset -g assertions` showed "Glade: a chat is working" while a chat ran, gone after.

- [x] **I-148** Sub-agent chip tooltip: a short task summary instead of the whole prompt — `workspace` / `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — hovering a sub-agent chip above the composer shows its whole, very long prompt; the user assumed a shorter version exists that could be shown instead.
  - Today: there's no short version. The tooltip (`features/workspace/SubagentStrip.tsx`) is "<Name> · <functional name> — <status> · <time> · <model>", then the latest activity (e.g. `report_done`), then "Task: <the whole spawn prompt>". Sub-agents don't get a generated title: title generation only runs for a first message typed by the user (`services/app/titles.ts`, `session-actions.ts`).

Store it as the sub-agent session's title, so it also shows in its tab and card.
    - **Tooltip:** "<Name> · <title> — <status> · <time> · <model>", then "Now: <activity in words>" (e.g. "Reporting back", not `report_done`), then "Click to open/hide". The full task stays in the sub-agent's Task card.

The tab reads "<Name> · <title>". Tooltip: "Name · title — status · time · model", then "Now:/Latest: <activity in words>" (report_done → "Reporting back", …; commands and files keep their text), then "Click to open/hide". Fallback: "<functional name>: <first sentence, ≤100 chars>".

- [x] **I-149** Know when this app is behind: build stamp, "commits behind", older Glade on other devices — `shell` / `settings` / `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — since the app is built locally, it can't know whether a newer version exists; asked whether the app could somehow tell that it's behind. Decision: no Update button yet; that part is F-024.
  - Plan:
    - **Build stamp:** `pnpm tauri:install` stamps the git commit, the build time and "dirty" into the app/server. Settings → About shows "Built from 1f00e8a on <date>" (+ "local changes").
    - **Behind check:** a read-only `git ls-remote` of origin main with the user's existing git credentials (no fetch, no writes), only when the build's repo folder exists on this Mac. Shows "N commits behind main" (count via the local repo if it has the commit, else just "Update available") / "Up to date" / "Couldn't check". Runs at startup and every few hours.
    - **Other devices:** `GET /api/environment` also reports the commit and build time. Connections shows "Running an older Glade (N commits behind this device)" or "newer", with a hint when a feature needs both (e.g. quick pairing).
    - **Notice:** quiet only: an "Update available" line in Settings → About and a small dot on Settings. Include the update command to copy (`git pull && pnpm install && pnpm tauri:install`).
    - Tests (stubbed git, fake environments).
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Stamp:** `bundle-server.mjs` injects `__GLADE_BUILD__` (sha, time, dirty, repo); `pnpm dev` reads live git. `GET /api/environment` includes `build` (not for unauthenticated callers).
    - **Behind check:** `services/update-check.ts` runs a read-only `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` (no prompt, 20 s timeout), counting with `rev-list` only when both commits are local. At startup, every 4 h, and on demand (`GET /api/version`, `POST /api/version/check`, `GET /api/version/compare`).
    - **UI:** Settings → About (build, status, Check Now, a copyable update command, a quit-completely hint), a quiet dot on Settings when behind, and Connections shows older/newer Glade per device.
    - **Install:** the `install.mjs` message now says to use Quit Glade Completely.
    - **Real check** against this repo: up to date (and HEAD~5 → "behind 5").

  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — Glade needs a way to run in the background: closing the window should let sub-agents continue and keep this device available to other Glade environments, with something in the menu bar to really quit it. Then: the user likes both optional ideas. ⌘Q should keep Glade running in the background in the menu bar, and the way to fully close everything is quitting from the menu bar. Like Tailscale, it can live only in the menu bar while still offering a full window.

    - Tests: the quit-path decisions (pure), settings, the tray menu model (stubbed).
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-27): Open Glade should bring you back to the last screen you were on, not to a new chat.
    - Open Glade (menu bar, Dock, Spotlight) restores the window at the route it was on when hidden/closed. Keep the web view alive while hidden (just hide the window); if it had to be recreated, restore the last route (persisted).
    - New Chat stays a separate menu item. A notification click still goes to its chat.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Shell modules:** `tray.rs` (6 template icons from `scripts/make-tray-icons.swift`), `shell_state.rs` (long-polls the server), `dock.rs`, `prefs.rs`, `login_item.rs`, and `quit.rs` / `menu.rs`.
    - **Quitting:** the app menu has Quit Glade ⌘Q (closes to the menu bar when the setting is on) and Quit Glade Completely ⌥⌘Q. The Dock's Quit, AppleScript and installer quits fully quit after the running-chats check; logout, restart and shutdown quit at once.
    - **Settings:** General → "Glade app" group: Show in Dock, Open at login, the ⌘Q toggle. `menuBarActions.ts` opens chats from the menu.
    - **Verified by hand:** with Show in Dock off, the app launches as UIElement.
    - **Not yet seen by hand:** the tray icon and menu, ⌘Q → menu bar, the first-time notice, Open at login, logout. These need the installed app (the user's check).
    - **Note:** Open at login opens the window rather than starting hidden. Test builds with `GLADE_APP_IDENTIFIER` pass `GLADE_HARNESS` through.
  - Follow-up (user, 2026-09-27): a Settings item in the menu would be useful, and the menu bar icon should be the normal Glade leaf. Status badges on it are fine, but nothing that looks like a network/Wi-Fi symbol. Done (lead): the icon is the leaf with the working (ring) / needs-you (dot) badges, but no sharing arcs. The menu gains **Settings…** (the same menu action as ⌘,).
  - User check (2026-09-27): everything except keep-awake was tried and works well.
  - Fix (user, 2026-09-27): the open menu bar menu closes whenever something updates (e.g. it closed when an agent finished while the menu was open). Cause: `refresh` replaced the tray menu (`set_menu`) on every state change, which closes an open NSMenu. Now one menu is kept (`TrayMenu` in app state) and updated in place: nothing when the model is unchanged, `set_text/enabled/checked` when only labels change, and removing/appending items in the same menu when lines come or go (e.g. "needs you").

- [x] **I-153** Composer: drop the "Send message with" and "While the agent is working" settings; ↩ sends, ⌘↩ sends a follow-up — `chat` / `settings` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — remove these two settings: Enter always sends and Shift+Enter makes a new line, with no option. Switch between steer and follow-up with ⌘: holding ⌘ changes the Send button to look like a follow-up, and ⌘↩ sends a follow-up. Asked whether that would work.
  - Keys while the agent is working:
    - ↩ steers (today's default `busyBehavior: "steer"`).
    - ⌘↩ sends a **follow-up** (waits until the agent has finished).
    - ⌥↩ asks aside (I-140).
    - ⇧↩ inserts a new line.

Letting go switches back ("Steer (↩): delivered after the current step"). Clicking while ⌘ is held sends a follow-up. Same size and slot, so nothing shifts.
  - The placeholder while running: "↩ steer · ⌘↩ follow-up · ⌥↩ ask aside" (short).
  - **Remove settings:** `general.sendKey` and `general.busyBehavior`, including the Settings UI and their tests. Keep old values tolerated when read, and drop them on write.
  - Harnesses without steering: ↩ and ⌘↩ both queue a follow-up (as today).
  - Tests: the key matrix (idle/running × ↩/⌘↩/⌥↩/⇧↩), button icon while ⌘ is held (keydown/keyup/blur), settings removed.
