Holding ⌘ while running swaps the button to "Send follow-up" (`ClockArrowUp`, `use-meta-held.ts`, reset on blur/visibility); a click while it's held sends a follow-up. Running placeholder: "↩ steer · ⌘↩ follow-up · ⌥↩ ask aside". Without steering both keys queue. `sendKey`/`busyBehavior` were removed from protocol, defaults and General; `migrateSettings` drops old values, and writes strip them. The server defaults to steer.

- [x] **I-154** Update Now: pull, install, rebuild and relaunch from Settings → About (promoted from F-024) — `shell` / `settings` / `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — can Glade run the update and restart itself for the user? Closing is probably possible; asked whether it can relaunch too.
  - Plan:
    - **Update Now** in About when behind. The server runs a background job in the stamped repo folder through a login shell (so node/pnpm/cargo are on PATH), streaming its progress and log to the page:
      1. `git pull --ff-only`
      2. `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`
      3. `pnpm tauri:install`
    - **Guards:** refuse with a clear reason when the repo has uncommitted changes, isn't on main, or can't fast-forward. Only one update at a time; Cancel is allowed before the install step; failures show the log tail.
    - **Relaunch:** when installed, "Restart Glade to finish". The shell quits completely (bypassing ⌘Q-to-menu-bar and stopping the server cleanly) and relaunches the new bundle (Tauri `restart()` / `open` after exit), then reopens at the same screen.
      - If chats are working, it offers "Restart when chats finish" or "Restart now" (running chats become interrupted/Continue).
    - Only the desktop app offers it (the `pnpm dev` server just shows the command).
    - Tests: job steps with stubbed commands (success, dirty repo, non-ff, failure mid-build, cancel), About UI states, the restart decision. Manual: a sandboxed test bundle only; never update or relaunch the user's installed app from tests.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Job:** `services/update-job.ts` (desktop server only): guards (repo exists, clean, on main, `git fetch` + `merge-base --is-ancestor`), then pull → `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` → `pnpm tauri:install` via `$SHELL -lc` with `~/.cargo/env` sourced. A 400-line log, one job at a time, Cancel until `tauri:install` (it kills the process group).
    - **API:** `GET/POST /api/version/update` and `/cancel`, local owner only, plus an `update` push.
    - **Web:** `UpdateNow.tsx` / `state/update.ts`: steps, log, "Restart Glade to finish", and Restart When Chats Finish / Restart Now / Don't Wait.
    - **Shell:** `relaunch.rs` records the bundle path at startup, saves the route, spawns a detached helper that waits for exit then `open -n <bundle>`, and quits completely (server stopped, no prompt).
    - **Install:** `install.mjs` accepts `GLADE_INSTALL_TARGET`.
    - **Verified by hand** in a throwaway bare-origin + clone + temp bundle: the real pull/install/rebuild ran with live steps; relaunch started the new bundle on the new commit; refusals and Cancel worked.
    - **Only in tests:** the restart-with-working-chats buttons, and the window opening at the saved route.

- [x] **I-155** Settings for a multi-device, multi-agent world: an Agents page, per-device AI settings, the host decides which agents it offers — `settings` / `server` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the available models depend on which remote connections you have and which harnesses each computer has set up. Listing "pi" in the settings sidebar doesn't make sense; rename it to "harnesses" or a friendlier term, a page to enable/disable and configure harnesses. Asked what the Models page shows (one merged list across all devices and harnesses?) and the same for slash commands. Each device should declare which harnesses it can use, and other devices can only use the harnesses that device offers.
  - Today: the AI pages (Models, pi/Agents, Slash Commands, Prompts) are already per device. With 2+ devices connected, a small environment switcher appears on top of those pages (`SettingsEnvironmentSwitcher`, hidden with one device). The agent page is named after the only harness ("pi").
  - Proposal:
    1. **"Agents" page (always this name)** lists every supported agent (pi, Claude Code, Gemini CLI, Codex, other ACP agents) with installed/not found, an Enable toggle, its settings (pi's page content; the ACP command/env), and install links when missing (overlaps F-023).
    2. **The device owns its agents.** Only enabled + installed agents are offered, to this device's chats and to every device using it. Other devices see and use only what it offers (the agent pickers, sub-agents, `/btw`).
    3. **One clear device switcher for the whole AI group:** "Settings for [This Mac ▾]" at the top of the AI group in the Settings sidebar, instead of per page. It's always shown once you use another device, and every AI page says which device it belongs to.
    4. **Models = this device's models, grouped by agent then provider** ("pi · anthropic", "Claude Code"). No merged cross-device list: a model only means something where it runs. Defaults (default agent + model, small model, side questions, sub-agents) are per device. The composer's model picker already shows the chat's device + agent only.
    5. **Slash commands: per device, grouped by source** (Glade's, the agent's own, project folders). The composer shows the chat's device + agent.
  - Open question: may another device *change* this device's agent settings (enable/disable, configure) through the switcher, as it can today, or only view them?
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-27): a device's harness settings are changed on that device itself; you can't turn on harnesses on another computer, only your own.
    - Agent settings (enable/disable, per-agent config) can only be changed on the device itself: the server refuses these writes from paired devices (403, only the local owner). Other devices see them read-only, with "Change this on <device>".
    - Confirmed (user, 2026-09-27): another device's settings are view-only and can only be edited on that device. This covers all host settings (agents, models and defaults, slash commands, prompts): the server refuses writes from paired devices, and the switcher shows remote devices read-only.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Server:** `harness/which.ts` (cached PATH lookup) and `isInstalled()`. `harness/agent-catalog.ts` lists Claude Code (`claude-agent-acp`/`claude-code-acp`), Gemini CLI (`gemini --experimental-acp`) and Codex (`codex-acp`); user entries override them. `HarnessRegistry` offers only installed + `settings.agents.<id>.enabled` agents (in `/api/harnesses`, defaults, new chats, sub-agents and inherited tabs). `GET /api/agent-catalog` lists all of them with install links.
    - **Turned-off agents:** chats on them stay readable, and sending returns 409 "<agent> is turned off on <device>".
    - **Writes:** `PATCH /settings` from a paired device → 403 `local_only`, except appearance-only patches (a remote browser's own look).
    - **Web:** the section is always **Agents** (one card per agent: status, Enable, settings inline, install link). `SettingsDeviceSwitcher` ("Settings for [device ▾]") sits at the top of the AI group, and each AI page names its device. Another device → view-only note + a disabled fieldset. Models are grouped by agent · provider; Slash Commands by Glade / agent / project folders.
    - **Verified** with two sandboxes (view-only, 403, turning off Echo ACP on B removed it from A's picker for B).
    - **Open:** in view-only mode the Slash Commands filter/Refresh are disabled too.

- [x] **I-156** Side questions: ask follow-ups in the same card (a small thread), and give the side call enough context — `chat` / `server` (2026-09-29)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — asked whether a side question (`/btw`) can have follow-ups, i.e. more than one message.
  - Today: one question, one answer per card. The answer only saw the last ~60k characters of the chat, so it wrongly said "the conversation doesn't show any mobile plans yet" (F-022 was discussed much earlier).
  - Plan:
    - **Thread:** a Reply field under the answer (↩ sends, same Stop). Each follow-up is sent with the card's earlier side questions and answers plus the chat context, and appended to the same card. Still never seen by the agent. Tell the Agent / Add to Queue act on the whole thread (or the latest answer; decide in review).
    - **Context:** when the chat is long, include the chat's compaction summary / the start of the chat, plus the recent tail, instead of only the tail. Consider the project's PLAN/AGENTS files for questions about plans (read-only, capped). Say "(only saw part of the chat)" when context was cut.
    - Tests.
  - Outcome (2026-09-29): a Reply field under the answer continues the same card as a thread (never seen by the agent; Stop / Dismiss cover the streaming follow-up). Follow-ups are sent with the card's earlier Q&A (up to 20k chars, newest kept) plus the chat. Context budget 100k chars; a longer chat sends its start (15k), your own messages from the middle (up to 15k) and the recent tail, and the card says "(only saw part of the chat)". Tell the Agent / Add to Queue pass on the whole thread. Protocol add-only (`SideQuestionTurn`, `followUps`, `parentId`, `partialContext`); follow-ups live inside the parent card's stored message (no migration).
    - Not done, by decision: pi's compaction summary isn't in the context (Glade keeps only "Compacted from N tokens"; start + middle + tail cover it; keeping the summary needs a pi translator change), and PLAN/AGENTS files aren't added (too big; the side call runs with `--no-context-files`).

- [x] **I-157** Store images as files, not inside the database — `server` / `store` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — asked how big the database is; then: images should definitely not be stored in the database, but as files the database points to.
  - Measured (read-only, 2026-09-27): `glade.db` 58 MB. `tool_results` holds 40 MB, of which 38 MB is 105 base64 screenshots (the largest 1.4 MB). Messages 12 MB, events 4 MB.
  - Plan:
    - **Blob store:** content-addressed files in the data folder, `blobs/<sha256[0:2]>/<sha256>.<ext>`, written atomically, deduplicated.
    - **References:** the DB and wire payloads carry `{ type: "image", blob: "sha256:…", mimeType, width?, height? }` instead of `data`. This covers tool-result images, user-pasted/attached images in messages, and any other inline image payloads.
    - **Serving:** `GET /api/blobs/:hash` (immutable, long cache; auth like other API reads, so paired devices can fetch from the host). The web resolves blob refs to URLs per environment (remote chats fetch from their host).
    - **Harness context unchanged:** pi still gets real images. Blobs are resolved back to data when feeding a harness, and pi's own session files are untouched.
    - **Migration:** move existing inline images out to blobs (a new migration; the older installed app refuses the DB afterwards, so install first as usual). Keep it idempotent and crash-safe (write the blob, then update the row).
    - **Cleanup:** delete blobs no longer referenced when chats are deleted (a periodic GC with a grace period). Optionally "remove images older than N days" in Settings later.
    - Tests: the store (dedupe, atomic write, GC), migration, serving + auth, the round trip in transcript/sync, and harness feeding.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Blob store:** `store/blobs.ts` (`<dataDir>/blobs/<aa>/<sha256>.<ext>`, temp file + rename, dedupe). Refs are `{type:"image", mimeType, blob:"sha256:…", width?, height?}`, converted in `live-pool.ts` (every harness event) and in `Store` writes/imports; old `data` images still read.
    - **Migration:** 005 is a version bump; then `store/migrate-images.ts` converts rows in batches (blob first, then a compare-and-set row update) and sets `meta.images_to_blobs`. A one-time VACUUM runs only when no other server is registered.
    - **GC:** unreferenced blobs go after a 1 h grace (60 s after a chat deletion, 10 min after start, then every 6 h). Re-saving refreshes the mtime.
    - **Serving:** `GET /api/blobs/:hash` (immutable, ETag, normal API auth). The web's `image-src.ts` uses a plain `<img>` URL locally and a token fetch → `blob:` URL for paired remotes.
    - **pi** still gets real data (`PromptImage.blob` is resolved server-side).
    - **Measured** on a /tmp copy of the real DB: 60.4 MB → 12.8 MB (147 images → 146 files, 34 MB).
    - **Open:** an older server still running during the migration could write new inline images (readable, but not moved); remote `blob:` URLs aren't revoked.

- [x] **I-163** Images per chat: each chat's image files live in its own folder and are deleted with it (no sharing, no delayed cleanup) — `store` (2026-09-27)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — the delayed cleanup (waiting, files having to be over an hour old) seems overcomplicated; clean up immediately. Also drop the "identical images are stored once" sharing: keep it simple and store copies.
  - Today (I-157): content-addressed, deduplicated blobs shared across chats, with GC 60 s after a delete / 10 min after start / every 6 h for unreferenced files older than 1 h.
  - Plan:
    - **Per-chat folders:** `<dataDir>/blobs/<sessionId>/<n>.<ext>` (or a short random name), written atomically. The ref is `{ type:"image", blob:"<sessionId>/<name>", mimeType, … }`. No dedupe: a picture pasted into two chats is stored twice.
    - **Deleting a chat** (and a sub-agent, and a project's chats) removes its folder right after the rows are deleted. Nothing else.
    - **Remove all GC:** the timers, the grace period, reference scanning, mtime refresh.
    - **Serving:** `GET /api/sessions/:id/images/:name` (or keep `/api/blobs/<sessionId>/<name>`), same auth as today, immutable cache. Update the web resolver.
    - **Migration:** move the existing hash blobs into per-chat folders (copy when shared) and rewrite the refs. Idempotent and crash-safe; delete the old hash files once no row points at them. Also accept old `sha256:` refs until migrated.
    - Tests: per-chat write, delete removes the folder, a copy per chat, the migration (incl. a shared blob copied to two chats), serving + auth, the remote fetch.
  - Addition (user, 2026-09-27): is everything cleaned up, including other uploaded files? The user expects one folder per chat holding all its assets, deleted as a whole. Also asked whether the database is fully cleaned up too, and how this works with remote connections.
    - **One folder per chat:** `<dataDir>/chats/<sessionId>/` holds `images/` and `files/` (uploaded attachments, formerly `<dataDir>/attachments/<sessionId>`). Deleting the chat removes the whole folder. Existing attachment folders migrate in.
    - **DB:** besides sessions/messages/tool_results/transcripts/summaries/agents, also delete the session's `events` rows (sync-log copies of message payloads, otherwise kept up to 7 days), except the deletion event clients need, and any other per-session rows.
    - **Remote:** a chat exists only on its host. Deleting it from any device deletes it on the host, and every connected device drops it via sync (offline ones on catch-up). Clients keep nothing on disk.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Layout:** `<dataDir>/chats/<sessionId>/{images,files}`. Image names are a 16-hex SHA prefix, so a re-save in the same chat reuses the file; two chats = two files, and a cross-chat ref is copied in. The ref is `"<sessionId>/<name>"`.
    - **Deleting** a chat, tab, sub-agent, workspace or project rm -rf's the folder right after the rows, and deletes the session's `events` rows except the deletion event. All GC removed.
    - **Serving:** `GET /api/blobs/<sessionId>/<name>` (strictly validated, images/ only), plus legacy `/api/blobs/<hash>` until migrated.
    - **Migration:** schema 006 + a compare-and-set pass (copy into the row's chat, rewrite, `meta.images_per_chat=done`), then old hash files and `blobs/` are removed. `attachments/<sid>` moves to `chats/<sid>/files`.
    - **Measured** on a read-only copy of the real data: 149 rows rewritten in 127 ms; 154 old files → 152 files in 1 chat folder; re-run no-op.
    - **Open:** moved attachment paths only resolve for Reveal (an agent re-reading an old path won't find it). A one-off unhandled error was seen once in `agents.test.ts` (not reproduced in 7 runs).

- [x] **I-180** Conversation mode on the iPhone: hands-free voice with Apple voices (promoted from F-012) — `iphone` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-09-29 — asked what a conversation (voice) mode would take, without starting it. Then: iPhone first, hands-free, Apple voices only. Answers to the lead's questions: only with the screen on, and keep the screen awake during voice mode; while the agent works, play something every so often so the user knows it's still going; defaults for the rest. Then gave the go.
  - Decisions (user + lead defaults the user accepted):
    - iPhone only for now; Apple on-device speech recognition and Apple voices only (no cloud, no keys, free).
    - Hands-free: listens, sends after a pause, reads the reply, listens again; talking over the reply stops it and listens (barge-in, echo cancellation).
    - Entry: a waveform button in the chat composer and on New Chat; a full-screen voice view for that chat (listening / thinking / speaking), closing returns to the chat with everything in the transcript.
    - Screen on only; the screen stays awake while voice mode is open; no background audio.
    - Replies read in full with the current word highlighted; code blocks and tool output announced, not read.
    - Permission cards by voice: read the question, answer "yes" / "yes, always" / "no" (then listen for what to do instead).
    - Voice picker + speed in Settings, starting on the best installed voice (Premium > Enhanced > default).
    - While the agent works: a soft cue when it starts, then a gentle periodic cue so the user knows it's still working.
  - Plan: contract `apps/iphone/src/voice/engine.ts` (lead); worker A the native side (a Tauri iOS plugin: speech recognition with partial results and end-of-speech, synthesis with word boundaries, voice list, audio session with voice processing for barge-in, keep-awake, mic/speech permissions); worker B the voice view, the conversation state machine, reading text, voice permission answers, cues, settings, against a fake engine. Real voice testing is the user's, on the phone.

  - Outcome (2026-10-05): the user considers it working on the iPhone; ticked. Device-only aspects listed below were not separately confirmed.
  - Status (2026-09-30): built and merged, **waiting for the user's test on the iPhone** (not ticked).
    - Native (Chiara): Swift Tauri plugin `apps/iphone/src-tauri/plugins/voice`: on-device SFSpeechRecognizer (one task per utterance, silence → final, renewed before the 1-min limit), replies synthesized with `write()` and played through a voice-processing AVAudioEngine so echo cancellation covers them, word events timed from markers against playback, `.playAndRecord`/`.voiceChat`, keep-awake, soft cues generated in code, interruptions/route changes. Simulator: speaking with word timing, stop, voice list (53), cues, both permission prompts. Recognition never ran in the simulator: macOS's microphone prompt for the Simulator was left unanswered, and while it's pending CoreAudio's input init times out and aborts the app (seen by the lead too, 2026-09-30 00:04, `AURemoteIO::Initialize` RPC timeout) — a simulator-only condition.
    - UI (Eunice): fake engine, pure state machine + `Conversation` runner (listening → sending → working with a start cue and a tick every 4.5 s → speaking → listening; barge-in; talking while it works steers/queues), speakable text with a map back to the markdown for the word highlight, permission answers by voice (kind → label → position; "no" asks what to do instead), full-screen voice view, waveform button in the chat composer and New Chat (`sendAccessory` / `startRef` props in app-core), Settings → Voice (Automatic = best installed English voice, grouped voices, preview, rate). Checked in the simulator on the fake engine.
    - Needs the device: recognition quality and silence timing, echo cancellation / barge-in on the speaker, loudness in `.voiceChat`, AirPods, cues, Personal Voice.
    - Not done: highlight in the transcript itself (map exists), streaming sentence by sentence, speaking non-permission questions.
- [x] **I-181** iPhone voice mode: the word highlight jumps around — `iphone` (2026-09-30)
  - Reported: 2026-09-30 — the word highlight in voice mode jumps around a lot and doesn't track the text well; look into it. (Also asked where the voice list comes from: only one Enhanced voice shows. Answer: it's the phone's installed voices, `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice.speechVoices()`, filtered to the device language + English; Enhanced/Premium only appear once downloaded in iOS Settings.)
  - Suspects (lead, from the code): (1) `byteSampleOffset` may restart per synthesis chunk/sentence while `onMarkers` sorts all pending marks by it, scrambling the order on longer replies; (2) markers arriving after playback has passed them release several words at once; (3) the byte→frame conversion assumes one buffer format for the whole utterance; (4) `scrollIntoView({behavior: "smooth"})` on every word makes the text itself move.
  - Plan: trace real marker values for a long multi-paragraph reply (Enhanced voice if one can be installed in the simulator), fix the timing (e.g. map markers against cumulative scheduled frames per buffer, keep text order), steadier scrolling (only when the word leaves a comfortable band, no smooth scroll per word); tests for the pure parts.

  - Outcome (2026-09-30): cause found by tracing: `AVSpeechSynthesizer.write` renders long text in chunks, each ending with an empty buffer, and the next chunk's `byteSampleOffset` restarts at 0, so sorting pending markers by it interleaved paragraphs (words emitted at positions 13, 261, 511, 17, …) and released later chunks' words in bursts. Fix (`VoicePlugin.swift` `MarkTimeline`): markers placed at the previous chunks' frames + offset, kept in text order, never backwards, only the latest reached word reported per tick, punctuation-only markers skipped; the reply ends at `didFinish` (not the first empty buffer). Measured in the simulator: 159 words, none backwards, lag ≤ 42 ms (median 15). The view scrolls only when the word leaves a 15–65 % band (`follow-scroll.ts`). Trace kept behind `-GladeVoiceTrace YES`. Left for the phone: Enhanced voices and the real-time feel.

- [x] **I-196** Local models: load and unload models on the Mac Studio from any device (llama-server first) — `server` / `settings` / `iphone` (2026-09-30)
  - Reported: 2026-09-30 — a main purpose of Glade is connecting to local models. They can't all run at once, so the user needs to load and unload them, ideally from anywhere: another room, or the phone remotely. After the research check (LM Studio bug #2040: GGUF prefill 2–3× slower on M5 in LM Studio's llama.cpp; the user's local-model research (ADR-0006) recommends `llama-server` router mode as pi's daily backend): queue it and start on it right away.
  - Design: Glade's server talks to the model server on its own Mac (localhost; never exposed), devices talk to Glade over the existing remote access. Backend adapter, first `llama-server` router mode (`GET /models`, `POST /models/load|unload`); LM Studio (`/api/v1/models/load|unload`) later. Contract in `packages/protocol/src/local-models.ts`: `GET /api/local-models`, `POST /api/local-models/load|unload`, `local_models` push, `Settings.localModels.url` (default `http://127.0.0.1:8080`, also `LLAMA_BASE_URL` for pi). UI: a Local Models page (desktop Settings → AI, per Mac with the device switcher; actions allowed on other Macs) and on the iPhone: models with status, size, context, Load/Unload, loading progress, memory used vs this Mac's GPU budget, a "not running" state with setup help, a warning before unloading a model chats use. After a load/unload, pi's model list refreshes so loaded llama.cpp models appear in the picker.
  - Later (not this round): pick an unloaded model in a chat → load it (offer to unload others that don't fit); LM Studio backend; starting/stopping `llama-server` itself from Glade.
  - Out of scope (user, 2026-09-30): **no automatic idle unloading**, ever; Glade never unloads a model on its own. **No model downloads** in Glade for now: the user handles that on the host machine.
  - Considered (2026-09-30): a "Loaded now" section and a one-tap "Switch to this" (unload current + load new). User: keep just the Load/Unload buttons for now, until they see how their workflow turns out. Revisit after real use on the Mac Studio.
  - Testing: no llama.cpp on this MacBook yet; built against a fake router (tests + sandbox). Real check: `brew install llama.cpp` + one tiny GGUF.
  - Outcome (2026-09-30): server `services/local-models/` (backend interface + `llama-server` router adapter: statuses incl. failed/sleeping/downloading→loading, size from `meta.size` or the GGUF file(s) on disk, context from `n_ctx`/`-c`, single-model and "not llama-server" errors, `LLAMA_API_KEY` as bearer), `LocalModelsService` (polls 2 s while loading, 30 s otherwise, only with a client connected; dedupes; pushes `local_models` incl. through the sync hub; `usedBy` from chats on `llama.cpp` models; refreshes agents' models when the loaded set changes), routes `GET/POST /api/local-models[/load|/unload]` (paired devices allowed; 400/404/409/502; `contextLength` accepted but ignored: the router's load takes only `{model}`), `LLAMA_BASE_URL` from `Settings.localModels.url` for every pi process. pi's first model list after a load was one step stale, so forced refreshes ask twice 1.5 s apart. Verified `--models-max 0` disables llama-server's own LRU eviction (`unload_lru` returns when `models_max <= 0`); the setup help recommends it. UI: app-core `state/local-models.ts` (per Mac, optimistic loading, plain-words errors), `features/local-models/` (shared hook + desktop panel), primitives `ui/Meter`, `ui/CopyableCode`; desktop Settings → Local Models (AI group; Load/Unload work for other Macs, Server URL only on this Mac; ⌘K entry); iPhone: Device screen → Local Models row ("N loaded" / "Not running") → LocalModelsScreen. Unload of a model chats use and a load that may not fit ask first. Loaded models sort first; a loading one stays in place (lead). Also fixed: `requestOpenChat` counted server-side listeners as windows. Checked in sandboxes against `scripts/fake-llama-server.mjs` (desktop, iPhone layout); real llama.cpp not yet tried. Note: on this MacBook :8080 is taken by another local site server.

- [x] **I-197** Restart into the new version automatically once an update is installed (no second click) — `shell` / `settings` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — after an update, a second click is needed to restart the app; once an install finishes, the app should restart automatically.
  - Notes:
    - Today: **Update Now** (I-154, `apps/web/src/features/settings/UpdateNow.tsx`, `apps/web/src/state/update.ts`) ends at "Restart Glade to finish" with a **Restart Glade** button (or Restart When Chats Finish / Restart Now when chats are working). **`pnpm tauri:install`** (`apps/desktop/scripts/install.mjs`), which an agent runs from a chat, only prints "restart it to use the new version". The restart itself already exists (`relaunch.rs`: quits completely, reopens the new bundle at the same screen).
    - Proposal: once the install finishes, restart on its own using the existing rules: no chats working → restart right away; chats working → go straight into "Restarting when chats finish…" (cancelable, Restart Now still there) instead of asking. Restarting never cuts off a working chat without the user choosing it.
    - `tauri:install` path: the chat that runs the install is itself working, so "when chats finish" naturally waits for that agent's turn to end. The running app needs to learn a new bundle was installed: e.g. the shell watches its own bundle (`relaunch.rs` already records the bundle path; compare the build stamp / `Info.plist` version on change), or `install.mjs` tells the running app through its local server. One trigger for both paths is simplest. Only for the installed app (`/Applications/Glade.app`), never for `tauri dev`, sandboxes or test bundles with their own identifier.
    - Drafts in composers must survive the restart (check they're persisted before quitting).
  - Decided (2026-10-05): restart **right away** when no chats are working (no countdown). **No setting** to turn auto-restart off.
  - Acceptance: Update Now with no chats working restarts into the new version with no extra click; with a chat working it waits and restarts when that chat finishes; an agent running `pnpm tauri:install` from a chat gets the app restarted after its turn ends; Cancel stops it. Test in a throwaway bundle (as I-154 did), never the user's installed app.
  - Outcome (2026-10-05): the bundle carries its stamp as `app/build.json`; the desktop server polls it every 3 s (`services/installed-build.ts`) and reports a different build as `VersionStatus.installed` (GET `/api/version` + new `version` push; held back while Update Now still runs so the restart can't cut off the installer). The Mac app's own window (`startAutoRestart()`, `apps/web/src/state/update.ts`) restarts right away when nothing is working, else waits with an app-wide notice (new `ui/Notice.tsx`, `app/RestartNotice.tsx`: "Glade restarts when N chats finish · Restart Now · Cancel"; Cancel sticks for that build). Composer drafts were in memory only: now in `features/chat/drafts.ts`, saved to localStorage just before the relaunch and restored once. `install.mjs` says it restarts on its own. Tests: watcher, version pushes, auto-restart decisions, drafts, UpdateNow states.
    - Not verified with a real bundle swap yet (throwaway bundle with `GLADE_INSTALL_TARGET` + `GLADE_APP_IDENTIFIER`). The first install of this change is done by an app without the watcher, so that one time it won't restart on its own.

- [x] **I-198** Settings per agent: models, defaults and updates on each agent's own page; one Glade-wide quick-tasks model — `settings` / `server` / `protocol` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — Claude Sonnet 5.5 was released today but isn't listed. Asked whether Glade can tell when an agent is out of date and offer a button that runs its update (e.g. `claude update`). Also: models are really per harness (each has its own list), yet there's a single place to set default models; that should be per harness too.
  - Why it came up: Sonnet 5.5 was in pi's list but Claude Code still offered "Sonnet 5", because Glade asks the installed `claude` for its models and it was 2.1.280 (latest 2.1.293; `"autoUpdates": false` in `~/.claude.json`). The Settings → Models pickers only list the default agent's (pi's) models, so there was nowhere to see or set Claude Code's.
  - Problems today (global `settings.models`, `packages/protocol/src/api.ts`):
    - `defaultModel` goes to every new chat whatever its agent (`services/app/sessions.ts`: `req.model ?? settings.models.defaultModel`), e.g. pi's `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5` to a Claude Code chat (ids `sonnet`/`opus`/…) or a Codex chat.
    - `smallModel`: titles are written by the chat's own harness with the global small model (`services/app/titles.ts`), so a Codex chat can be asked for an Anthropic model; search summaries use the default harness (`services/search/create.ts`).
    - `hiddenModels` are `provider/id` keys, and pi and Claude Code both use `anthropic/…`: hiding pi's `anthropic/claude-opus-4-8` also hides Claude Code's (`visibleModelsOf`, `state/store.ts`).
    - `subagentModel` / `sideQuestionModel` already fall back when the chat's harness doesn't list them (`agent-team.ts`, `side-questions.ts` `availableModel`), but only one value exists for all agents.
    - Thinking levels differ per agent too.
  - Decided (2026-10-05):
    - **Per agent** (pi, Claude Code, Codex; ACP agents that choose their own model have no model settings): default model + thinking level for new chats, sub-agent model + thinking, side-question model, shown/hidden models.
    - **Glade-wide, one quick-tasks model** (titles, `/name`, summaries, search): picked as *agent + model* (e.g. "pi · Claude Haiku 4.5", later a local model), used for every chat whatever its agent. Fallback when it's unset or unavailable: today's behaviour (Haiku if listed, else the chat's model). The chosen agent must support one-shot completion (`harness.complete`).
    - **UI: one page per agent under Settings → Agents** (status, enable switch, version + Update, defaults, models list with show/hide). The global Models page keeps only Glade-wide settings (quick-tasks model; default agent stays where it is) or is folded into Agents; no duplicated controls.
    - **Agent updates are part of this item**: each agent page shows the installed version, "X available" and an **Update** button that runs the agent's own updater and shows its output; afterwards Glade reloads that agent's models (new ones like Sonnet 5.5 appear without a restart). Update runs right away when no chat of that agent is working on this Mac, else when they finish (same rule as I-197; open but idle chats pick up the new version when their process next starts).
  - Version checks (verified 2026-10-05):
    - Claude Code: `claude --version`; latest from `https://downloads.claude.ai/claude-code-releases/latest` (also `/stable`; that base URL is in the `claude` binary). Update: `claude update`. Compare against the channel the user's `claude` uses (assume `latest` unless its settings say otherwise; verify).
    - pi: `pi --version` vs the npm registry (`@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent`). Update: `pi update self`.
    - Codex: `codex --version` vs `@openai/codex` on npm (or GitHub releases; the user's install is the standalone one under `~/.codex/packages/standalone`). Update: `codex update`.
    - Check on opening the page and about daily (like Glade's own update check, `services/update-check.ts`); a dot on Settings → Agents when one is behind. Each Mac checks and updates its own agents; other Macs through the device switcher (like Local Models).
  - Model keys include the agent (`<harness>/<provider>/<id>`) wherever settings store them, so the same provider/id under two agents never collide.
  - Migration: today's global values become pi's (the default agent's) per-agent settings; `smallModel` becomes the quick-tasks model as `pi · <model>`; hidden keys move under pi (and under Claude Code only if Claude Code lists that exact id; otherwise drop). Existing chats keep their model. Settings import/export and older clients keep working.
  - Not decided (not blocking): automatic agent updates when idle. Build the button only; ask the user after they've used it.
  - Acceptance: each agent's page lists only its models and its own defaults; a new Claude Code / Codex chat starts on that agent's default, never another agent's model; hiding a model in pi doesn't hide it in Claude Code; titles/summaries use the quick-tasks model for chats of every agent; with an outdated `claude`, the Claude Code page says so and Update brings in the new version and its models (test with a fake updater/version source in a sandbox; never run the real updaters from tests). Desktop and iPhone (shared app-core) both work.
  - Outcome (2026-10-05):
    - **Settings:** `Settings.models = { quickTasks, agents.<harnessId> }` (`AgentModelSettings`, read via `agentModelSettings()`; an older server's global fields are read as its default agent's). Stored settings migrate on read and write (`migrateAgentModels`): the old global fields → the default agent's entry (pi), `smallModel` → `quickTasks`. Hidden models only move to the default agent (Claude Code's list isn't known at load time), so models hidden for pi that Claude Code also lists show again there.
    - **Server:** new chats, sub-agents and side questions use their own agent's settings; `services/app/quick-tasks.ts` routes titles, `/name`, summaries, search and commit messages through the quick-tasks agent (`capabilities.quickTasks`: pi, Claude Code) with its model, else the old fallbacks; `GET /api/models/default?harness=`; PATCH validation of `models`.
    - **Agent versions:** `services/agent-versions/` (installed via `<cmd> --version` on the login-shell PATH; latest from npm for pi/Codex and `downloads.claude.ai/claude-code-releases/<channel>` for Claude Code), checks ~30 s after start, daily and when the page opens; Update runs the agent's updater in the login shell, waits for that agent's working chats, then re-reads the version and reloads models (Codex's shared app-server restarts once no chat uses it). Routes `/api/agent-versions[/check|/:harness/update[/cancel]]`, `agent_versions` push. Testing overrides `GLADE_AGENT_UPDATE_COMMAND_<ID>` / `GLADE_AGENT_VERSION_COMMAND_<ID>`.
    - **UI:** Settings → Agents = Glade-wide group (new chats' agent, sub-agents, Quick tasks model) + agent rows (dot when an update is available) → `/settings/agent/<id>`: status + Enable, Version (Check Now / Update / log / waiting), Defaults (thinking levels the agent's models support; "Default (<agent's own default>)"), Models (show/hide). `/settings/models` redirects to Agents. Another Mac: settings view only, updates work. New-chat composer uses the target agent's defaults. iPhone Device screen shows the quick-tasks model and per-agent defaults.
    - Checked in sandboxes with fake updaters (versions read for real: pi 1.0.0 → 1.1.0, Claude Code 2.1.280 → 2.1.293, Codex 0.159.1 → 0.161.0). Real updaters never run by tests. `pnpm check` passes in a clean worktree (one rare pre-existing flake: `agents.test.ts` closing an agent after the test's database closed).
    - Open: the iPhone's "Available Models" list still shows only the default agent's models; another Mac's update progress is polled (1.5 s) rather than pushed.

- [x] **I-200** Composer: one Send button whose icon shows what ↩ will do (send / steer / follow-up / ask aside), switched by held modifier keys — `chat` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — a message can be sent normally, as a follow-up, as steering, or as an aside. All of these should be keyboard-shortcut based, with the Send icon changing to show which one will be sent.
  - Notes: the modes and keys already exist (I-140, I-153, I-164): idle ↩ = Send; while running ↩ = Steer, ⌘↩ = Follow-up, ⌥↩ = Ask Aside (also `/btw`); harnesses without steering queue on ↩/⌘↩ ("Queue message"); shell input = "Run command". Today only ⌘ changes the icon (`ArrowUp` → `ClockArrowUp`, `use-meta-held.ts`); Ask Aside has its own violet `?` button next to Send (`Composer.tsx` ~L824); Steer and Send share the same icon (only the tooltip differs).
  - Proposed: track ⌥ like ⌘ (generalise `useMetaHeld` into a held-modifier hook); while running, Send's icon/colour/label/tooltip reflect the mode: Steer (distinct icon from idle Send), ⌘ held → Follow-up, ⌥ held → Ask Aside (violet, `MessageCircleQuestionMark`), and clicking with the modifier held does that mode. Optionally a distinct icon for Run command and Queue message. `data-send-behavior` and aria-label follow the mode. Touch (iPhone) keeps its hold-for-sheet behaviour.
  - Decided (user, 2026-10-05): **one Send button, no separate Ask Aside button.** The main Send button reacts to all of these shortcuts so there aren't several buttons that send; its (blue) icon updates to show what ↩ will do in the current context with the modifier keys currently held.
  - States the Send button must cover (lead, 2026-10-05):
    - Idle: Send. ⌘/⌥ change nothing (follow-up and Ask Aside only exist while the agent runs).
    - Running, harness that steers: ↩ Steer · ⌘ held → Follow-up · ⌥ held → Ask Aside (only with `sideQuestions` and text typed; attachments-only → Ask Aside shown disabled).
    - Running, harness without steering: ↩ and ⌘↩ both Queue message · ⌥ → Ask Aside.
    - Shell input (`!cmd` / `!!cmd`): Run command; modifiers ignored. Check what ↩ does for a slash command typed while running and show that.
    - Nothing to send: the icon still shows the mode, disabled. Stop stays its own button (it doesn't send; Esc also stops).
    - Modifier tracking: reset on window blur / visibility change (a missed keyup must not leave it stuck); a quick ⌘ shortcut (⌘K, ⌘C…) shouldn't flash the icon (e.g. only swap after ~120 ms held, or only while the composer has focus); clicking with ⌘/⌥ held does that mode.
    - Touch (iPhone): unchanged (no modifiers; hold Send for the sheet).
  - Decided (user, 2026-10-05): **right-click on Send opens a small menu with the modes** that apply now (Steer / Send as Follow-up / Ask Aside, or Queue message; idle: Send), shortcuts shown, impossible ones disabled (the user liked this proposal).
  - Acceptance: each mode has a shortcut and a distinct Send icon while it applies; holding/releasing ⌘/⌥ swaps the icon live; clicking Send with a modifier held does that mode; tests for the icon/behaviour per mode.
  - Outcome (2026-10-05): `sendModeFor` (`features/chat/send-mode.ts`) decides the mode from state + held keys: idle Send (ArrowUp); running Steer (ShipWheel) · ⌘ Follow-up (ClockArrowUp) · ⌥ Ask Aside (violet, only with side questions and text; attachments-only → disabled); no steering → Queue message (ListPlus); `!cmd` Run command (Terminal, green); `/btw …` Ask Aside; a Glade built-in like `/compact` "Run /compact"; a saved prompt typed in full "Insert prompt". The separate Ask Aside button is gone; clicking with ⌘/⌥ held sends that way; right-click lists the modes that apply now with their keys (`sendMenuModes`, existing ContextMenu). `use-held-modifiers.ts` (replaces `use-meta-held.ts`) tracks ⌘/Ctrl/⌥, resets on blur/hidden, shows a new key after 120 ms and ignores modifiers used for other shortcuts (⌘K…). Changed on purpose: ⌥↩ sends when Ask Aside doesn't apply (idle, agents without side questions); the Steer icon also shows on the iPhone (touch otherwise unchanged). `Tooltip` passes extra props to its trigger (so a context menu around it works). Tests for every mode, the menu, hold/release/blur; screenshots in a sandbox (light/dark).

- [x] **I-201** Custom command and flags per agent, behind an "Advanced" toggle on the agent's page — `settings` / `server` / `protocol` (after I-198) (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — let users change the command run for each harness (e.g. a work setup that must call pi through a small wrapper command instead of plain `pi`), which would also let them add flags they always want. Then: hide this behind an Advanced toggle on the same page, not visible or settable by default. When Advanced is off, Glade uses its own executables even if custom ones are set; the custom values are saved and apply again when Advanced is turned back on.
  - Note: this brings back what I-159 removed (the user had decided on 2026-09-27 not to allow changing the pi executable or passing extra arguments), now for every agent and behind Advanced.
  - Design:
    - **Where:** each agent's page (I-198, Settings → Agents → <agent>) gets an **Advanced** switch on that page, off by default. Off: the command fields are hidden. On: a "Command" field (e.g. `mywrapper pi --offline`) with the default as placeholder (`pi`, `claude`, `codex`), and a **Test** button that runs `<command> --version` and shows the result.
    - **Off means ours, always:** Glade runs the built-in command whenever Advanced is off, even if a custom command is saved. The custom value stays stored, so turning Advanced back on uses it again. Settings shape (proposal): `Settings.agents.<id>.advanced: boolean` + `command: string | null` (next to `enabled`); effective command = `advanced && command ? command : default`, resolved in one server helper used everywhere an agent is started or detected.
    - **Per device** (settings are per environment): a work Mac can use `mywrapper pi`, a home Mac plain `pi`. Another device's page shows it view only.
    - **How it runs:** split the command shell-style (quotes respected, no shell expansion) into program + leading args; Glade appends its own args. pi: `mywrapper pi <user flags> --mode rpc …` (`harness/pi/rpc-process.ts`, `side-question.ts`, the model-listing process: all via `PiHarness.command`). Codex: `<program> <args> app-server …` (`harness/codex/rpc.ts`). Claude Code: the SDK's `spawnClaudeCodeProcess` option (or a generated shim script), since `pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` takes a single path. Detection ("installed") looks for the custom program on the login-shell PATH. A change applies to agent processes started afterwards (new chats, restarts); the model list reloads.
    - **Glade's own flags are reserved:** refuse (clear message under the field) user flags that clash with what Glade passes: pi `--mode`, `--session`/`--no-session`/`--session-dir`, `--model`/`--provider`/`--thinking`, `-p`, `-e` of Glade's extension; Codex's `app-server` arguments; the flags the Claude SDK sets. Harmless ones (`--offline`, extra `--skill` paths, …) pass through.
    - **Wrapper requirements** (help text under the field): it must pass arguments through and leave stdin/stdout untouched (the RPC is JSON lines over stdout; stderr logging is fine), e.g. end with `exec pi "$@"`.
    - **Versions/updates (I-198):** with a custom command in effect, the installed version comes from `<command> --version`; npm/channel "latest" may not apply (a wrapper may pin the pi version).
  - Decided (user, 2026-10-05): with a custom command in effect, **Update runs the agent's updater through that command** (e.g. `mywrapper pi update self` instead of `pi update self`; likewise `<custom> update` for Claude Code and Codex). Version checks also go through it (`<command> --version`). Started.
  - Acceptance: Advanced is off by default with the fields hidden; Advanced on + `mywrapper pi` saved → new pi chats start via `mywrapper pi … --mode rpc` (test with a fake wrapper script that execs a fake pi); Advanced off → `pi` again while `mywrapper pi` stays saved and comes back when switched on; clashing flags are refused; same for Claude Code and Codex; desktop and iPhone (view only) show it correctly.
  - Outcome (2026-10-05): `Settings.agents.<id>` = `{ enabled?, advanced?, command? }` (`AgentSwitch`); one server helper (`harness/agent-command.ts`) resolves the effective command (Advanced off → built-in, saved value kept). Parsing is shell-like without a shell (quotes/backslashes; `| & ; < > ( ) $` and backticks and `VAR=x` prefixes refused); Glade's reserved flags refused (pi `--mode`, `--session*`, `--model`, `--provider`, `--thinking`, `-p`, `--tools`, `--system-prompt`, …; the Claude SDK's flags; Codex `app-server`); a user's own `-e` is allowed. pi (RPC, model listing, one-shot, side questions), Codex (`<cmd> app-server`) and Claude Code (a generated exec script for commands with arguments) start through it; the catalog, installed-version check and Update (`<cmd> update self` / `update`) too; a change reloads the harness (Codex restarts its app-server), models and versions. `POST /api/agent-command/test` runs `<cmd> --version` (this Mac only). UI: Advanced group at the bottom of the agent page (Command, Test, help, refusal reason; view only on another device). Tests with a real wrapper script exec-ing fake agents; sandbox check. Open: "latest" still comes from npm / Claude's channel even when a wrapper pins a version.
  - Decided (user, 2026-10-08): a user's own `-e` flags stay allowed in a custom command; with a wrapper that pins a version, "latest" is still compared against the public release (keep the update notice).

- [x] **I-202** Sidebar: free drag-to-reorder of chats and folders inside a project (and in the standalone Chats section), into, out of and within folders — `shell` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — drag to reorder in the sidebar. Nothing inside a project can be moved out of it, but any chat inside a project can be freely reordered, together with the project's folders (e.g. chat, folder, chat): drag both chats and folders within the project, drag chats into a folder, and reorder within the folder. The same applies to the standalone chats and folders at the bottom: chats can be dragged into and out of folders and reordered in any way.
  - Today (I-019, I-165): unpinned chats are sorted by creation date (newest first) and aren't draggable; only pinned chats reorder among themselves (`Workspace.pinOrder`, `PUT /api/chats/pin-order`, drag + Move Up/Down in `ChatRow.tsx`). A project's folders are always listed above its chats in their own order (`Folder.sortOrder`, `PUT /api/folders/order`). Chats can already be dragged into/out of folders (`ChatList.tsx`, `useSortable`'s `into`). Top-level folders (`projectId: null`) sit in the Projects list, share its order with projects, and also hold standalone chats. Standalone chats of several environments are interleaved by a client-side order (`clientOrders`, `workspacesForProject` in `app-core/state/store.ts`).
  - Wanted (supersedes I-019 decision 1 for chats):
    - **Inside a project**: one manual order of mixed items, chats and the project's folders interleaved (e.g. chat, folder, chat). Drag any chat or folder to any position. Drag a chat into a folder, out of it (back into the project list at the drop position), and reorder chats inside a folder. Nothing dragged from a project can leave that project (no drop targets outside it; no Move to another project).
    - **Standalone Chats section**: same rules for standalone chats and their folders.
    - **No keyboard alternative; remove Move Up / Move Down** (user, 2026-10-05: Move Up / Move Down aren't needed; drag and drop is enough, and keyboard use doesn't matter for this). Remove them from the sidebar menus: chats (`ChatRow.tsx`), folders (`FolderGroup.tsx`) and projects (`ProjectGroup.tsx`), and the iPhone long-press equivalents if any (assumed to mean every Move Up/Down action; projects stay draggable).
    - **Obvious drop feedback** (user: make it visually obvious where things will be dropped, maybe a ghost copy; the design is left to the lead). Design (lead): the dragged row follows the pointer as a translucent ghost copy (slight shadow, ~80% opacity) while its original slot is dimmed; between items, an accent insertion line with a small dot at its start, indented to the depth the item will land at (project top level vs inside a folder); dropping *into* a folder highlights the whole folder row (accent tint + rounded outline) and auto-expands a closed folder after a short hover (~600ms); drop targets that aren't allowed (outside the item's project) show nothing and the ghost turns "not allowed" (cursor + reduced opacity); rows below the insertion point slide open a gap with a short animation (respect reduced motion). Applies to project dragging too, so all sidebar drags feel the same.
    - Nothing moves on activity (as today).
  - Sketch: one order number space per container (a project's top level, each folder, the standalone top level): e.g. `Workspace.sortOrder` + `Folder.sortOrder` in the same space for a project's top level, and `Workspace.sortOrder` within a folder; one `PUT …/order { container, ids }` that rewrites positions (chat and folder ids mixed). Migration: today's visual order (pinned by pinOrder, then folders, then chats newest first) becomes the initial manual order. Extend `useSortable` / `reorder.ts` (`resolveTreeDrop` already handles projects+folders) to a mixed chat/folder tree per container. Desktop sidebar first; the iPhone chat list (shared app-core order) shows the same order (drag there too if feasible; no Move Up/Down). Multi-environment standalone lists: keep the per-device interleave approach for items of different environments.
  - Note: first queued as a second "I-201" (id clash with the agent-command item queued in parallel); renumbered 2026-10-05.
  - Decisions (user, 2026-10-05):
    1. **New chats and new folders go to the top** of their container (project top level, folder, or Chats section).
    2. **Pinned chats stay at the top** of their list (as today) and can be reordered among themselves; the free mixed order applies below them.
    3. **Folders not in a project show up in the Chats section** (at the bottom), not the Projects list, since folders without a project aren't project-related. They hold standalone chats and are freely ordered among the standalone chats (same rules as inside a project). Today top-level folders live in the Projects list and can also hold projects (`Project.folderId`); the user's data has no top-level folders yet (checked 2026-10-05), so no real data needs migrating.
    4. **Folders never hold projects; projects are always top level** (user, 2026-10-05). Drop `Project.folderId` (and "Move to Folder" / "New Folder" in the project menu, dragging a project onto a folder); the Projects list holds only projects, drag-reordered. Folders exist only inside a project (its chats) or in the Chats section (standalone chats). Migration: clear any `Project.folderId`; top-level folders become Chats-section folders.
  - Acceptance: in a project, chats and folders can be dragged into any mixed order with a clear ghost + insertion line / folder highlight showing where they'll land, chats into/out of/within folders, and the order survives reload and syncs to other clients (desktop + iPhone); a drag never moves an item into another project or out to the standalone section; same behaviour in the Chats section; activity never re-sorts; no Move Up/Down left in sidebar menus; tests for the reorder math and the server order route.
  - Outcome (2026-10-05):
    - **Model:** `Project.folderId` removed (projects are always top level; `PATCH /projects/:id {folderId}` → 400). `Workspace.sortOrder` places a chat in its container: a project's top level or the Chats section (one number space with that list's `Folder.sortOrder`), or a folder. Pinned chats stay on top by `pinOrder`. Shared sort rule in `packages/protocol/src/chat-order.ts` (no `sortOrder` yet → first, newest first). New `PUT /api/workspaces/order { projectId, folderId, ids }` (every unpinned item of the container; may pull chats in from elsewhere in the same list; never across projects/sections) → `{ workspaces, folders }`; `PUT /api/folders/order` removed. Synced with the existing `workspace_upsert`/`folder_upsert`.
    - **Where things land:** new chats/folders at the top; unpinning → top below the pins; Move to Folder → the folder's top; Remove from Folder → right after the folder; deleting a folder puts its chats in its place.
    - **Migration** (`store/migrate-sidebar-order.ts`, once per data folder, `meta.sidebar_order`, event rows for other servers): today's visual order becomes the manual order; `Project.folderId` dropped; project-less folders become Chats-section folders. Checked on a copy of the user's data (5 projects, 6 chats, no folders): newest-first order kept.
    - **Sidebar:** `useChatTree` + pure `resolveTreeDrop`; translucent ghost (`ui/drag-ghost.ts`) trailing the pointer, accent insertion line with a dot at the landing depth, rows below slide 6 px (off with reduced motion), folder highlight + auto-open after 600 ms, not-allowed outside the item's project/section. Move Up/Down removed everywhere; "Move to Folder" removed from the project menu; New Folder moved from the Projects header to the Chats header. The project menu keeps "New Folder" (a folder inside the project). iPhone: same mixed order and Chats-section folders, no dragging.
    - Tests: drop math, route + migration, sidebar components (ghost, depth, gap, highlight, auto-open, not-allowed, Esc). `pnpm check` passes in a clean worktree (2183 tests). Checked in a sandbox (light/dark).
    - Open: a new client reordering on a Mac that hasn't updated gets a 404 toast there (and an old client gets 404 from the removed folder-order route) until both are updated.

- [x] **I-203** Bookmarks: bookmark a message, jump back to it, copy it or reference it in the composer; searchable across chats in ⌘K — `chat` / `server` / `protocol` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — the user often asks a chat for some data, then asks many follow-up questions and keeps scrolling back up to that data. They'd like to bookmark a message, then click the bookmark to scroll it back into view, copy it, or reference it in the chat. Searchable across all chats would be nice, but not if it makes things much more complicated.
  - Design (lead, proposed 2026-10-05; user didn't object):
    - **Add**: a bookmark icon in each message's hover actions (user and agent messages) + "Bookmark" in its context menu. **⌘D** toggles a bookmark on the latest agent reply. Optional: select text → context menu "Bookmark Selection" (stores the passage).
    - **Label**: auto from the message's first heading / first line; renamable.
    - **In the transcript**: a small accent ribbon on bookmarked messages; tick marks on the scroll edge (click to jump).
    - **Chat header**: a bookmark button with a count opens a popover list (label, time, first line). Row: click → scroll into view + flash (reuse `jump-to-message.ts` / I-093); Copy (Markdown); **Reference** (inserts a chip in the composer that is sent as a short quote of the message, so the agent knows exactly which one); Remove. **⌘⇧D** opens it (↑/↓, ↩ jumps).
    - **Across chats (⌘K)**: a "Bookmarks" group in the palette listing bookmarks of all chats (label, chat title, project); picking one opens the chat and jumps. Cheap given the server already stores them: one `GET /api/bookmarks` (all, per environment) and the palette's existing open-and-jump path. If it turns out costly, ship per-chat first and leave this for later (the user's call: skip it if it adds much complexity).
  - Storage: per environment on the server (synced like pins/folders, so iPhone + other devices see them), keyed by session id + `MessageAnchor` (role + timestamp; harness message ids are positional and shift after compaction). Removed with the chat. iPhone: long-press a message → Bookmark; list from the chat header.
  - Status (2026-10-05): I-202 finished; started.
  - Acceptance: bookmark from hover/context menu/⌘D; the header list jumps to, copies and references bookmarks; ribbons and scroll ticks show; bookmarks survive reload, compaction and show on other clients; ⌘K lists bookmarks from all chats and opens the right one; tests for anchor resolution, the store/route and the composer reference.
  - Outcome (2026-10-05):
    - **Model/API:** `packages/protocol/src/bookmarks.ts` (`Bookmark`: session, workspace, `MessageAnchor`, label auto/user, excerpt, optional selection). `GET/POST /api/bookmarks`, `PATCH/DELETE /api/bookmarks/:id`, `GET /api/bookmarks/:id/content`; pushes `bookmark_upsert` / `bookmark_removed`, in shell snapshots and live checks. Re-bookmarking a message returns the existing one. An agent reply's bookmark anchors its first message and covers the whole reply.
    - **Storage:** migration `008-bookmarks` (store schema 8), writes through the event log (other servers on the data folder, sync hub, iPhone/remote clients); removed with their chat; orphans dropped at open. Checked on a copy of the user's data: opens and migrates cleanly.
    - **UI:** bookmark button in message hover actions, one transcript context menu (Bookmark / Remove Bookmark, Copy; with a selection Copy + Bookmark Selection), accent ribbon + scroll-edge ticks; header button with count → list (new `ui/ListPopover.tsx`): jump with flash (opens a sub-agent's parent tab + side pane when needed), Reference, Copy, Rename, Remove. ⌘D toggles the latest agent reply, ⌘⇧D opens the list (no clashes, tested). Reference = a composer chip sent as a short quote (≤ 8 lines / 600 chars) before the text. ⌘K "Bookmarks" group (5 newest when empty) opens the chat and jumps. iPhone: long-press a message → Bookmark; nav-bar button with count once there is one → sheet.
    - Tests for anchors, store/migration/sync/routes, client state, references, palette, shortcuts, transcript and popover, iPhone sheet; sandbox check (fake agent) of the whole flow. `pnpm check` passes in a clean worktree (2232 tests).
    - Open: iPhone layout not checked visually (needs pairing); a reference chip doesn't survive an app restart (drafts do); Bookmark Selection jumps to the whole message; list rows with action buttons may read poorly to screen readers.

- [x] **I-204** Check for Glade updates every 5 minutes instead of every 4 hours — `server` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — check for updates every five minutes instead of every four hours.
  - Outcome (2026-10-05): `CHECK_INTERVAL_MS` (`services/update-check.ts`) is 5 min; the client's fallback re-read of `/api/version` (`state/version.ts`) too (the server also pushes `version` after each check). Agent version checks (I-198) stay daily (they hit npm / Claude's release channel).

- [x] **I-205** PLAN.md records the user's intent, not their words; backfill the whole file — `docs` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — the user wants agents to stop quoting them verbatim in PLAN.md (dictated requests ramble, and the repo is going public). Items should state the intent in the agent's own words, complete enough to act on. Every existing quote (≈211 `Reported:` lines, ≈55 quoted decisions, plus quotes inside notes and outcomes) is to be rewritten as intent, and the rules that told agents to quote are to change.
  - Done already (lead, 2026-10-05): `.agents/skills/issue-queue/SKILL.md` and `AGENTS.md` now say to record intent, never quotes, and no personal paths/private-project details.
  - Backfill: rewrite every user quote in PLAN.md as intent/decisions (keep ids, ticks, dates, structure, technical notes and outcomes; keep every requirement and nuance; attributions like "(user, 2026-10-05)" stay). Also sweep `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md` for user quotes. Personal paths and private-project names are F-017's job (left as they are here unless trivially inside a quote being rewritten).
  - Acceptance: no verbatim user quotes left in PLAN.md (spot-check with a grep for quoted first-person text: `"I `, `"we `, `"let's`, `"can we`, `"it would`…); nothing lost: each item still says what was wanted and decided.

ARCHITECTURE.md, CHANGELOG.md, README.md, AGENTS.md had no user quotes. - [x] **I-206** Bookmarks: the ribbon sits by the reply's first part (e.g. "Thought") instead of its text, and clicking it should remove the bookmark — `chat` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — after bookmarking an agent reply, the user saw the gutter ribbon next to the reply's "Thought" row, above the actual answer text, which looks like the thought was bookmarked. They also expected a click on the gutter ribbon to remove the bookmark (today only the button next to the time toggles it): if the ribbon is shown, it should work as the toggle too.
  - Cause: `TurnView` (`packages/app-core/src/features/chat/Transcript.tsx` ~L308) places `BookmarkRibbon` at `top-[9px]` of the whole turn, i.e. beside its first part (thinking / tool group), because a reply is anchored by its first message (I-203). `BookmarkRibbon` (`MessageBookmark.tsx` ~L98) is a non-interactive `role="img"` span.
  - Fix: keep the anchor (the turn), but draw the ribbon beside where the reply's answer starts: the first text part of the turn (fall back to the top when the reply has no text, e.g. only tools); jumping to the bookmark should land there too. Make the ribbon a button: click removes the bookmark (several bookmarks on one message, e.g. a passage + the message: remove the message's own, or ask/list), tooltip "Remove Bookmark", hover state, accessible label; same on user messages (`UserBubble.tsx`) and the iPhone (tap). The scroll-edge tick should point at the same spot.
  - Addition (user, 2026-10-05): opening a bookmark scrolls the message to the middle of the screen; the user wants the top of the message near the top of the view, with a little breathing room above it (not flush against the edge).
    - Cause: jumps use `centeredScrollTop` (`features/chat/jump-to-message.ts`, via `useStickToBottom.ts`), which centers the target; only elements taller than the viewport are top-aligned (24 px margin).
    - Fix: bookmark jumps (header list, ⌘K Bookmarks, scroll ticks, iPhone sheet) align the start of the bookmarked message (for a reply: where its answer text starts, see above) to the top, below anything overlaying the transcript (header, banners), plus a margin of ~1.5–2 lines (≈24–32 px). Clamp at the end of the transcript (a short last message can't reach the top). Decided (user, 2026-10-05): **search-hit jumps (I-093) align to the top the same way** (one shared jump position for bookmarks and search).
  - Acceptance: bookmarking a reply that starts with thinking/tool calls shows the ribbon next to its first line of answer text; clicking the ribbon removes it (and the scroll tick); opening a bookmark puts the message's start near the top with a small margin (not centered); tests for the placement, the toggle and the scroll position (pure function).
  - Outcome (2026-10-05): the ribbon sits in a zero-height slot before the reply's first text part (top when the reply has no text), so it follows the text when a thought/tool group above expands; `answerPartIndex` (`jump-to-message.ts`) gives jumps and scroll ticks the same spot. The ribbon is a button ("Remove Bookmark"); with several bookmarks on a message a small menu lists them + Remove All; iPhone: tap. All jumps (search hits, header list, ⌘K, ticks, iPhone sheet) use the pure `jumpScrollTop` (start of the target 28 px below the top, clamped at both ends; optional `topInset` for future overlays). Tests for placement, removal, menu and scroll position; checked in a sandbox (ribbon beside the text, not the tool row; jumps land 28 px from the top). Not checked visually: iPhone, a reply with a real thinking block, the several-bookmarks menu.

- [x] **I-207** Agent page Models list: a filter box and grouping by model family — `settings` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — on an agent's page (Settings → Agents → <agent>, I-198) the Models list is long (pi lists 17 Anthropic models: several Opus, Sonnet, Haiku and Fable versions plus dated and "(latest)" aliases). The user wants a text box to filter the list, and the list grouped by model family (Claude Fable, Claude Haiku, Claude Opus, Claude Sonnet, …). Their way of working is to show only the newest model of each family, and grouping makes it easy to check that this is set up right.
  - Facts (lead, 2026-10-05): no agent reports a model family. pi's model list has id, name, provider, context, max output, thinking and inputs; Claude Code gives a value, display name and description; Codex an id, display name and description. So families are derived from the name.
  - Design:
    - **Family from the name** (pure function + tests): drop version numbers (`4.5`, `5`, `5.1`, `4-5`), date suffixes (`-20251001`), and qualifiers like `(latest)`; what's left is the family ("Claude Opus 4.5 (latest)" → "Claude Opus"; Codex "GPT-6.1-Sol" → "GPT Sol"; a local model such as "Qwen3.8-27B-Q4_K_M" → its leading name). A model with nothing left to strip is its own family. Groups stay under their provider/agent group as today.
    - **Order:** families A–Z; inside a family newest first (by parsed version, aliases next to the version they point to); the family header shows "N of M shown".
    - **Filter box** above the list (matches name and id, case-insensitive; Esc clears); families with no match are hidden; the switches still work while filtered.
    - Shared app-core code, so the iPhone's (view-only) model lists can group the same way later.
  - Decided (user, 2026-10-05): **no** one-click "Show only the newest"; just the filter and the family groups.
  - Acceptance: typing filters the list live; models are grouped under family headers with the newest first; the family name is right for pi's Claude models, Codex's GPT models and a few local-model names (tests); show/hide works unchanged.
  - Outcome (2026-10-05): pure `packages/app-core/src/lib/model-families.ts` derives the family from the name (drops versions, dates, sizes, quantization, "(latest)"/preview/instruct…, an `org/` prefix; the id fills in a missing version/family) and orders a family newest first (a versionless alias first, "(latest)" next to its version). pi's Claude models → Claude Fable / Haiku / Opus / Sonnet; Codex → GPT, GPT Astra, GPT Luna, GPT Sol, GPT Terra; local names like "Qwen3.8-27B-Q4_K_M" → Qwen. New `ui/SearchField.tsx` (magnifier, clear, Esc). The agent page's Models group: filter row (name or id, every word must match), provider headers, family headers with "N of M shown", hidden empty families/providers, a no-match state; a one-model family named like its model shows as a plain row. Another device's page: the filter works, switches/defaults/Refresh are disabled. Tests: families for every real name + local names, ordering, filter, component behaviour, the primitive. `pnpm check` passes (2267 tests); checked in a sandbox.

- [x] **I-209** Public website (GitHub Pages) with screenshots, videos and motion; a short README pointing to it; MIT license (promoted from F-030) — `docs` / `infra` (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — the user wants the website built now to show off the app: pictures, videos and motion graphics. The README becomes sparse (a few pictures, mainly pointing to the site).
  - Decisions (user, 2026-10-05):
    - **License: MIT** (`LICENSE`, `"license": "MIT"` in package.json; done by the lead).
    - **Install: build from source** for now (no signed download; the site's main button leads to the GitHub repo / build steps).
    - **Pitch:** a native Mac app for your coding agents: pi, Claude Code and Codex side by side, with sub-agents, local models, and your phone as a remote.
    - **Feature order:** multiple agents in one app; sub-agents working in parallel; iPhone app + remote access; local models; worktrees + changes panel; search, bookmarks and ⌘K; terminal; voice mode.
    - **Look: matches the app's dark mode** (its colours, type and native feel; the leaf icon), with the motion the user described in F-030: scroll animations, floaty movement, overlapping layers with depth.
    - **Media: scripted demo chats**, never the user's own chats or data. The demo must read like real agent work: realistic replies, thinking, tool calls with real diffs, sub-agents, not the fake harness's echoes.
    - **URL** `jas7457.github.io/glade`; code in `site/` in this repo, deployed by a GitHub Action.
    - **README:** short intro, one hero image plus two or three more, build-from-source steps, link to the site; developer details move to `CONTRIBUTING.md`.
  - Contract between the workstreams (lead): the site reads its media from `site/public/media/` by these names; captures are dark mode at 2× (desktop window ~1440×900 CSS px; iPhone in the simulator). Videos: short (5–15 s), muted, looping, WebM + MP4, a few MB each; a still poster PNG for each.
    - `hero` (video + poster): a project with several chats; a reply streaming in with thinking and grouped tool calls; sub-agent cards above the composer.
    - `agents` (image): the new-chat agent picker / Settings → Agents with pi, Claude Code and Codex; a Claude Code or Codex chat.
    - `subagents` (video): a chat spawning 2–3 sub-agents that work in parallel, their cards updating, reports landing.
    - `iphone-chat`, `iphone-list` (images, iPhone simulator): a chat and the chat list; `iphone-voice` (image): voice mode.
    - `remote` (image): Share This Device / connected devices on the Mac.
    - `local-models` (image): Settings → Local Models with a loaded model.
    - `worktrees` (image): a worktree chat with the changes panel and a diff.
    - `search` (video): ⌘K finding a chat and a bookmark, jumping to the message.
    - `bookmarks` (image): bookmark ribbon + the header list.
    - `terminal` (image): a terminal tab next to a chat.
    - `composer` (video, optional): the Send button changing with ⌘/⌥.
  - Addition (user, 2026-10-05, after looking at the site with the first captures): every feature asset shows the whole window, so it's small, hard to read, and unclear what to look at. Feature assets should focus on the part that matters: cropped or zoomed in.
    - Plan (lead): the hero keeps the full window. Each feature gets a **focus asset**: just the relevant region (e.g. the sub-agent cards + composer, the ⌘K palette, the agent picker, the changes panel, the Local Models list, the bookmark list), captured at 3× so it's sharp when shown large; videos recorded/cropped to that region. Files `site/public/media/<name>-focus.(png|webm|mp4)` plus `site/public/media/focus.json` with each focus rectangle in the full capture's coordinates. The site shows the focus asset large in front, with the full window behind it, dimmed and pushed back in depth (fits the layered look), and may animate from the full window into the focus region on scroll so the visitor sees where it sits.
  - Addition (user, 2026-10-05, second review of the site):
    - **Text and media must be on screen together.** The zoom into the focus region finishes only after the section's text has scrolled away, so it's unclear what's being shown. Each feature's text and its media must be visible at the same time while the animation plays.
    - **Media must fit the viewport.** Some focus images/videos are taller than the screen, so you scroll to see them. Every asset fits within the visible area (below the header, next to/with its text).
    - **Animations finish while fully in view.** Some only complete once the element is partly scrolled out. Animations run (and end) while the stage is fully visible, e.g. pinned sections or time-based playback triggered when in view, not scrubbing that ends off screen.
    - **The hero is the main selling point and should be the best animation, with less going on.** Today it's too busy (floating pieces, a full busy window) with no clear focus. Direction from the user: start from an empty new chat and guide the eye step by step with highlights: starting a new chat, what appears in the sidebar, asking a question, what happens as the agent answers.
    - Plan (lead): a new hero recording **`hero-story`** (video + poster + `hero-story.json`): a scripted flow in the demo sandbox from an empty New Chat: (1) the empty composer with the agent/model pickers, (2) typing a question, (3) sending: the chat appears at the top of the sidebar (quick title → generated title), (4) the reply streams: thinking, then grouped tool calls, (5) the finished answer (summary + changes). `hero-story.json` = `{ width, height, duration, steps: [{ id, start, end, label, rect: {x,y,w,h} }] }` in the capture's CSS px and seconds, recorded from the real element positions during capture. The site plays it large and calm, dims everything outside the current step's rect (spotlight) with a short caption beside it, and shows the step list so the visitor can follow (or jump). No floating extra pieces. Also: fix the `search-focus` crop so the ⌘K palette stays in frame, and the dark strip on `terminal-focus`.
  - Workstreams: (A) demo mode + capture scripts (realistic scripted chats, a demo sandbox, scripted screenshots/videos incl. the iPhone simulator); (B) the site itself (static, built from `site/`, GSAP motion, placeholders until A's media lands, a Pages workflow); (C, lead) LICENSE, README + CONTRIBUTING once media exists, enabling Pages.
  - Outcome (2026-10-05): live at https://jas7457.github.io/glade/ (GitHub Pages, source "GitHub Actions"; `.github/workflows/pages.yml` builds `site/` on pushes to main that touch it). Site: hero = the `hero-story` recording (empty new chat → ask → sidebar → work → done) shown large with a moving spotlight, captions and a step bar; feature panels one screen each, text alternating left/right, the zoomed-in focus asset in front of the dimmed window, motion played (not scrubbed, no pinning) once a panel is mostly on screen; phones stacked; reduced motion static. Demo mode (`pnpm dev:agent --demo`: a made-up "Lantern" project with scripted, realistic chats for pi, Claude Code and Codex) and `pnpm capture` (headless Chrome + iOS simulator) produce every asset into `site/public/media/`. README is short (hero + two focus shots, build steps, link to the site); developer notes moved to CONTRIBUTING.md, the remote-access guide to docs/remote-access.md. MIT license.
    - Open (optional): the New Chat subtitle in the hero shows the sandbox folder path; the composer-keys section of the old README was dropped (the site covers it).

  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — the user saw the website's screenshots and likes how the sidebar looks there better than in the real app, and wants the app's sidebar closer to it.

The capture tool (`tools/capture/lib/browser.mjs`, `MAC_WINDOW`) has no vibrancy, so it paints its own backdrop: two soft radial gradients (slate blue `rgba(78,92,110,.55)` from the top left, muted violet `rgba(64,58,84,.5)` from the bottom left) on `#232327`. The sidebar's translucency over that gradient is what the user likes.

Light mode needs its own matching variant either way. Browser (`pnpm dev`) could use it too, so all three look the same.
  - Decided (user, 2026-10-05): **(b)**: keep the partly see-through sidebar (native vibrancy) and tint it toward the bluish gradient of the screenshot. Lead default (not asked): the browser version (`pnpm dev`, no vibrancy) gets a matching gradient backdrop so it looks the same.

The app's sidebar = 25% veil of `--pi-sidebar` + the tint over the native vibrancy (still see-through; material unchanged); the browser = veil + tint + backdrop colour. Checked by the user in a test build next to the installed app; browser checked dark/light. The capture script stops painting its own gradient so captures aren't tinted twice.
