- [x] **I-213** Group projects: a project with no folder of its own whose chats each pick their own folder; the folder browser reopens where it was last left — `server` / `shell` / `protocol` / `iphone` (2026-10-08)
  - Reported: 2026-10-08 — in a monorepo the user organises work per checkout (e.g. a git worktree named after itself) and, inside it, by segment of the repo (admin web, Polaris, Polaris internal, …). Today's projects map one-to-one to a folder, so that layout can't be expressed. The user wants the same sidebar layout as projects (project heading, chats under it, tabs inside a chat) but for arbitrary groupings: the heading is just a name the user picks, and each chat under it points to its own folder on disk. Those folders will usually be inside the same checkout, but nothing may enforce that: any folder, in any checkout, is allowed. Inside such a chat, new tabs run in that chat's folder like any project chat.
  - Discussed and rejected (2026-10-08): a group rail / space switcher on the far left and a group → project → chat nesting; the user wants the existing project layout, not a new level.
  - Decided (user, 2026-10-08):
    - A chat's folder is chosen when the chat is created and can never be changed afterwards.
    - No per-group list of remembered folders is needed. Instead the folder browser (everywhere it's used) reopens in the folder where it was last left; if that folder no longer exists (deleted, moved, unmounted volume) it falls back gracefully (nearest existing parent, else home).
    - iPhone: the lead decides what makes sense.
  - Design (lead, 2026-10-08):
    - **Model:** `Project.path` becomes `string | null`; `null` = a group project (stored in `data_json`, so no DB migration). `CreateProjectRequest.path` becomes optional: absent = a group, `name` then required. A chat's folder is its existing `Workspace.cwd` (worktree chats already run outside the project folder, and tabs/sub-agents already inherit `cwd`), so no new chat field.
    - **Creating a chat in a group:** `CreateWorkspaceRequest.folder` (absolute path) is required for a group project and rejected (400) for any other kind; it must be an existing directory inside the folder browser's roots (home, `/Volumes`). The chat's default title is the folder's name (a user title, so the first prompt doesn't rename it; renaming still works). No API changes the folder later. Worktree creation is a 400 for group chats (v1).
    - **Folder-level routes** (`/commands`, `/files`, `/permission-modes`) also accept `workspaceId` (that chat's `cwd`) and `folder` (an absolute folder, for the new-chat screen of a group before the chat exists); precedence `workspaceId` > `folder` > `projectId`. Project endpoints that need a folder (`/projects/:id/open`, `/projects/:id/git`, checkout/branch, project changes) answer 400 for groups.
    - **Desktop:** "New Group…" next to Add Project (name only); group rows in the sidebar look like projects with a distinct icon and no path/Copy Path/Open In items. New chat in a group (row "+", ⌘N, palette) shows a required **Folder** chip in the context bar that opens the folder browser (Work in / Branch pickers hidden for groups); Send stays disabled until a folder is chosen. Chat rows keep showing their title; the tooltip / header names the folder.
    - **Folder browser memory:** per device and per environment (localStorage), the last folder the browser was in; on open, walk up to the nearest existing parent when it's gone, else home. Applies to Add Project and the group folder chip, desktop and iPhone.
    - **iPhone:** groups appear in the project picker; picking one adds a Folder row to the new-chat screen offering the folders of the group's existing chats (derived, not stored) plus Browse… (the shared `FolderBrowser` in a sheet); Send needs a folder.
  - Outcome (2026-10-08):
    - **Server:** `POST /projects` without `path` creates a group (name required). `POST /workspaces {folder}` is required in a group, refused elsewhere and with `worktree`, and checked against the folder browser's area (`FsBrowseService.requireFolder`, symlinks resolved). The folder becomes `cwd` and the title is the folder's name. `PATCH` refuses `folder`/`cwd`. Folder routes take `workspaceId` > `folder` > `projectId`, and a group's folder endpoints answer 400. Tests: `test/group-projects.test.ts`.
    - **Desktop / app-core:**
      - The Projects `+` is a menu with Add Project… and New Group… (`NewGroupDialog`), also in the palette and the project picker. Groups use a `Layers` icon (`ui/ProjectIcon`) and have no path, Copy Path or Open In.
      - New chat in a group has a required Folder chip (`context-bar/FolderPicker`, with the group's chat folders as Recent). Work in and Branch are hidden, and Send is blocked with a reason until a folder is chosen.
      - `@` search, slash commands and permission modes use the chat's `workspaceId`, or the picked `folder` on the new-chat screen.
      - A group chat's header and sidebar tooltip name its folder.
      - `FolderBrowser` has `memoryKey` (it reopens where it was left, falls back to the nearest existing parent, else home) and `openOnTap`.
    - **iPhone:** groups appear in the project sheet. A Folder chip opens a sheet with the group's chat folders and Browse… (`FolderBrowser` with memory). Send and voice are blocked until a folder is chosen. Chat list headers use `ProjectIcon`.
    - Screenshots: `/tmp/glade-groups-desktop-*.png`, `/tmp/glade-groups-iphone-*.png`.
    - Left open:
      - A group chat inside a larger repo shows the whole repo's changes count and changes panel (git runs at the repo root); scoping it to the chat's folder could be a follow-up.
      - The desktop folder dialog has no native "Choose in Finder…".
      - Group chats can't use worktrees.
      - Folders under `/tmp` aren't allowed (outside the browser's area).

Add a `compacting` input: while compacting, ↩, ⌘↩ and clicks all give Follow-up (button shows the follow-up icon/colour, tooltip says it's sent after compacting); right-click menu lists only Follow-up. Also covers `/compact` run while idle (if `running` is false during compaction, compacting still counts) and harnesses without steering (Queue message → Follow-up wording is the same queue). Server side: check a follow-up sent during compaction is delivered after it for pi, Claude Code and Codex (a steer sent then may be lost or misapplied). Table in the header comment + `send-mode` tests.

Found and fixed: during an idle `/compact` pi, Claude Code and Codex all refused a prompt (the message was lost) and pi could start a turn beside an auto-compaction; each harness session now holds messages while compacting and sends them in order afterwards (also after a failed or stopped compaction). - [x] **I-217** Sub-agents on a different harness than their parent — `server` / `protocol` (2026-10-08)
  - Reported: 2026-10-08 — while discussing sub-agent models and agent roles (see I-218), the user wants any orchestrator to be able to launch a sub-agent on another harness (e.g. a pi chat starting a Codex sub-agent), so an agent definition can say which harness and model it runs on. Running sub-agents on another *device* was considered and dropped as overkill (2026-10-08).
  - Notes: today a sub-agent always runs in its parent's harness, folder and server (ARCHITECTURE "Sub-agents run in their parent's harness"; `services/app/agent-team.ts`, `POST /agents/spawn`). Glade runs sub-agents itself (sessions, messages as prompts, report/close, tabs), and all three harnesses use Glade's tools, so the change is mainly: start the child session on the requested harness (a disabled/missing harness = a clear spawn error), resolve its model/thinking within that harness (`modelsForHarness`, its sub-agent defaults), and make the rest (messages, steering, report-done, close, cards, tabs, side questions, usage meter) work when parent and child differ. Usage is billed to the child's harness account; its permission rules apply. Spawn request gets an optional `harness` (or comes from the agent definition, I-218); `inherit` = the parent's, the default.
  - Status: started 2026-10-08 (the user said go for I-217 + I-218 together, to be finished and tested unattended). Contract by the lead: `packages/protocol/src/agent-defs.ts`, `Settings.agentDefs`, `SpawnAgentRequest.harness`, `AgentInfo.icon/harness`, `Session.agentIcon`, `apps/server/src/services/agent-defs/service.ts` (method signatures). Workers: agent-defs (service + REST), spawn (cross-harness + per-harness application + tool lists), ui (Settings → Sub-agents + identity on cards/tabs).
  - Acceptance: from a pi chat, a sub-agent runs on Claude Code (and Codex), streams in its tab, can be messaged, reports back; FakeHarness tests for mixed harnesses.
  - Outcome (2026-10-08): a spawn's harness = request → agent definition (unless `inherit`) → the parent's; must be offered here (400 worded for the orchestrator). Model: the request's/definition's when that harness lists it → that harness's sub-agent setting → the parent's (same harness) → its default; thinking clamped. Messages, steering, report_done, close, crash restart (on its own harness, definition re-applied) work across harnesses; `AgentInfo`/`SpawnedAgentRef` carry `harness` (and icon/agent). Glade's own tools no longer ask for permission on Claude Code (`allowedTools: mcp__glade`). New: one automatic reminder when a sub-agent's turn ends without report_done (re-armed by a team message, never after the user typed in it). No schema change. Tests: `cross-harness-agents.test.ts` (17). Real run in a sandbox: a pi chat (Haiku) started a Claude Code scout (Haiku, native agent, found the function in ~3 s) and a Codex oracle (luna, low, read-only), results reached the parent, cards showed "Brandon · scout [Claude Code]" / "Dolly · oracle [Codex]".

- [x] **I-218** Glade agents: reusable sub-agent definitions per harness, with a human identity — `server` / `settings` / `protocol` (after I-217) (2026-10-08)
  - Reported: 2026-10-08 — the user wants to set up agents (like pi's agent definitions) that Glade stores itself, each with its own prompt, permissions and model, which orchestrators pick when launching sub-agents. Came out of a discussion about letting the orchestrator pick a cheaper or stronger model per task (compared Claude Code's per-call `model`, Amp's fixed roles, Cursor); the user prefers roles defined as agents.
  - Decided (user, 2026-10-08):
    - **Harness-first.** Creating an agent starts with choosing its harness (pi, Claude Code, Codex); the editor then offers that harness's own capabilities rather than a lowest-common-denominator generic set. Shared basics on top (name, description, prompt, model, thinking/effort, via the existing per-harness pickers), harness-specific settings below (Claude: tools, permission mode, MCP servers; Codex: sandbox, MCP servers; pi: tools). "Read-only" is a preset button that fills in each harness's settings, not a generic field.
    - **`inherit`**: `harness: inherit` / `model: inherit` (also the default when omitted, like Claude Code/Cursor) = the parent's harness / model (or the sub-agent model setting). A specific model requires a specific harness.
    - **Glade's own format, never touching other harnesses' files.** Markdown + YAML frontmatter (Claude Code's field names where they mean the same), plus Glade's fields: `harness`, `extends`, `nickname`, `color`, `icon`. Stored in Glade's data folder (personal) and `.agents/agents/*.md` in a project (shared). Glade never writes to `~/.claude/agents`, `~/.codex/agents`, pi's folders etc.
    - **Reference, don't copy: `extends`.** `extends: claude:<name>` / `codex:<name>` / `pi:<name>` (looked up in that tool's personal and project locations and pi packages) or a file path; read fresh at every spawn so upstream edits apply; the extended agent's harness defaults to its source; Glade's fields override theirs; the Glade file's body is appended to their prompt; a missing source shows "source not found" and can't be used. Discovered native agents are also listed live without any Glade file (no nickname: random name); to add a nickname/colour/icon or change anything, Glade creates a small Glade file that extends it.
    - **Names:** three names: the agent (`scout`, what the orchestrator picks and the UI's subtitle), the human name (`Brandon`, shown on cards/tabs/mentions: "Brandon · scout") and the per-run id the orchestrator uses for message/close. `nickname` is one name or a list: the first free one is used; when all are taken, numbering (`Brandon 2`). No nickname = the existing random pool (`services/agent-names.ts`).
    - **Colour** (optional, from the existing `AGENT_COLORS`; unset = assigned as today) and **icon** (optional, from a small built-in set, e.g. search, hammer, shield, book, flask, compass, bug, pen; unset = today's look).
  - How it reaches each harness (lead research, 2026-10-08): Glade starts the sub-agent itself and applies the definition at session start: Claude Code natively through the Agent SDK (`agents` option with the definition + `agent` to run the session as it, so hooks/MCP/permission mode apply); Codex via thread settings (`developerInstructions`, model, effort, sandbox; check whether thread/start can take the agent's TOML as a config layer); pi via `--append-system-prompt`, `--tools`, `--model`, `--thinking` (already used for agent-teams definitions, I-037).
  - Live tool lists, no static lists: pi via the Glade extension (`pi.getAllTools()`, incl. extension/MCP tools); Claude via the session's `system/init` (`tools`, `mcp_servers`) and `supportedAgents()`; Codex has no tool allowlist (sandbox modes are a fixed protocol set; MCP servers from its config). Saved per harness + project from the last session, refreshed when a chat starts; tools named in a file but not currently available show as "not available right now", never dropped.
  - Orchestrator side: how it chooses (discussed 2026-10-08). Each agent has a **description** (read only by the orchestrator: when to use it, written as a trigger, "Use when…, not for…") and a **prompt** (read only by the sub-agent: how to do the job); the orchestrator adds the task at each spawn. The available agents are listed **in `spawn_agent`'s own tool description** (name, description, harness · model, read-only), so the orchestrator doesn't have to call `list_agents` first (still available); no agent = today's generic sub-agent. The user naming an agent ("have scout look") always wins; an `@agent` mention in the composer can come later. Each agent can be turned on/off globally and per project to keep the list short (descriptions cost context). The editor shows a hint + example for descriptions and a "Write description for me" button (quick-tasks model, from the prompt). A short guideline in the orchestrator's instructions: prefer a fitting agent over the generic one, read-only agents for looking things up. Picking one starts it on its harness (I-217). Settings → Agents gets an agents list + editor (create, edit, extend a discovered one, delete Glade's own).
  - Status: started 2026-10-08 (the user said go for I-217 + I-218 together, to be finished and tested unattended). Contract by the lead: `packages/protocol/src/agent-defs.ts`, `Settings.agentDefs`, `SpawnAgentRequest.harness`, `AgentInfo.icon/harness`, `Session.agentIcon`, `apps/server/src/services/agent-defs/service.ts` (method signatures). Workers: agent-defs (service + REST), spawn (cross-harness + per-harness application + tool lists), ui (Settings → Sub-agents + identity on cards/tabs).
  - Acceptance: create a Claude Code `scout` (read-only preset, Haiku, nickname Brandon, colour, icon) from Settings; a pi chat launches it and the card shows "Brandon · scout" on Claude Code; a discovered `~/.claude/agents` agent is listed and usable without copying; tests for the format, `extends`, `inherit`, nickname numbering.
  - Outcome (2026-10-08): `services/agent-defs/` (format, discovery of Claude Code / Codex / pi agents honouring `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, pi's agent dir; `extends` up to 4 deep, cycles refused; precedence + `shadowedBy`; availability/problems; `base` for the editor; live tools cache `<data>/agent-tools.json`; small YAML/TOML subset parsers, no new dependency) + REST `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/agent-defs`, `/describe`, `/tools` (another device: read-only). Spawn resolves `agent` on the server; Claude Code runs it natively (SDK `agents` + `agent`, role prompt via `systemPrompt.append`; `hooks` dropped: no SDK field), Codex via developer instructions + sandbox preset + some config keys, pi via its flags (missing tools refused). `spawn_agent`'s description lists the available agents (+ guideline); pi fetches them at start and reports its tools. Identity: nickname list with numbering, colour, icon on cards/tabs/chips/transcript ("Brandon · scout", harness badge when different). Settings → Sub-agents: list by source with global/per-project switches (+ reset to the global one), harness-first editor (shared fields, per-harness section, Read only preset, live tool checklists, Write description for me), Customize a discovered agent = an `extends` file. Tests: agent-defs format/service/http (41), Sub-agents settings (18), identity (9), plus harness tests.
    - Open (small): an `extends` written as a file path into a custom Claude config folder is read as a Glade file (use `claude:<name>`); Claude Code's tool list is only recorded from main Claude chats; small models may still skip report_done after the reminder.

Customizations are saved in one place (personal); per-project use stays the on/off switches. The server refuses a second `extends` of the same source (409).

If a clash appears from outside (e.g. a new `~/.claude/agents/scout.md` while Glade has a scout), both stay listed with a problem "Two agents are named scout; rename one" and neither is offered to chats until it's resolved (no silent winner).

The list shows one row under the source with a Customized badge; Customize on a customized agent opens it; the customization editor has a fixed name, no scope choice and Reset to Original (deletes the Glade file). Chats get the customization, never the source. Same name anywhere else = a clash: every agent with that name gets a problem and isn't offered until one is renamed (no silent winner); PUT refuses (409) a second customization of a source, creating/renaming onto a taken name ("pi already has an agent named scout — customize it instead") and overwriting a customization with a standalone agent; a customization must be named like its source (400). Customized in both personal and project scope: a problem asking to reset one, nothing deleted. The user's existing pi customizations (scout, worker, reviewer, researcher) already follow the rule. Tests: service, HTTP, spawn list, Settings UI.

    - **Use other agents for sub-agents** (cross-harness). Off: a sub-agent always runs on its chat's harness; agents pinned to another harness aren't offered to that chat (listed with "Runs on Codex; other agents are turned off for sub-agents"), and `spawn_agent` drops its `harness` parameter.
    - **Use other models for sub-agents** (cross-model). Off: every sub-agent uses its chat's model and thinking; agent definitions' model/thinking and the per-agent "Sub-agent model" settings are ignored (rows show a note; the Sub-agent model pickers in Settings → Agents are greyed with the reason). Prompts, tools, permissions, nicknames still apply.
    - The server enforces both at spawn (not just the tool text), and `spawn_agent`'s agent list follows them.
  - Decided (user, 2026-10-08): both switches **default off** (a sub-agent uses its chat's harness and model until the user opts in), and they are **per device** only (no project overrides).
  - Status: started 2026-10-08 (user said go). Contract: `Settings.agent.subagentOtherHarnesses` / `subagentOtherModels` (default false).
  - Acceptance: with each switch off, spawns stay on the chat's harness / model regardless of definitions and settings; Settings explains what's ignored; tests.
  - Outcome (2026-10-08): two switches at the top of Settings → Sub-agents (`Settings.agent.subagentOtherHarnesses` / `subagentOtherModels`, per device, default off). The server enforces them at spawn: other agents off → the child runs on the caller's harness, an agent pinned elsewhere or an explicit other harness is a 400 worded for the orchestrator, such agents aren't offered and `spawn_agent` loses its `harness` parameter; other models off → the caller's model and thinking always (requests, definitions and the per-agent sub-agent settings ignored; prompts, tools, permissions, identity still apply). Settings shows notes on affected agents (they stay available for chats of their own harness) and greys the Agents pages' sub-agent model/thinking pickers with a link. Tests: spawn matrix, listing, notes, UI.

## Future features

Ideas we want documented but are **not planned**. Nothing here is worked on until the user
promotes it (it then becomes an Inbox item with a new `I-` id and a link back to its `F-` id).
Next id: F-031.

- **F-001** Connectors: Glade as an MCP hub for any harness — `server` / `settings` / `chat`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — asked what Codex "connectors" are; wants the idea for later, harness-agnostic, not tied to pi (pi itself has no notion of MCP).
  - Background: in ChatGPT/Codex a connector (now "app") is an MCP server with packaging on top: a directory, one-click OAuth handled by ChatGPT, workspace permissions, optional custom UI (Apps SDK). Plugins bundle skills + apps/MCP servers + hooks. Codex exposes connected apps as tools of one built-in MCP server and supports `$app-name` mentions. Pi's core has no MCP (by design); the user has it only via the `pi-mcp-adapter` extension.
  - Design: Glade manages connectors (Connectors page: catalog + custom URL, OAuth sign-in by Glade, tokens in the macOS Keychain, enable per project/chat) and exposes the enabled ones as **one MCP gateway endpoint** per session on its local server. Harnesses connect the simplest way they can: Claude Code / Codex natively (Glade passes the gateway in their MCP config); pi through a tiny Glade extension loaded with `-e` that bridges the gateway's tools (independent of `pi-mcp-adapter`). Composer: `$connector` mention + per-chat toggles. Later: custom connector UI (cards).
- ~~**F-002** Drag tabs into new panes (bottom/right) with their own tab sets — `shell` (was I-039; follows I-036)~~ — dropped 2026-09-27: the user no longer wants split panes
- **F-003** Other tab kinds: terminal, diff, file — `shell` (was I-040; terminal needs xterm.js + a pty on the server) — terminal promoted to I-187 (2026-09-30)
- ~~**F-004** Keyboard shortcuts settings page (rebinding) — `settings`~~ — dropped 2026-09-27: the user doesn't want shortcut rebinding
- ~~**F-005** Remote access from your phone (Glade keeps running on the Mac) — `server` / `shell` / `infra`~~ — promoted 2026-09-27 to I-121–I-127 (environments, remote access, own store)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — asked whether Glade could be used remotely, securely: it keeps running on one Mac, and the user reaches their conversations, projects and chats from the phone and can chat from there.
  - Design sketch:
    - **Network, secure by default:** don't open a port to the internet. Recommended path: **Tailscale** (private network between your devices): Glade serves on the tailnet, ideally through `tailscale serve`, which gives a real HTTPS certificate on `<mac>.<tailnet>.ts.net` and only your devices can reach it. Alternatives for later: Cloudflare Tunnel + Access, or a self-signed/`mkcert` certificate on the LAN.
    - **Authentication on top:** device pairing. Settings → Devices shows a QR code with a one-time pairing code; the phone scans it and receives a long-lived device token (HTTP-only cookie, stored hashed on the Mac); list, name and revoke devices in Settings. Every non-loopback request needs a valid device token (replaces today's loopback-only check with "loopback OR paired device"); rate-limit pairing attempts; WebSocket authenticated the same way.
    - **Which server:** the phone talks to the installed app's server (always running on the Mac; I-062 lets it share data with `pnpm dev`). Setting to allow remote access (off by default) with the chosen interface.
    - **Phone UI:** responsive layout: sidebar as a slide-over list of workspaces, one chat full-screen, tabs as a compact switcher, composer with the model/thinking pickers; installable as a PWA on the iPhone home screen (icon, full-screen). Same features: projects, chats, sub-agent tabs (read + type), slash commands, `@` files, search/Ask.
    - **Keeping the Mac available:** option to prevent sleep while remote access is on (a power assertion) and to keep running with the window closed (already the case).
    - **Nice to have:** push notifications to the phone when a chat needs input (opt-in; the user doesn't want system notifications on the Mac), "open on Mac" handoff.
  - Decisions (user, 2026-09-26): **Tailscale** is the default route in the plan (the user wants to review its security before this is built). **Not phone-only**: e.g. Glade running on a Mac Studio, used from a MacBook Pro (browser or the desktop app pointed at a remote server) and from the phone, several devices at once. So: any number of paired devices, each with its own token and name; the desktop app gets a "Connect to a remote Glade…" option (server URL + pairing) instead of starting its own server; per-device "viewing"/unread works like multiple browser tabs today. Before building: a short security review (Tailscale ACLs, token storage, CSRF/Origin rules for non-loopback hosts, what an attacker with a stolen device token could do).
- ~~**F-006** Show full history across compactions (`get_entries` instead of `get_messages`) — `pi-adapter` / `chat`~~ — dropped 2026-09-27: the user doesn't want it
- ~~**F-007** Attach non-image files by reference — `chat` (see I-010 notes)~~ — promoted to I-090 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-008** Agent tool for the chat finder (`find_chats` / `open_chat`) — `server` / extension kit (I-046 part b)~~ — promoted to I-091 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-009** `@` mentions shown as path chips in sent messages — `chat` (I-044 optional part)~~ — promoted to I-092 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-010** Search: jump to the exact matching message when opening a hit — `chat` (I-045 gap)~~ — promoted to I-093 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-011** Alternative harness: Claude Code — `server` (harness adapter + tests; the Connectors gateway and sub-agent API are designed to be harness-neutral)~~ — done as I-173 (2026-09-29)
- **F-012** Conversation mode: talk to the agent, hear the replies — `chat` / `settings` / `server` — iPhone part promoted to I-180 (2026-09-29)
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — a conversation mode: speak to the agent, the speech is transcribed to text, the agent answers in text, and a text-to-speech model reads the reply aloud. No voice model set by default (cost is unknown); clicking the button without one tells the user to set one, with a button that opens the settings page. We probably need to know models' capabilities to tell whether they can do this.
  - Design sketch:
    - **Flow:** a conversation button in the composer (waveform icon). Speech → transcription → sent as a normal prompt (the chat's own model answers in text as usual) → the reply's text is read aloud. Push-to-talk (hold a key/button) first; hands-free with voice-activity detection (auto-send after a pause, interrupt playback by speaking) later. Reading aloud skips code blocks and tool output (e.g. "I ran 3 tools" / "here's the code on screen") and can stop anytime.
    - **Voice models are separate from the chat model** and **off by default** (cost). Settings → Voice: a *transcription* model and a *speech* (text-to-speech) model, chosen independently, each showing where it runs and a rough cost note. Clicking the conversation button with nothing configured shows a dialog "Choose a voice model to use conversation mode" with a button that opens Settings → Voice.
    - **Capabilities:** pi's model list only reports text/image input (no audio), and harnesses don't do speech, so Glade keeps its own registry of voice providers with capabilities (`transcribe`, `speak`, streaming, languages, voices, local/cloud, pricing hint), and the pickers only offer providers that have the needed capability.
    - **Provider options:** free/local first — macOS on-device speech recognition (Apple Speech / SpeechAnalyzer via the Tauri app) and macOS voices for speech, or local whisper.cpp; cloud options later (e.g. OpenAI transcription/TTS, ElevenLabs) with API keys stored in the Keychain.
    - Works from the phone too once remote access (F-005) exists.
  - Reading replies (user, 2026-09-26: ideally replies are read in full, maybe highlighting the text as it's spoken, or perhaps only the first couple of paragraphs; when the user starts talking over it, it should stop speaking and listen):
    - Default: read in full, **highlighting the current word** (and auto-scrolling). Feasible: macOS voices report word boundaries (`AVSpeechSynthesizer` `willSpeakRangeOfSpeechString`; the web view's `speechSynthesis` `boundary` events give character offsets); ElevenLabs returns per-character timestamps; OpenAI TTS has no timings (approximate or sentence-level highlight).
    - Controls: stop, skip to next paragraph, speed. Setting for long replies: read all / first N paragraphs / a spoken summary written by the fast model. Code blocks and tool output are announced ("there's a code block on screen"), not read.
    - **Barge-in:** keep the mic listening with echo cancellation while speaking; detecting the user's voice stops playback immediately and switches to listening (like ChatGPT/Claude voice modes). Most reliable with headphones.
  - Open questions for when it's promoted: which providers first (local-only to start?); push-to-talk key.

- ~~**F-013** Usage view, later maybe soft budgets — `settings` / `server`~~ — dropped 2026-09-27: the user doesn't want the usage view and asked to remove it
- ~~**F-014** Opt-in git worktree per conversation — `server` / `shell`~~ — promoted to I-096 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-015** Changes panel (git status of the chat's folder) — `server` / `chat`~~ — promoted to I-097 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-016** Saved prompts and snippets — `chat` / `settings`~~ — promoted to I-098 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-017** Prepare the repo for open source: scrub personal info from files and git history — `infra` / `docs`~~ — promoted to I-208 (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — check the repo for sensitive information, since the user may open-source it at some point; asked whether it can be replaced in the git history too. Not going open source yet.
  - Audit (2026-09-27): **no secrets** (no API keys, tokens, private keys or passwords; no `.env` ever committed; `.gitignore` covers `.env*`; `anthropic-auth.ts` only reads pi's auth file at runtime). Personal identifiers found: the commit author email, a home-folder path in a test fixture, a personal bundle id, references to private repos and local paths in the docs, and the user's own words quoted in PLAN.md. Re-run on the whole history on 2026-10-05 before the cleanup (see I-208).
  - The plan and the user's decisions moved to I-208 (same URL with a fresh repo, the GitHub private email address, an `io.github` app id, AGENTS.md trimmed, PLAN.md paraphrased and its old versions dropped from history).
- ~~**F-021** Carry uncommitted changes into a new worktree — `server`~~ — promoted to I-117 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-018** Hand off a chat between Local and Worktree — `server` / `chat`~~ — dropped 2026-09-27: not wanted for now (the user asked to remove it)
- ~~**F-019** Glade's own bundled pi extension for sub-agents and chat tools (no extension kit needed), plus a "Use sub-agents" setting — `server` / `settings` / the extension kit~~ — promoted to I-116 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-020** ACP harness: run any Agent Client Protocol agent; dedicated connectors only for the agents the user cares most about — `server` / `settings` / `chat`~~ — promoted to I-119 (2026-09-27)
- ~~**F-022** iPhone app: an installable iPhone app (web view, not native) as a separate app with its own phone layout over a shared core — `shell` / `infra` (after I-127)~~ — promoted to I-164 (2026-09-27); plan in `docs/design/iphone-app.md`
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — not a native phone app: an installable iPhone app, probably using web views. The desktop and phone apps get two completely separate layouts that share components (e.g. the chat composer, chat messages). The always-open left sidebar is desktop-only; on the phone it might overlay the chat. The phone app has no tabs and no opening of sub-agents. To be tackled after the remote work is finished.
  - Idea:
    - **Two layouts, one app.** `apps/web` gets `layouts/desktop/` (today's `app/Layout.tsx`, sidebar, tabs, sub-agent pane, changes panel) and `layouts/phone/` (chat list → chat screen, sidebar as an overlay drawer, no tabs, no sub-agent pane or changes panel). The layout is picked once at startup by device (not responsive CSS squeezing the desktop layout). Everything under `ui/`, `state/` and most of `features/` is shared: Composer, Transcript and messages, tool cards, pickers, search, settings sections. Only the shells differ.
    - **Prep while doing I-123:** keep feature components free of desktop-layout assumptions (no reaching into tab or pane state from Composer or Transcript), so the phone shell can reuse them unchanged.
    - **Phone features:**
      - chats, the composer (with photos), permission cards, stop, the model/agent pickers for the chat's environment, and search;
      - sub-agents shown read-only as their cards and reports in the parent chat, with no tabs;
      - later: notifications when a run finishes or needs input.
    - **Packaging options:** (a) an installable web app (PWA, "Add to Home Screen") served by the host over Tailscale HTTPS; the cheapest, and usable right after I-127, but it's one icon per host and has limited Keychain and camera access. (b) A thin shell app (Tauri 2 iOS or Capacitor, a WKWebView) bundling the same web build; one app for all environments via the I-123 multi-environment client, QR scanning, Keychain for device tokens, and push notifications; needs Xcode, signing and TestFlight. Suggestion: (a) first, (b) if notifications or several Macs matter.
  - Open questions (for when it's promoted): Which packaging? Should sub-agents be hidden entirely, or visible read-only? Which settings belong on the phone? Notifications?
  - Decisions (user, 2026-09-27): **option (b)**, a real iPhone app wrapping the web code (not a home-screen web app). **Two separate apps** from one shared core: the Mac app has only the desktop layout and the iPhone app only the phone layout. Proposed structure: `packages/app-core` (ui primitives, state, composer, transcript, tool cards, pickers, API and sync client), `apps/web` (desktop layout, bundled into the Mac app) and `apps/iphone` (phone layout, bundled into the iOS shell: Tauri 2 iOS or Capacitor). The iPhone app runs no server; it connects to Macs through the I-123 environment connections and I-126 pairing (scanning the QR code with its own camera, tokens in the Keychain).
  - Naming (user, 2026-09-27, via a side question): there will be an iPhone app and maybe eventually an Android app, so make the distinction now: call it the iPhone app, not "the phone app". Use "iPhone app" (`apps/iphone`) in plans, docs and code. Android is a separate future app (F-025). Tooling when it starts: Xcode (Mac App Store), an Apple ID for your own iPhone (7-day signing), and the Apple Developer Program ($99/yr) for TestFlight and App Store.
- **F-023** External dependencies: list them, document them, and make Glade work well without each one — `infra` / `shell` / `settings` / `docs`
  - Reported: 2026-09-27 — find our external dependencies, document them, and handle every case well. E.g. Tailscale: someone without it installed must not hit a crash on any of the remote pages. pi: on a second Mac the app wouldn't even launch without pi installed. When no harness is detected, the app should still launch and let the user view settings, or at least show a better page telling them to install one of the supported harnesses, with links to their install pages.
  - Scope ideas:
    - **Inventory:** a list of every external dependency, with what uses it and what happens when it's missing, in docs and the README. Candidates:
      - pi (CLI + auth/models), the ACP agents (claude/gemini/codex adapters), Tailscale (CLI, running, HTTPS certs)
      - git, the user's shell/PATH, Node ≥ 22.13 for `pnpm dev`, macOS UNUserNotificationCenter, Keychain, the Chrome/default browser for links
    - **No harness installed:** the app always launches. A welcome / "Install an agent" screen lists supported harnesses with install links and a "Check again" button. Settings, projects and remote environments stay usable, so you could still use another device's agents over remote access.
    - **Tailscale missing, logged out or without HTTPS:** every remote page renders with a clear status and a fix-it link, and nothing crashes.
    - **Tests:** each dependency missing, e.g. a fake PATH without pi / tailscale / git.
- Not wanted (2026-09-27 discussion, recorded so they aren't re-proposed): checkpoints / undo of a turn (definitely not wanted), edit and resend, forking a chat.
- **F-029** Tokens per second in the chat: exact read/write speed for local models, measured write speed for cloud models — `server` / `chat`
  - Reported: 2026-09-30 — asked whether we can show tokens per second for our models, ideally both read and write speed, but only if the numbers are accurate. → Filed as a future feature; the user won't decide until they see whether they'd actually use it much.
  - Background: llama-server reports engine-measured `timings` on each reply (`prompt_n`/`prompt_ms`/`prompt_per_second` = read/prefill, `predicted_n`/`predicted_ms`/`predicted_per_second` = write; `cache_n` = tokens reused, not counted in `prompt_n`); with `timings_per_token: true` every streamed chunk carries the running write speed (live). pi ignores `timings` (checked: not in pi's code). Anthropic/OpenAI report no timings, only exact token counts at the end of a message.
  - Design sketch: (1) local: Glade points pi's `LLAMA_BASE_URL` at a pass-through on its server that adds `timings_per_token`, reads `timings` from the stream and attaches them to the chat's current reply; exact read + write, live; harness-neutral (any agent using the local model). (2) cloud: write speed = provider-reported output tokens ÷ (last − first streamed token), shown after each reply ends, labelled "measured by Glade". No read speed for cloud (time to first token mixes queueing, network and caching), no character-based estimates anywhere. (3) UI: a quiet line under each assistant reply ("Read 1,850 tok/s · Write 32.4 tok/s" / "Write 78 tok/s"), chat averages in the usage popover.

- ~~**F-030** Public website for Glade: what it is, its features, why use it — motion-first — `docs` / `infra`~~ — promoted to I-209 (2026-10-05)
  - Reported: 2026-10-05 — Glade will be open-sourced relatively soon, and it needs a site where people can see the features and why they'd use it. Motion should be a big part of it: scroll animations and animation in general, things moving and floating, overlapping, with layering that gives a 3D feel. Hosting: preferably not self-hosted. Timing: probably right after the current agent's work (I-203).
  - Order (user, 2026-10-05): after F-017 (repo cleanup first).
  - Hosting (researched 2026-10-05): **GitHub Pages** (free for public repos; site ≤ 1 GB, soft 100 GB/month traffic, files ≤ 100 MB; deploy from a `site/` folder with a GitHub Action; custom domain + HTTPS). Alternatives: Cloudflare Pages (free, no traffic cap, PR previews), Netlify (300 credits/month, pauses when out), Vercel Hobby (non-commercial only). Recommendation: GitHub Pages, Cloudflare Pages if traffic ever matters. Needs the repo public (or a paid plan), so it lines up with F-017.
  - Sketch: static site (no server), built with the `web-design` skill (from the user's extension kit): GSAP + ScrollTrigger set pieces, floaty layered/overlapping panels with depth (3D transforms, parallax), real UI captured from Glade (short, muted, compressed WebM/MP4 loops; long demos as click-to-load YouTube embeds), `prefers-reduced-motion` respected. Sections: hero, the main features (agents: pi / Claude Code / Codex; sub-agents; remote access + iPhone; local models; worktrees + changes; search/⌘K; terminal; voice), why Glade, install/build from source, link to the repo.
  - Open questions (when promoted): domain (custom or `<user>.github.io/glade`); in the Glade repo (`site/`) or its own repo; which features lead; screenshots/recordings made by agents in sandboxes (fake data) vs by the user.

## Phase 0 — Foundations

  - Correction (2026-09-26): it's actually Apple Intelligence **Writing Tools** ("Write with Siri"), which WebKit attaches to focused text fields; see I-042. Original conclusion: not an app bug. Probed the page at that spot: nothing rendered there. It's macOS's text-caret indicator (Sonoma+: input source / Caps Lock / Dictation badge next to the caret in the focused empty composer). Can be disabled system-wide with `defaults write kCFPreferencesAnyApplication TSMLanguageIndicatorEnabled 0` (log out/in).
  - Status (2026-09-26): root cause found (stream-probe, instrumented every hop with real Opus 5.5): Glade adds no delay. After tool calls Opus thinks silently (~7 s, no thinking text), then sends ~2000 chars within 20 ms and streams the rest at ~250–300 chars/s; I-072's reveal drained any backlog in 200 ms, so the burst still popped in. Fix ready (`/tmp/glade-stream-fix.keep.patch`, not applied because chat-colour owns `Transcript.tsx` right now): pace backlogs at ~1 char/ms (≥ 200 ms, ≤ 2.5 s lag), first mount mid-stream still catches up in 200 ms, Markdown keeps tolerating unfinished syntax until the reveal is done; tests included. Follow-up in the same change: buffer session events while the store is "loading" (deltas arriving between snapshot and response are dropped today; not observed).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): applied the stream-probe fix: backlogs reveal at ~1 char/ms (≥ 200 ms, ≤ 2.5 s lag; `drainTime` in `features/chat/smooth-text.ts`), a mid-stream first mount still catches up in 200 ms, Markdown tolerates unfinished syntax until the reveal is done. Measured on real Opus: before ~1850 chars in 0.5 s, after steady ~900 chars/s. Not done: buffering events while a chat is "loading" — replaying them could append deltas already in the snapshot twice; needs per-session event sequence numbers (the final message repairs any gap today).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): CSS only (`features/chat/chat.css`): the Working/Thinking/Compacting labels are grey (`--pi-fg-subtle`) with a narrow rainbow band (`--pi-rainbow`) sweeping left to right every 2.4 s (sped up to a 1.4 s pass after the user found it too slow, then a ~0.8 s rest between passes like the tool-group shimmer, then a grey veil layer (`--pi-shimmer-base`) so the band is brightest in the middle and fades into the grey at its edges, 2026-09-26); spinner plain muted; reduced motion = plain grey; `--pi-thinking-gradient` removed.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): the app's server prefers port 4327 (`tauri dev` 4328; builds with `GLADE_APP_IDENTIFIER` take a free port; falls back to a free port if taken), so the origin and all `localStorage` UI state (sidebar width/collapsed, collapsed projects) survive relaunches. The web app stores the last route (`glade.lastRoute`, incl. `?tab=` and settings) and restores it on a first load at bare `/` when the chat/project/section still exists (`app/lastRoute.ts`, 8 tests). Not verified: a real relaunch of the installed app (needs an install).
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): server assigns a fun name (60-name list) and an `AGENT_COLORS` colour at spawn, unique among the workspace's active agents (`services/agent-names.ts`), stored on the session and agent record; the parent lists all spawned agents in `SessionSummary.spawnedAgents` (kept after close). pi `spawn_agent` → tool kind `task` with `agentName`. Web: wrapping chips above the composer (colour, name, status, latest message truncated; Stop all in a "…" menu); `AgentSpawnCard` in the transcript ("Maya · reviewer", status, time; expands to task + live activity / report); finished/message/exited prompts fold into the card, `AgentMessageCard` only as fallback; names/colours on tabs and the always-shown AgentBar (`--pi-agent-<key>` tokens). Open: a reused agent name with a failed spawn call that has no error status could match the wrong card; `spawnedAgents` isn't capped; a change to agents.json from another server doesn't re-push the parent.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): hits carry `message: MessageAnchor { role, timestamp }` (harness message ids are positional and change on reload/compaction, so no ids); the palette calls `requestJump` before navigating; the transcript centers the matched message and highlights it for 2.2 s, turning off stick-to-bottom; if it isn't loaded, the chat opens at the bottom with a "Message not in loaded history" toast. Also `search`/`ask` take `{ exclude }` (used by I-091).
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): the user-bubble image row is capped at `max-w-[85%]`; thumbnails `min-w-0 max-w-full`, images `h-auto max-w-full object-contain`. The same limits apply to tool-result and assistant images. Checked with a 4000×400 image in both themes. The fake harness now keeps attached images.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): `app/lastSettings.ts` (`glade.lastSettings` = {section, envId for host sections}); a `/settings` index route reopens it (⌘,, sidebar, palette and `/settings` all use `routes.settings()`). Deep links win and become the remembered page; unknown sections fall back to General, and a disconnected environment falls back to this machine.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Mac app:** its own `src-tauri/src/notifications.rs` on UNUserNotificationCenter, because tauri-plugin-notification on macOS has no click callback and always reports "granted". It has a real permission state, and clicks focus the window and open the chat (even after a relaunch). It reports "unavailable" instead of crashing when there's no app bundle.
    - **Web:** `state/notifications.ts` + `lib/system-notifications.ts`. Triggers: needs input / finished / failed; top-level chats only; only after the sync `live` marker (no replays); one window per transition (localStorage claim). Settings → General → Notifications (4 switches, per device, "Allow Notifications…", a denied hint).
    - **Server:** `SessionSummary.attentionLine` (the question, error or last sentence).
  - Open: the native path is verified only by compiling and tests; the first real banner, the permission prompt and click routing are checked on the user's install.
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-27): the "let other devices use this Mac" switch should come right after the master switch, since it's one of the most important things to see immediately. Order: (1) Remote access master switch, (2) the sharing switch ("Let other devices use this device", with the address under it when on), (3) the Tailscale status row, (4) Connect to a Device… / Share This Device… and the Connections list, (5) Recent activity.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27):
    - **Order:** master switch → "Let other devices use this device" (+ address) → Tailscale → Connect to a Device… / Share This Device… (offers to turn sharing on first) → Connections → Recent activity.
    - **Connections:** one row per device (`state/connections.ts` merges saved environments and paired devices via `clientEnvironmentId`). The "You use it" line has status plus Pair Again… / Retry / Disconnect…; the "Uses this device" line has last seen plus Rename / Revoke…. Each direction's buttons sit on their own line.
    - **Wording:** "device" instead of "Mac" throughout remote access.
  - Verified with two sandboxes paired both ways: one row each side, and removing one direction keeps the other.
  - Outcome (2026-09-27): a known target shows a read-only "Connecting to <name> · <host>"; a typed code gets the address field, a hint and quick picks. No name field: the pair request sends this device's own name. `connectionName()` resolves local alias > the other device's own name > the host-side device name, and is used by the row, badge, pickers, settings switcher and notifications. One Rename per row sets the server device name and the local alias (`SavedEnvironment.alias`, which survives Pair Again). `adoptDeviceName` keeps a host-side name when a connection becomes two-way. Open: the Allow prompt shows the name the device sent, even for a known device with an alias.
  - Outcome (2026-09-29): a small "Model · Thinking ▾" line under the chat title opens the Model & Thinking sheet (`ModelThinkingPicker` `trigger` prop; the model/thinking actions moved to `features/chat/chat-model.ts`); hidden for agents without models. Also (I-170 follow-up by the lead): the chat screen shows the "Can't reach" notice with Retry above the composer, and instead of a blank screen when its Mac's chats have left the store.
- [x] **I-041** Sidebar alignment, take 3: projects flush with the section header; status back on the right — `shell`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — project names are indented; they should be left-aligned with the "Projects" header. Move the status indicator back to the right so things line up better overall: as before, the status shows when not hovering, and on hover it swaps to the menu actions. (Screenshot: project rows indented under "Projects")
  - Notes: Supersedes the column layout from I-006/I-030 (both were done as asked; this revises them).
    Target grid (x = content start inside the row inset):
    ```
    Projects                          +
    📁 piui-demo
        Current working directory…  ●     ← status or age on the right
    📁 pi-ui
        New Chat Session           now
    Chats
    New Chat Session Started       34m
    ```
    - Project rows: folder icon flush with the "Projects" header text (indent 0), name after the icon.
    - Project chats: title aligned with the **project name** (one icon column in).
    - Standalone chats: title flush with the "Chats" header (indent 0).
    - Right side of chat rows: status indicator when not idle, otherwise age/pin; on hover (or while the menu is open) it's replaced by the hover actions, as before I-006. Collapsed projects keep their rolled-up status on the right too.
    - Remove the leading status column from `SidebarItem` (or keep the prop unused) and simplify `ui/sidebar-metrics.ts` accordingly; drop line and pinned divider follow the new title columns. Settings sidebar unchanged.
  - Acceptance: screenshots (dark + light) with a working + an unread chat in a project and a standalone chat, hovered and not hovered; sidebar tests updated.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): leading status column removed; project rows and standalone titles at x=15 (flush with section headers), project names and project-chat titles at x=34; right side shows pin + status (or age when idle), replaced by "…" on hover/menu open; collapsed projects show rolled-up status on the right.
- [x] **I-042** Hide the "Write with Siri" / Writing Tools button on the composer — `shell` / `infra`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — an odd "Write with Siri" icon appears on the composer and looks bad; turn it off if possible. (Screenshot: round Writing Tools button at the composer's left edge; the same thing as I-034)
  - Notes: It's Apple Intelligence Writing Tools, which WebKit (the Tauri app's web view) attaches to editable fields. Options, in order:
    1. Web: add `writingsuggestions="false"` (+ `autocorrect="off"`, keep `spellcheck`) to the composer textarea and other text inputs (`ui/TextField`, `TextArea`, `features/chat/Composer.tsx`); check whether WebKit hides the Writing Tools affordance for it.
    2. Desktop: set `WKWebViewConfiguration.writingToolsBehavior = .none` (macOS 15+) for the app's web view. Tauri/wry don't expose it; options are setting it via the native web view handle (`webview.with_webview(|wv| …)` + objc2, if WebKit honours changing it after creation or via `wv.configuration`), or creating the window's web view with a patched configuration. Check the WebKit source/behaviour (WebKit PR #30310 fixed a case where the menu item still showed with `.none`).
    3. Fallback (user's machine only): a system setting to hide the floating Writing Tools button, or turning Writing Tools off under Apple Intelligence & Siri. Document it.
    Should keep normal text editing, spellcheck and copy/paste working. The browser version (Chrome) doesn't show it.
  - Acceptance: in the installed Mac app, focusing the composer shows no Writing Tools button (screenshot); the choice is documented in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md.
  - Status (2026-09-26): fixed in code: the web view answers NO to `-allowsWritingToolsAffordance` (`src-tauri/src/writing_tools.rs`); `writingsuggestions`/`autocorrect` and `writingToolsBehavior = .none` did not hide it on macOS 27 (tested in a standalone WKWebView). Verified in the dev app and by the user in the installed app (typing and paste fine).

- [x] **I-035** Workspaces & sessions: data model (foundation for tabs and sub-agents) — `protocol` / `server`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — design agreed with the user over several messages (cmux-like workspaces; see I-036/I-037).
  - Design (agreed):
    - A **workspace** is one sidebar row (today's "chat"): title, project (or standalone), pin/pinOrder, created date, status, saved layout. Workspaces are independent of each other, even in the same project/folder.
    - A workspace contains **sessions**: each is one agent conversation (one pi session file) running in the workspace's folder. Kinds: `main` (a tab in the main area; the first one is created with the workspace) and `subagent` (spawned by a session of this workspace; shown in the right half).
    - The user decides when main tabs are created ("New tab" in the workspace); nothing is grouped automatically. Sessions never get their own sidebar rows.
    - Workspace status rolls up from its sessions (blocked > working > unread > idle; failed/interrupted flags too).
  - Notes: Protocol: rename/reshape `Chat` → `Workspace` + `Session` (ids, `workspaceId`, `kind`, `parentSessionId` for sub-agents, title, sessionRef, model, thinking, status inputs, runInProgress/interrupted, agent name for sub-agents). Server: store (`workspaces.json` + sessions, **migration**: every existing chat becomes a workspace with one main session, keeping ids so URLs/sessions survive), AppService keyed by session for live processes, `ChatSummary`-style `WorkspaceSummary` with rolled-up status and per-session status, WebSocket messages for workspace/session upserts, `chat_event` carries `sessionId`. Routing: `/w/:workspaceId` (project variant as today) with the active tab `?tab=<sessionId>` (or `/w/:id/s/:sessionId`) so refresh restores the focused tab.
  - Acceptance: existing data migrates losslessly; all current features (titles, unread, interrupted, ordering, pinning, usage, slash commands) work per session / per workspace; tests.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): protocol `Workspace`/`Session` (+ summaries, rollup helpers) in `packages/protocol/src/workspaces.ts`; `<dataDir>/workspaces.json` with a one-time migration (each chat → workspace + one main session, same id; `chats.json` kept as backup); session-level REST (`/api/sessions/:id/*`) and workspace CRUD; WebSocket `session_event`/`session_upsert`/`workspace_upsert`…, client `viewing` takes a list of session ids; web data layer keyed by session; routes take workspace ids + `?tab=`. UI unchanged apart from renames. Verified: migrated data loads, a real prompt streams, a second session created via the API streams and rolls up. `AppService.createSession(…, {kind:"subagent"})` ready for I-037 (no HTTP route yet).

Replace it with a helper that only adjusts the list container's `scrollTop` (row above the viewport → align top; below → align bottom, accounting for group headers and padding), and use it for every keyboard-navigated list: the ⌘K palette (`ui/CommandPalette.tsx`), the future `@` file menu (I-044), and any others using `scrollIntoView` (grep). Keep group headers visible when the first item of a group is selected (scroll to include the header).

On exit: decrement the ref-count and delete the sandbox when it reaches zero; a stale-sandbox sweep removes leftovers older than a day. AGENTS.md + issue-queue skill require it for agent testing. Verified: parallel sandboxes, sharing, --keep, crash sweep, a --real run cleaned up its session files, user data untouched.

(3) Opening a closed sub-agent's tab restarts its pi process (like any session); consider not starting it until the user types. (4) Desktop menu/tab shortcuts come with I-053.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): `SessionSummary.agent` pushed to the browser; sub-agent tabs show ✓ done / ⊘ stopped with a tooltip (name, status, task, result) and an `AgentBar` above the conversation; agent-teams wording backend-neutral (the extension kit); crashed agents are viewed from the session file without restarting and start again when typed in. Left: idle-close timers are in-memory only (a kept-open done agent isn't auto-closed after a server restart).
- [x] **I-055** Finished sub-agents don't close their tab, and their tab stays stuck on "Working…" — `server` / `chat`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — a finished sub-agent's tab doesn't close: the agent said it closed the pane but it hadn't, and when told to close it, it said it couldn't. (Screenshot: hello-agent tab still open after report_done, showing "Working…" and a Stop button; the parent's close_agent returned "No active agent named hello-agent")
  - Notes: Root causes found:
    1. By design in I-037, `stopAgent` (`apps/server/src/services/app-service.ts`) stops a finished agent's process but **keeps its tab and transcript**, while the agent-teams prompts (and the user) expect the pane to close like in cmux. Afterwards `closeAgent` only looks at non-closed records, so `close_agent` on a finished agent fails with "No active agent".
    2. `closeLive()` unsubscribes and disposes the pi process without emitting `state {isRunning:false}` / `run_end` for that session, so clients keep showing "Working…" and a Stop button (affects any session stopped by the server, not only agents).
    Fix: (a) when an agent is closed (report_done with auto-close, `close_agent`, idle timeout), **remove its tab**: delete the sub-agent session (its result has been delivered to the parent) unless it was kept open or the user typed in it; (b) `close_agent` on an agent whose tab still exists closes it (idempotent: "already closed" is not an error); (c) `closeLive` (or its callers) broadcasts a final `state`/`run_end` so no UI stays stuck; (d) `list_agents` reports closed agents as closed (no tab). Update agent-teams wording ("pane" → neutral) with I-054. Tests: auto-close removes the session; close_agent idempotent; stopped session emits run_end.
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-26): closing = the tab disappears and the sub-agent's conversation is **deleted**, like a cmux pane; its summary stays in the parent chat. No "finished agents" list.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): closing a sub-agent (report_done auto-close, close_agent, idle timeout) deletes its session and tab; the agent record stays as `closed`; close_agent is idempotent ("already closed"); `closeLive` emits `run_end` / `state {isRunning:false}` so nothing stays on "Working…". Verified end to end with real pi.

  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — tabs are too wide (a minimum width stretches them out); they should size to their content, with a max width. Make the active tab more obvious too, e.g. a blue border along its top. (Screenshot: two tabs each ~180px wide with the title left and the × far right)
  - Notes: `apps/web/src/ui/TabStrip.tsx` gives every tab `basis-[180px] min-w-[96px] max-w-[220px]` and the label `flex-1`, so short titles stretch to 180px. Make tabs content-sized: `basis-auto`, no stretching label, padding ~10px, a small min width (~48px, enough for a short title + ×) and `max-w-[220px]` with truncation; the × sits right after the title (keep space reserved for it so hovering doesn't shift the layout). When tabs overflow, keep the strip scrollable (today's behaviour).
    Active tab: a 2px accent (blue) line along its top edge, a slightly lighter background that connects to the content below (no bottom separator under it), and full-strength text; inactive tabs muted. Status indicators and focus rings unchanged. Applies to both the main and sub-agent strips.
  - Acceptance: short titles give short tabs, long titles truncate at the max width; the active tab is obvious at a glance in dark and light (screenshots); TabStrip tests updated.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): tabs content-sized (48–220px, truncating), × right after the title; active tab: 2px accent top line, lighter background joined to the content, full-strength text. New optional `badge` / `tooltip` on `TabStripTab`.

Remove it from the sidebar and keep it only in the chat's usage popover, made more visually appealing so the percentages are easy to read on hover. The popover also needs more padding and sits too close to its icon. (Screenshot: the sidebar usage popover with bars for session / week / Fable)

The ring in the composer could reflect the most constraining of context % and the active limit, or stay context-only (decide during build; keep it simple).

- [x] **I-058** Dogfooding: develop pi-ui from inside pi-ui safely — `infra` / `docs`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the user will soon develop the app from inside the app; it must be able to start sub-agents and support their normal workflow there.
  - Notes: Pieces already in place: sub-agents (I-037, polish in I-054/I-055), the issue-queue skill and AGENTS.md (discovered by pi from `.agents/skills` when a chat runs in this repo), agent sandboxes (I-052). Risks to address before relying on it:
    1. **Server restarts kill in-flight chats.** The installed app connects to a running `pnpm dev` server (I-022); `tsx watch` restarts that server whenever an agent edits `apps/server/**`, stopping every pi process, including the chat orchestrating the work (it comes back as "interrupted" with Continue, I-025). Options: run the installed app with its own server while dogfooding (don't run `pnpm dev`, or add a setting / env to never borrow a dev server), and/or make `pnpm dev` not auto-restart on server edits (manual restart command).
    2. **Updating the app kills running chats.** `pnpm tauri:install` quits pi-ui (the quit confirmation, I-024, protects running chats but blocks the install) — document an update flow: finish or pause the round, then install; maybe `tauri:install --when-idle` that waits until no chat is working.
    3. **Agents must never touch the app they run in**: sandboxes only (AGENTS.md already says so); double-check the skill's worker template and that sub-agents spawned from pi-ui inherit the right cwd/env.
    4. A short "Dogfooding" section in README/AGENTS.md describing the recommended setup.
  - Decision (user, 2026-09-26): the installed app always runs **its own server**, reading the same data folder as `pnpm dev` (no borrowing). Requires I-062.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): README + AGENTS.md "Dogfooding" sections; `pnpm tauri:install --when-idle` waits until no chat on the app's server is working, and install now aborts if the app didn't quit.

Local folder: rename `~/src/pi-ui` → `~/src/glade` as the **very last step** (user, 2026-09-26), after everything is committed and pushed and no agents run, because the lead's own session runs in that folder. Afterwards: reopen terminals/editors in the new path; pi stores new sessions under `~/.pi/agent/sessions/--Users-me-src-glade--` (old sessions stay under the old folder name); update any Glade project that points at `~/src/pi-ui` (e.g. the "pi-ui" project) to the new path — offer a "project folder moved" fix-up (edit a project's path in its menu) so this isn't manual JSON editing.

GitHub repo renamed to `jas7457/glade`; local folder renamed to `~/src/glade` as the last step.

Closing the last main tab therefore means deleting the whole workspace (its sidebar row, sub-agents and pi session files): show the × on every main tab; for the last one (and ⌘W / "Close Tab" in the palette and menu) show a destructive confirm like "Delete “<title>”? This is the last tab, so the whole chat will be deleted." then call the workspace delete and navigate to the project's new-chat screen (or home for standalone). Other tabs keep today's behaviour (confirm only if the tab has history or sub-agents). Code: `features/workspace/WorkspaceView.tsx` / `layout-actions.ts` (`closeTab`), `ui/TabStrip.tsx` (closable flag), `app/commands.tsx` (Close Tab availability).
  - Acceptance: a single-tab chat shows ×; closing it asks, then removes the chat from the sidebar; tests.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): every main tab has ×; closing the last tab (×, ⌘W, palette, menu) confirms "Delete … This is the last tab, so the whole chat will be deleted" → deletes the workspace and replaces the URL with the project's new-chat screen (or home).
- [x] **I-062** Two servers on one data folder: installed app always runs its own server, sharing data safely with `pnpm dev` — `server` / `infra`
  - Reported: 2026-09-26 — the installed app should run its own server, reading the same data as the dev server; asked whether that isn't already the case.
  - Notes: Today (I-022) a data-dir lock allows one server per folder; the desktop app borrows a live dev server. Replace "one server" with "many servers, safe sharing":
    1. **Store:** every read-modify-write of `workspaces.json` / `projects.json` / `settings.json` / `agents.json` / `session-summaries.json` under a short cross-process file lock (e.g. `proper-lockfile`-style lock dir with stale detection) that re-reads the file first; atomic writes as today. Watch the files (`fs.watch` + debounce) and apply external changes, broadcasting `workspace_upsert` / `session_upsert` / `project_upsert` / `settings` to the server's own clients.
    2. **Session leases:** a server claims a session while it runs its pi process (`<dataDir>/leases/<sessionId>.json` with pid, server id, heartbeat); another server shows that session as "active in <other app>" (read-only composer, status from the shared file) and may take over when the lease is idle/stale. Prevents two pi processes on one session file.
    3. **Status across servers:** each server streams only its own runs; the other refreshes status from the shared store (working → unread). Unread/viewing stays per server.
    4. **Desktop:** drop the "borrow a running server" path from `src-tauri/src/server.rs` (always spawn its own, `PI_UI_SERVER_KIND=desktop`); the dev server keeps `kind: dev`. Remove/replace the exit-code-3 lock (`services/data-lock.ts`) with a per-server registry (`<dataDir>/servers/<pid>.json`) used for diagnostics and stale-lease cleanup.
    5. Search index, usage poller, agent tokens and titles are per server (fine); sub-agent records need the lock + lease rules.
  - Acceptance: `pnpm dev` and the installed app run at the same time on the same data: a chat created in one appears in the other within a second; a chat running in one shows as active (not startable) in the other; no lost writes under concurrent edits (test with two AppServices on one temp dir); desktop app never borrows.
  - Depends on / supersedes: the borrowing behaviour of I-022; needed for I-058.
  - Outcome (2026-09-26): op-based `JsonFile` writes under a cross-process lock with re-read; data-folder watcher pushes the other server's changes (~0.1 s); session leases (`<dataDir>/leases/`) with take-over when idle and 409 "Running in pi-ui (dev)…" otherwise; server registry (`<dataDir>/servers/`) replaces the exit-code-3 lock; desktop always spawns its own server. Verified with two real servers and the desktop build on one folder. Known limits: the other server's transcript isn't streamed live; same-record concurrent edits are last-write-wins.
